Maximal parahoric arithmetic transfers, resolutions and modularity
Zhiyu Zhang

Abstract
. For any unramified quadratic extension of p p -adic local fields F / F 0 F/F_{0} ( p p odd), we formulate several arithmetic transfer conjectures at any maximal parahoric level, in the context of Zhang’s relative trace formula approach to the arithmetic Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture. The formulation involves a way to resolve the singularity of relevant moduli spaces via natural stratifications and modify derived fixed points. By a local–global method and double induction, we prove these conjectures when F 0 / ℚ p F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}_{p} is unramified and the arithmetic fundamental lemma for any F 0 F_{0} . We introduce the relative Cayley map and also establish explicit Jacquet–Rallis transfers at maximal parahoric levels. Moreover, we prove new modularity results for arithmetic theta series with levels via a method of modification over fibers. Along the way, we study the complex and mod p p geometry of Shimura varieties and special cycles.



1.
Introduction

The Gross–Zagier formula
[
14
]
relates Néron–Tate heights of Heegner points on modular curves to central derivatives of
L
-functions of modular forms, which has fundamental applications to the Birch and Swinnerton–Dyer (BSD) conjectures for elliptic curves. As a higher dimensional generalization, the
arithmetic Gan–Gross–Prasad (AGGP) conjecture

[
8
,
48
]
relates heights of special diagonal cycles on unitary (resp. orthogonal) Shimura varieties to central derivatives of automorphic
L
-functions for unitary groups (resp. orthogonal groups), which has fundamental applications to the Bloch–Kato conjectures for automorphic motives.

In the
relative trace formula
(RTF) approach proposed by W. Zhang
[
66
]
(inspired by the RTF approach of Jacquet–Rallis
[
22
,
68
,
69
]
to the Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture
[
8
]
), the AGGP conjecture for
unitary groups
is reduced to following local conjectures:

•

The
arithmetic fundamental lemma
(AFL) at hyperspecial places formulated by W. Zhang
[
66
]
.

•

Suitable
arithmetic transfer
(AT) conjectures at places with levels. See the works of Rapoport–Smithling–Zhang
[
49
,
50
]
for some formulations and known low-dimensional cases.

Over a prime
p\geq 2
, these conjectures relate central derivatives of orbital integrals of suitable test functions to intersection numbers of special diagonal cycles on unitary Rapoport–Zink spaces :

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma,f^{\prime})=-\Int(g)\cdot\log q.

See the ICM report of W. Zhang
[
70
]
for details and some applications. Significant progress has been made in recent years: the AFL over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(resp. any
p
-adic field
F_{0}
) for
p\geq n
(resp.
q\geq n
) is proved via a global method in the work of W. Zhang
[
67
]
(resp. Mihatsch–Zhang
[
41
]
).

For arithmetic applications, we could not avoid local ramifications. Therefore, it is important and necessary to establish AT identities at these places. Compared to AFL, it is more difficult to formulate AT conjectures: the relevant local moduli spaces (and global integral models of Shimura varieties) are no longer regular, and there may be no natural test functions on the analytic side. The relevant reductive group may not be quasi-split, and the geometry is also more complicated. New ideas and techniques are needed for both the formulation and proof of AT conjectures.

The first goal of this paper is to formulate and prove arithmetic transfer conjectures for
all maximal parahoric levels
at
unramified places
. Our formulation is coordinate free. On the geometric side, we introduce a general method to resolve the singularity via natural stratifications and blowing up along
the local model diagram
. We do intersections and modify
derived fixed points
on the resolution. On the analytic side, we construct explicit test functions and study their orbital integrals. Moreover, we formulate and prove explicit transfer conjectures at these levels, generalizing the work of Beuzart–Plessis
[
2
]
on Jacquet–Rallis fundamental lemmas.

We also use similar resolutions to construct regular integral models for products of unitary Shimura varieties at these levels appearing in the AGGP conjecture, which is necessary to compute Beilinson–Bloch heights of cycles via arithmetic intersection theory.

The second goal of this paper is to prove new modularity results for arithmetic theta series on unitary Shimura varieties with parahoric levels, in the context of the Kudla program. For hermitian (resp. quadratic) lattices
L
with suitable signatures over a totally real number field
F_{0}
,
Kudla’s generating series of special cycles
on unitary (resp. orthogonal) Shimura varieties
[
27
]
for
L
could be thought as arithmetic and geometric analogs of classical theta series, which are conjectured to be modular. The conjectural modularity on unitary Shimura varieties (in different set ups) has several arithmetic applications.

•

In the work of Liu and Li–Liu
[
34
,
36
]
, the conjectural modularity over the generic fiber is used to construct arithmetic theta liftings;

•

In the work of Bruinier–Howard–Kudla–Rapoport–Yang
[
4
]
, the modularity in the arithmetic divisor case is proved when
F_{0}=\mathbb{Q}
and
L
is self-dual, which applies to the
averaged Colmez’s conjecture
on the Faltings heights of abelian varieties with complex multiplication;

•

In the work of Mihatsch–Zhang
[
41
]
, an almost modularity result is obtained in the arithmetic divisor case when
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
and
L
is self-dual (away from
\Delta
), which is used in their proof of AFL when
q\geq n
.

•

In the work of Howard–Madapusi
[
18
, Section 8-9]
, the modularity on orthogonal Shimura varieties in the arithmetic divisor case is proved for maximal quadratic lattices, which has applications to exceptional jumps of Picard ranks of reductions of K3 surfaces over number fields
[
55
]
.

Now we go to more details on our main results.

1.1.
Arithmetic transfers at maximal parahoric levels

Let
p
be an odd prime. Let
F/F_{0}
be an unramified quadratic extension of
p
-adic fields with residue fields
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}/\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Denote by
\breve{F}
the completion of the maximal unramified extension of
F_{0}
.

1.1.1.
Geometric side

Let
V
be a
F/F_{0}
hermitian space of dimension
n\geq 1
. Let
L\subseteq V
be a vertex lattice of type
t
, i.e.
L\subseteq L^{\vee}
and
L^{\vee}/L
is a
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}
-vector space of dimension
t
. Consider the unitary group
G:=\mathrm{U}(V)
over
F_{0}
, which may be non-quasi-split. Stabilizers of vertex lattices give all maximal parahoric subgroups of
G(F_{0})

[
61
, Section 4]
. Associated to
L
is
the relative Rapoport–Zink space

[
52
]
(a formal scheme) parameterizing certain deformations of
p
-divisible groups with
O_{F}
-action:

\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}.

It can be regarded as an integral model of local unitary Shimura varieties
[
60
]
with maximal parahoric levels.

The morphism
\mathcal{N}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
is formally smooth if
t=0,n
, and is of
strictly semi-stable reduction
if
0<t<n
by
[
6
,
10
]
. For example,
\mathcal{N}
is isomorphic to
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
if
n=1
, to a height
2
Lubin–Tate space if
n=2,\,t=0,2
, and to the Drinfeld half plane
\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{F_{0}}

[
29
]
if
n=2,t=1
.

Let
\mathbb{V}
be
the hermitian space of special quasi–homomorphisms
(
5.2
) which is of dimension
n
and
not isomorphic
to
V
. The group
J_{b}(F_{0}):=\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
acts on
\mathcal{N}
naturally by changing the framing.

The space
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
(product over
O_{\breve{F}}
) is singular if
0<t<n
. A question is how to resolve the singularity in a natural way. Inspired by recent work on Beilinson–Bloch–Kato conjectures
[
35
, Section 5]
, we introduce the
J_{b}(F_{0})
-stable
formal Balloon–Ground stratification
on the formal special fiber
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{F}}
:

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{F}}=\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}.

Our definition (
5.3
) is purely Lie algebra theoretic and indicates these strata are examples of (closed) formal Kottwitz–Rapoport strata. We blow up along the product
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
(a Weil divisor of
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
) and resolve the singularity:

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}

which may be thought as an arithmetic analog of the Atiyah flop
[
1
]
.

Using the action of
J_{b}(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{N}
, now we formulate an arithmetic transfer conjecture for any orthogonal decomposition

L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e

where
(e,e)
has valuation
i\in\{0,1\}
. Firstly, the decomposition gives a natural embedding

\mathcal{N}^{\flat}:=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L^{\flat})}\mathcal{N}:=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}

by adding a universal factor of relative height
2
and dimension
1
. The induced embedding
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}
lifts to an embedding of regular formal schemes via strict transforms inside
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
:

\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}.

Consider the conjugacy action of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})
on
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})
. For a regular semi-simple element
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we consider its derived intersection number as the Euler characteristic

\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})=\chi(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}},\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}_{(1\times g^{\prime})\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\in\mathbb{Q}.

1.1.2.
Analytic side

Fix an orthogonal basis of
L^{\flat}
and let
L_{0}
be the
O_{F_{0}}
-span of this basis. Set

L_{0}:=L_{0}^{\flat}\oplus O_{F_{0}}e,\,V_{0}:=L_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q},\,V_{0}^{\flat}:=L_{0}^{\flat}\otimes\mathbb{Q}.

We have an induced Galois involution
\overline{(-)}
on
V
with fixed subspace
V_{0}
. Consider the symmetric space over
F_{0}
:

S(V_{0})=\{\,\gamma\in\mathrm{GL}(V)\,|\,\gamma\overline{\gamma}=id\,\}.

Consider the conjugacy action of
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})
on
S(V_{0})
. For a regular semi-simple element
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
and
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})(F_{0}))
, we consider the twisted orbital integral (
s\in\mathbb{C}
)

\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},f^{\prime},s):=\omega(\gamma)\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})}f^{\prime}(h^{-1}\gamma h)\eta(h)|h|^{s}dh,

\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},f^{\prime})=\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},f^{\prime},0),\,\,\,\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma^{\prime},f^{\prime})=\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},f^{\prime},s).

Here we insert the transfer factor
\omega(\gamma^{\prime})\in\{\pm 1\}
(
2.8
). There is a matching relation (
2.3
) between regular semi-simple orbits

[S(V_{0})/\!/\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})]_{\mathrm{rs}}\cong[\mathrm{U}(V)/\!/\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})]_{\mathrm{rs}}\coprod[\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})/\!/\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})]_{\mathrm{rs}}.

1.1.3.
Explicit transfers and arithmetic transfer conjectures

Write
S(L,L^{\vee})
as the stabilizer of the lattice chain
L\subseteq L^{\vee}
inside
S(V_{0})(F_{0})
. Firstly, we prove the following explicit Jacuqet–Rallis transfer result.

Theorem 1.1

(Theorem
2.7
)
.

For any
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have

\Orb(\gamma,1_{S(L,L^{\vee})})=\begin{cases}\Orb(g,\,1_{\mathrm{U}(L)})&\text{if $\gamma$ matches some $g\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}$,}\\
0&\text{if $\gamma$ matches some $g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}$.}\end{cases}

In fact, we also establish the theorem when
p=2
. The proof is via a pure local method and double induction, generalizing the work of Beuzart-Plessis
[
2
]
. Our coordinate-free formulation allows us to establish the general reduction and induction of orbital integrals.

Now it is natural to study the derived orbital integrals. We formulate the following arithmetic transfer conjecture for
(L,e)
.

Conjecture 1.2

(Group arithmetic transfer for
(L,e)
, see also Conjecture
6.9
)
.

For any
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matching
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have an equality in
\mathbb{Q}\log q

(1.1)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma^{\prime},1_{S(L,L^{\vee})})=-\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})\log q.

It recovers the AFL conjecture
[
66
]
when
t=0,\,i=0
and a version of AT conjecture in the work of Rapoport–Smithling–Zhang
[
50
, Section 10]
when
t=0,\,i=1
.

1.1.4.
Semi-Lie version arithmetic transfers

We also formulate two semi-Lie version arithmetic transfer conjectures for any vertex lattice
L
, in the context of the Fourier–Jacobi AGGP conjecture studied by Yifeng Liu
[
33
]
.

For
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
, we introduce the
modified derived fixed point locus

\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)
as the
derived fiber product
of strict transforms of the graph of
g
and the diagonal inside
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
, which is a derived
1
-cycle. This modification procedure is general and explains the embedding points phenomenon in the work of Kudla–Rapoport on orthogonal Shimura curves
[
28
]
.

On the geometric side, for any
u\in\mathbb{V}-\{0\}
we have
Kudla–Rapoport divisors

\mathcal{Z}(u)
and
\mathcal{Y}(u)
on
\mathcal{N}
studied by Kudla–Rapooprt and Cho
[
6
,
30
]
. For
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we define
\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u)
(resp.
\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,u)
) as the derived intersection number between
\mathcal{Z}(u)
(resp.
\mathcal{Y}(u)
) and
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)
on
\mathcal{N}
.

On the analytic side, similarly we consider the derived orbital integral
\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(-,-)
on
(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})
with respect to the natural action of
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(F_{0})
.
There is also a matching relation (
2.1
) between regular–semisimple orbits

[S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}/\!/\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}\cong[\mathrm{U}(V)\times V/\!/\mathrm{U}(V)]_{\mathrm{rs}}\coprod[\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V}/\!/\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})]_{\mathrm{rs}}.

Define two standard functions on
(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})
:

(1.2)

f_{\text{std}}:=1_{S(L,L^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{(L^{\vee}_{0})^{*}},\quad f_{\text{std}}^{\prime}=1_{S(L,L^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee}}\times 1_{(L_{0})^{*}}.

Conjecture 1.3

(Semi-Lie arithmetic transfers for
L
, also see Conjecture
6.4
)
.

For any
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matching
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have equalities in
\mathbb{Q}\log q
:

(1)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f_{\text{std}})=-\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u)\log q
.

(2)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f_{\text{std}}^{\prime})=-(-1)^{t}\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,u)\log q
.

Our first main theorem in this paper is a proof of these group and semi-Lie arithmetic transfer conjectures (under a mild assumption on
F_{0}
) in arbitrary higher dimension.

Theorem 1.4
.

Assume
F_{0}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(e.g.
F_{0}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
) if
0<t<n
. Then the above semi-Lie (resp. group) version arithmetic transfer conjecture (
1.3
) (resp.
1.2
)hold for any vertex lattice
L
(resp. any orthogonal decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
).

In particular, we prove the AFL over any
p
-adic field
F_{0}
for
p>2
, including small primes which is not covered by the previous works of Wei Zhang and Mihatsch–Zhang
[
41
,
67
]
. The proof is based on a local-global method and double induction, using new modularity results of
arithmetic theta series
at maximal parahoric levels that we now discuss.

1.2.
Modularity of arithmetic theta series

Let
F/F_{0}
be a CM quadratic extension of a totally real number field. Fix a CM type
\Phi\subseteq\Hom(F,\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and a distinguished element
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi
.

Let
V
be a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n\geq 1
and signature
\{(n-1,1)_{\varphi_{0}},(n,0)_{\varphi\in\Phi-\{\varphi_{0}\}}\}
. Choose a hermitian lattice
L
in
V
and a finite collection
\Delta
of finite places for
F_{0}
. Assume that

•

F/F_{0}
is unramified outside
\Delta
, and all
2
-adic places are in
\Delta
.

•

If
v\not\in\Delta
is a place of
F_{0}
such that
L_{v}
is not self-dual, then
v
is inert in
F
,
L_{v}
is a vertex lattice, the prime underlying
v
is unramifed in
F_{0}
.

Consider
G=\mathrm{U}(V)
over
F_{0}
. Choose a level
K\subseteq G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
associated to
L
and
\Delta
, which agrees with the stabilizer
\mathrm{U}(\widehat{L})
away from
\Delta
, and could be chosen to be sufficiently small at
\Delta
. We consider unitary Shimura variety
M_{G,K}
over
F
. By the work of Rapoport–Smithling–Zhang
[
48
,
51
]
, we have a natural
regular
integral model

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]

of (the RSZ-variant of)
M_{G,K}
. Here the number field
E:=\varphi_{0}(F)F^{\Phi}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. If either
F
is Galois over
\mathbb{Q}
or
F
contains an imaginary quadratic field, then
E=F

[
48
, Remark 3.1]
. An important advantage is that the RSZ-variant
\mathcal{M}
admits a PEL type moduli interpretation (
10.3
).

As in the Kudla program, for any function
\phi=\phi_{L}\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K}
that agrees with the function
1_{\widehat{L}}
away from
\Delta
, we can consider the
arithmetic theta series
for
L
as Kudla’s generating series

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi)

of
arithmetic Kudla–Rapoport divisors

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi)
(
13.7
)
[
30
,
31
]
equipped with
admissible automorphic Green functions
. It has coefficients in the
arithmetic Chow group

\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})
and lifts the generating series
Z(\phi)
(
11.3
)
[
34
]
of Kudla–Rapoport cycles on the generic fiber
M_{G,K}
.

Assume that
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
so that
\mathcal{M}
is projective. Consider the arithmetic intersection pairing (
13.2
) between arithmetic divisors and proper
1
-cycles on
\mathcal{M}
:

(-,-):\quad\widehat{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}

As
O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
is not “compact” in the sense of Arakelov geometry, the intersection pairing takes values in the quotient
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space

\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}:=\mathbb{R}\,/\sum_{v|\ell,v\in\Delta}{\mathbb{Q}\log\ell}.

If
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})
is a vertical
1
-cycle at
v\not\in\Delta
, we may take the value
(-,\mathcal{C})
in
\mathbb{R}
. Our second main theorem in this paper is the modularity of arithmetic theta series when intersecting with
1
-cycles.

Theorem 1.5

(Global modularity, also see Theorem
13.5
)
.

Assume that
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
and
E=F
.

(1)

Assume that
\Delta
is large enough (see Theorem
13.5
for precise conditions). For any proper
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{M}
and
\phi=\phi_{L}
, the generating series
(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi),\mathcal{C})
is modular of weight
n
, up to adding a constant in
\mathbb{R}
.

(2)

For any linear functional
\ell:\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}
that has degree
0
over
\mathbb{C}
and trivial on irreducible components of special fibers of
\mathcal{M}
over
v\in\Delta
,
\ell(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi))
is modular of weight
n
.

The modularity in
(1)
shows that there exists a Hilbert modular form on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
whose Fourier expansion agrees with
(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi),\mathcal{C})
under the projection
\mathbb{R}\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}
(up to the constant terms). We avoid the use of elementary CM cycles in Mihatsch–Zhang
[
41
]
hence give a different proof of the almost modularity result (in which case
\mathcal{M}
has good reduction away from
\Delta
) in
loc. cit.
. The related results on mod
p
geometry of Shimura varieties may be of independent interest. We also note the recent result of Qiu
[
47
]
prove some general arithmetic modularity results on admissible extensions (rather than moduli extensions here) of arithmetic special divisors on unitary Shimura varieties with no levels at inert places (rather than maximal parahoric levels here) and Drinfeld levels at split paces.

Now we briefly describe the proofs of our main theorems.

1.3.
Modularity by modification and geometry of Shimura varieties

We introduce a modification method to reduce the conjectured modularity of arithmetic theta series on integral models to the modularity over the generic fiber (known by the work of Liu
[
34
]
) and over the basic locus (proved in §
13.4
). The method relies on the complex and mod
p
geometry of Shimura varieties, and may apply to more general levels.

Our arithmetic theta series
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi)
lies in the admissible arithmetic Chow group
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{M})
(
13.1
). We have a short exact sequence (
13.1
)

0\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{M})\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{M}){\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{E})0.

Here the subgroup
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{M})
is generated by

•

(0,c_{\nu})
, where
c_{\nu}
is a locally constant function on
\mathcal{M}_{E,\nu}(\mathbb{C})
for an embedding
\nu:E\mathbb{C}
;

•

(X_{s},0)
, where
X_{s}
is an irreducible component of the special fiber
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
at a finite place
\nu
of
E
(with residue field
k_{\nu}
) such that
\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}
is non-smooth over
O_{E_{\nu}}
. In our cases, when
v\not\in\Delta
the non–smoothness means that
v
is inert and
L_{v}
is a vertex lattice of type
0<t_{v}<n
.

Consider any linear functional
\ell:\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{C}
, e.g.
\ell=(-,\mathcal{C})
for a proper
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
on
\mathcal{M}
. We make three observations of the arithmetic modularity for
\ell
:

(1)

If
\ell
is trivial on
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{M})
, then Theorem
1.5
holds for
\ell
, i.e.
\ell(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi))
is modular by the known modularity on the generic fiber
\mathcal{M}_{E}
. If
\ell=(-,\mathcal{C})
, this means
\mathcal{C}
has degree
0
over
\mathbb{C}
and the intersection
(X_{s},\mathcal{C})=0
for any irreducible component
X_{s}
of a non-smooth special fiber of
\mathcal{M}
.

(2)

(Comparison to automorphic generating functions in specific cases) For specific
\ell^{\prime}:\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{C}
, we may prove Theorem
1.5
for
\ell^{\prime}
directly.

Over
\mathbb{C}
, we consider
\ell^{\prime}=(-,\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
for specific Hecke CM cycle
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\mathcal{CM}(\alpha_{0},\mu_{\Delta})
(§
11
) where
\alpha_{0}
has maximal order. Using simple cases of AFLs and ATs (proved in §
9
), we show that
\ell^{\prime}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi))
corresponds to
\alpha_{0}
-part of the analytic generating function
\partial\mathbb{J}(h,\Phi^{\prime})
in §
14
, which is known to be modular by Poisson summation formula.

Over a non-smooth special fiber
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
(over a place
v\not\in\Delta
with residue field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
), we consider
\ell^{\prime}=(-,\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
for any
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\nu}
in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. Assuming the local modularity Conjecture
8.2
on relevant Rapoport–Zink spaces, we show that under the basic uniformization (§
11.6
),
\ell^{\prime}(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}(\phi))
corresponds to a classical theta series on the nearby totally definite hermitian space
V^{(v)}
of
V
at
v
, which is known to be modular by Poisson summation formula. We prove the local modularity conjecture for
very special
(
12.14
)
1
-cycles (Theorem
8.1
) and use them as specific functionals
\ell^{\prime}
.

The local modularity Conjecture
8.2
is quite interesting as these Deligne–Lusztig varieties appearing in the reduced locus are not of Coxeter type in general
[
11
, Section 6]
and mysterious.

(3)

(Modification via complex and mod
p
geometry) To prove Theorem
1.5
in general, under our assumptions we show that for any given
\ell
, we may find specific
\ell^{\prime}
such that
\ell-\ell^{\prime}
is trivial on
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{M})
. Hence the modularity for
\ell
is proved by the known modularity of
\ell-\ell^{\prime}
and
\ell^{\prime}
.

To do the modification i.e. show the existence of such specific
\ell^{\prime}
for given
\ell
, we use
global geometry
of Shimura varieties. Over
\mathbb{C}
, we show the unitary Shimura variety
M_{G,K}
is connected over
E
(assuming
E=F
) by studying the Galois and Hecke action on connected components of complex Shimura varieties.

Over a non-smooth special fiber
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, we introduce the
Balloon–Ground stratification
(§
10.4
)

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}=\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}\cup\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}.

Our construction may be thought as a moduli definition of top dimensional Kottwitz–Rapoport strata, which is not considered before in the unitary case. Irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
are given by irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
. If
t_{v}=1
, then irreducible components of the Balloon stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
are isomorphic to projective spaces
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{k_{\nu}}
and contained in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
(
[
35
, Section 5]
). And the Ground stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
is irreducible in any given connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, see the Figure below. Therefore, we can choose
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
to be a finite linear combination of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
in different irreducible components
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
of the Balloon stratum.

Figure 1.
“Balloons” (with grey colors) on the “ground” (the bottom line) for almost self dual
L_{v}

The case
t_{v}=n-1
is similar to the case
t_{v}=1
by duality. If
1<t_{v}<n-1
, the level
K_{v}
is a non-special parahoric. For dimensional reasons, the Balloon and Ground strata are not completely in the basic locus. Nevertheless, we show
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
are both irreducible in any given connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, which is related to the irreducibility of non-basic Kottwitz–Rapoport strata at parahoric levels (Conjecture
12.24
(2)) studied in the work of van Hoften
[
16
]
. Then we choose a very special
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
lying in the basic locus but not in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}\cap\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
to do the job.

In fact we obtain a double modularity result (i.e., for both special divisors
\mathcal{Z}(\phi_{L})
and
\mathcal{Z}(\phi_{L}^{\vee})
) as “vector-valued” modular forms. Their generating series may be related via the action of local Weil representation on hermitian spaces
\{V_{v}\}_{v}
, see
\lx@sectionsign

13
. As in the work on AFL
[
67
]
, the modularity of arithmetic theta series plays a similar role in the proof of arithmetic transfer identities as the “perverse continuation principle” in the geometric approach (see
[
45
]
,
[
64
]
) to fundamental lemmas over function fields.

1.4.
Arithmetic transfer identities via modularity and double induction

Our approach to arithmetic transfers is local-global.

•

(Local aspects)
We introduce the
relative Cayley map
as a more natural reduction tool (
3.1
) for a decomposition of
F
-vector spaces
V=V^{\flat}\oplus Fe
. The unitary (
3.2
) and symmetric (
3.4
) variants behave well for all primes
p\geq 2
. They give reductions of orbits and (derived) orbital integrals §
3
.

We establish the reduction of intersection numbers in §
6.3
. A key point is the smallness of the resolution
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
similar to the smallness of the Atiyah flop. Given an orthogonal decomposition of vertex lattices
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
, consider
the unitary relative Cayley map
for the decomposition
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}^{\flat}\oplus Fe^{[i]}

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 21.20143pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.20143pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}:\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.8596pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 76.8596pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})\times\mathbb{V}^{\flat}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-17.43233pt\raise-9.46326pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g^{\prime}={\begin{pmatrix}t&u\\
w&d\end{pmatrix}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.43233pt\raise-9.46326pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.20143pt\raise-9.46326pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.20143pt\raise-9.46326pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g=t+\frac{uw}{1-d},\,u_{1}=\frac{u}{1-d}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

For example when
i=0
, for
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
with
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
and
u_{1}\not=0
we have
the reduction equality
:

\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})=\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u_{1}).

In conclusion, we establish a reduction from group version AT conjectures (
6.9
) of rank
n
to semi-Lie version AT conjectures (
6.4
) of rank
n-1
. Moreover, we show semi-Lie AT conjectures for
\mathcal{Z}
-cycles on
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}
and
\mathcal{Y}
-cycles on
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n-t]}
are equivalent using the dual isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n-t]}
(
5.1
). Hence we may focus on semi-Lie version AT conjectures for
\mathcal{Z}
-cycles.

•

(Global aspects) We globalize the local data to cycles on suitable global RSZ unitary Shimura varieties as above. We follow the strategy of
[
67
]
, but the original induction in
[
67
]
for the proof of the AFL doesn’t apply directly at parahoric levels. We introduce a method of double induction at parahoric levels in §
15.1
to prove one AT conjecture of rank
n
using two AT conjectures of rank
n-1
(hence the name of “double induction”). Using the new (double) modularity result above as an input (we may enlarge
\Delta
to be sufficiently large), and local reductions and equivalences above as base cases, we finish the proof of AT conjectures.

We also study the geometry of relevant Rapoport–Zink spaces and reconstruct the Bruhat–Tits stratification (
[
6
,
63
]
) on the reduced locus via special cycles. The phenomenon that there are two kinds of Bruhat–Tits strata at our parahoric levels is related to the formal Balloon–Ground stratification, see Remark
7.12
. We also use Bruhat–Tits strata to study fixed points of
g
on
\mathcal{N}
in simple cases, see Example
9.5
. A new pheonomenon in parahoric levels is that the behavior of
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
in general depends on the embedding
O_{F}[g]\End(\mathbb{V})
rather than just
O_{F}[g]
.

1.5.
Further directions

Firstly we comment on the assumptions. The assumption that
F_{0}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is used to show the relevant Rapoport–Zink spaces appear in the basic uniformization of relevant unitary Shimura varieties. We hope to establish a comparison theorem and basic uniformization at general parahoric levels as in
[
40
]
. Applications include the proof of AT conjectures (including
p=2
) and understanding the cohomology of local Shimura varieties (e.g. the work of Hansen
[
15
]
) for any
p
-adic field
F_{0}
via globalization. Moreover, the modification method for arithmetic modularity could be applied to general levels.

Our method may be further developed to formulate and prove arithmetic transfer identities for more general levels (e.g. the Iwahori level) and other set ups (e.g.
[
19
,
38
,
53
,
71
]
). In general, the singularity of suitable integral model is controlled by certain sections of universal vector bundles (or “Shtukas” as in
[
46
]
), and we may blow up along the local model diagram (using Kottwitz–Rapoport strata) to resolve the singularity. They will be used in the proof of AGGP conjectures in higher dimensions over number fields with ramifications.

Finally, we give an overview of the paper, which is divided into
4
parts. We formulate the set up of Jacquet–Rallis transfers in §
2
. In §
3
, we introduce the relative Cayley map and do reductions for orbits and (derived) orbital integrals. In §
4
, we establish the Jacuqet–Rallis transfer for vertex lattices using a double induction and local uncertainty principles.

In Part
2
, we go into the local geometric story. In §
5
, we introduce relevant moduli spaces and cycles, and use the formal Balloon–Ground stratification to resolve the singularity hence modify derived fixed points . In §
6
, we formulate semi-Lie and group version arithmetic transfer conjectures, and use relative Cayley map and derived intersection theory to show the reduction of intersection numbers. In §
7
, we introduce the Bruhat–Tits stratification. In §
8
, we formulate the local modularity conjecture and prove the local modularity for very special
1
-cycles. In §
9
, using moduli interpretations and Bruhat–Tits strata we prove our ATCs in the unramified maximal order case.

In Part
3
and
4
, we go into the global story. In §
10
, we introduce RSZ Shimura varieties for parahoric hermitian lattices and study the singularity using Balloon–Ground stratification and local models. In §
11
, we introduce relevant special cycles on Shimura varieties. In §
12
and
13
, we formulate prove global modularity by modification over
\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. In §
14
, we introduce the global analytic side and geometric side. In §
15
, we do a double induction and prove our arithmetic transfer conjectures by globalization.

Notations and Conventions

•

Unless otherwise stated, any hermitian space
V
over a quadratic extension
F/F_{0}
in this article is assumed to be non-degenerate. We always denote by
(\cdot,\cdot)_{V}
the hermitian form on
V
, which is by definition
F
-linear on the first factor and conjugate symmetric. The
F_{0}
-valued bilinear form
(x,y)\tr_{F/F_{0}}(x,y)_{V}
makes
V
a quadratic space over
F_{0}
.

•

Let
X
be an affine variety with an action of a reductive group
G
over a field
F_{0}
. Then an element
x\in X(F_{0})
is called regular semi-simple if and only if the stabilizer of
x
inside
G
is trivial, and the orbit of
x
is closed in
X
under the Zariski topology. If
F_{0}
is a
p
-adic field, this is equivalent to that
G(F_{0})x
is closed in
X(F_{0})
under the analytic topology.

Local notations

•

In local set up, we denote by
F/F_{0}
an unramified quadratic extension of
p
-adic fields with residue fields
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}/\mathbb{F}_{q}
, where
p\geq 2
. Denote by
\varpi=\varpi_{F_{0}}
a uniformizer of
F_{0}
, and write the non-trivial Galois involution on
F
by
x\overline{x}
. The standard valuation map is denoted by
v=v_{F}:F^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}
, where
v_{F}(\varpi)=1
. Choose a unit
\delta\in O_{F_{0}}
such that
O_{F}=O_{F_{0}}[\sqrt{\delta}]
and
\bar{\delta}=-\delta
.

•

We denote by
\eta:F_{0}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow F_{0}^{\times}/NF^{\times}\cong\{\pm 1\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\times}
the quadratic character associated to
F/F_{0}
by local class field theory. We extend it to
\eta:F^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
by
\eta(x)=(-1)^{v_{F}(x)}
.

•

For an
O_{F}
-lattice
\Lambda\subseteq V
(of full rank), denote by
\Lambda^{\vee}=\{x\in V|(x,\Lambda)_{V}\subseteq O_{F}\}
its dual lattice. A lattice
\Lambda
is called a
vertex lattice
if
\Lambda\subset\Lambda^{\vee}\subset\varpi^{-1}\Lambda
. The type of a vertex lattice
\Lambda
is
t(\Lambda):=\dim_{k_{F}}\Lambda^{\vee}/\Lambda\in[0,\dim_{F}V]
.
A
self-dual
lattice is a vertex lattice of type
0
. An
almost self-dual
lattice is a vertex lattice of type
1
.

•

For an
O_{F_{0}}
-lattice
\Lambda\subseteq V_{0}
(of full rank) in a
F_{0}
-vector space
V_{0}
, denote by
\Lambda^{*}=\{y\in(V_{0})^{*}|y(\Lambda)\subseteq O_{F_{0}}\}
its linear dual lattice in
(V_{0})^{*}
.
Let
\breve{F}
be the
p
-adic completion of the maximal unramified extension of
F_{0}
,
W=O_{\breve{F}}
be its ring of integers, and
\sigma\in\Gal(\breve{F}/F_{0})
be the Frobenius element
x\rightarrow x^{q}
.

•

We fix a non-trivial unramified additive character
\psi:F_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, which induces a character
\psi_{F}:F\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
given by
\psi_{F}:=\psi\circ\tr_{F/F_{0}}
.

•

For a smooth affine algebraic variety
X
over
F_{0}
, let
\mathcal{S}(X(F_{0}))
be the space of Schwartz functions on
X(F_{0})
. Here all Schwartz functions on totally disconnected topological spaces are
\mathbb{Q}
-valued locally constant functions.

•

For a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space
V
, denote by
\omega
the Weil representation of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{S}(V(F_{0}))
, which commutes with the natural right translation action of
\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})
.

•

We use covariant version of the relative Dieudonne theory.

•

For any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
W_{\xi}^{(n)}(h)
be the standard weight
n
Whittaker function on
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. More concretely, for
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
under the Iwasawa decomposition

h=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a^{1/2}&0\\
0&a^{-1/2}\end{pmatrix}\kappa_{\theta},\quad a\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\quad b\in\mathbb{R},

and

\kappa_{\theta}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\\
-\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),

we have

(1.3)

W_{\xi}^{(n)}(h):=|a|^{n/2}e^{2\pi\xi i(b+ai)}e^{in\theta}.

Global notations

•

In global set up, we always denote by
F/F_{0}
a CM quadratic extension over a totally real number field. Let
F_{0,+}
be the subset of totally positive elements in
F_{0}
.

•

We denote by
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{A}_{0}
, and
\mathbb{A}_{F}
for the adele rings of
\mathbb{Q}
,
F_{0}
, and
F
, respectively. We use the subscript
(-)_{f}
to denote the terms of finite adeles. We use the subscript
(-)_{p}
,
(-)_{v}
, and
(-)_{w}
to denote the local term at a place
p
of
\mathbb{Q}
, a place
v
of
F_{0}
, and a place
w
of
F
, respectively.

•

Let
\Delta
be a finite collection of places of a number field
F_{0}
. We use the lower index
(-)_{\Delta}
to denote the product of
(-)_{v}
over all places
v\in\Delta
.

•

We denote by
\Phi
for a chosen CM type of
F
. A CM type
\Phi
is called unramified at
p
, if
\Phi\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}:F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is induced from a CM type of the maximal subalgebra
(F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{u}
of
F\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
that is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

•

For a smooth affine algebraic variety
X
over
F_{0}
, let
\mathcal{S}(X(\mathbb{A}_{0}))
be the space of Schwartz functions on
X(\mathbb{A}_{0})
.

•

We use
\Hom^{\circ}(-,-)
to mean
\Hom(-,-)\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. All
K
-groups and Chow groups have
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients.

•

We use the standard additive character
\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}:\mathbb{Q}\backslash\mathbb{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, which induces an additive character
\psi_{F_{0}}:F_{0}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
given by
\psi_{F_{0}}:=\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}\circ\tr_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}
.

•

Denote by
N^{+}\leq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}
the subgroup of upper triangular unipotents. For any continuous function
f:{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})\mathbb{C}
that is left
N^{+}(F_{0})
-invariant and
\xi\in F_{0}
, the
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient of
f
is the following function on
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
:

(1.4)

W_{\xi,f}(h)=\int_{F_{0}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{0}}f\left[\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}h\right]\psi_{F_{0}}(-\xi b)db.

•

For a quadratic space
V
over
F_{0}
, and
\xi\in F_{0}
, we denote by
V_{\xi}
the
F_{0}
-subscheme of
V
defined by
(x,x)_{V}=\xi
.

•

For a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space
V
, denote by
\omega
the Weil representation of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
on
\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))
, which commutes with the natural right translation action of
\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
.

•

We denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)
the space of automorphic forms on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
(left invariant under
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
) that is of parallel weight
n
, holomorphic and right invariant under a chosen compact open subgroup
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
.

Part I
Jacquet–Rallis transfers

2.
Explicit transfer conjectures

Let
p
be a prime. Let
F/F_{0}
be an unramified quadratic extension of
p
-adic local fields, and
\eta
be the associated quadratic character
\eta(x):=(-1)^{v_{F}(x)}:F^{\times}\{\pm 1\}
. In this section, we recall the set up of Jacquet–Rallis transfers and formulate explicit transfer conjectures for suitable hermitian lattices.

2.1.
Semi-Lie version transfers

Let
L
be a hermitian lattice in a
n
-dimensional
F/F_{0}
hermitian space
V
. We consider the natural right action of the unitary group
\mathrm{U}(V)
on
\mathrm{U}(V)\times V

h.(g,u)=(h^{-1}gh,\,h^{-1}u).

For
f\in\mathcal{S}((\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0}))
, define its orbital integral at
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
:

\Orb((g,u),f):=\int_{h\in\mathrm{U}(V)}f(h.(g,u))dh.

Choose an orthogonal basis
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
of
L
, which exists for
p\geq 2
by
[
23
, Section 7]
. Let
\overline{(-)}
be the induced Galois involution on
V
with fixed subspace
V_{0}:=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}F_{0}e_{i}
. Let
L_{0}=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}O_{F_{0}}e_{i}
. Consider the symmetric space over
F_{0}
:

(2.1)

S(V_{0})=\{\gamma\in\mathrm{GL}(V)|\gamma\overline{\gamma}=\mathrm{id}\}.

The general linear group
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
acts on the space
S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
by

h.(\gamma,\,u_{1},\,u_{2})=(h^{-1}\gamma h,\,h^{-1}u_{1},\,u_{2}h).

Define the
transfer factor
on
(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
with respect to
L
by

(2.2)

\omega(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}):=\eta(\det(\gamma^{i}u_{1})_{i=0}^{n-1})\in\{\pm 1\}.

For
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0}))
and
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, define twisted orbital integrals:

(2.3)

\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime},s)=\omega(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})}f^{\prime}(h.(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}))\eta(h)|h|^{s}dh,\quad s\in\mathbb{C}.

(2.4)

\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime}):=\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime},0).

We introduce the derived orbital integral:

(2.5)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime}\bigr):=\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime},s).

Here we write
\eta(h):=\eta(\det h)
and
|h|^{s}:=|\det h|^{s}
for short. These integrals converge absolutely for regular semi-simple elements. By
[
22
]
, an orbit
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0})
is regular semi-simple if and only if
\{g^{i}u\}_{i=0}^{n-1}
spans the space
V
.

Definition 2.1
.

We say
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
and
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
match if they are conjugated by
\mathrm{GL}(V)
in
\End(V)\times V\times V^{*}
, where we use the embedding
\mathrm{U}(V)\times V\End(V)\times V\times V^{*}
given by
(g,u)(g,\,u,\,u^{*}:=(x(x,u)_{V})).

The matching relation is equivalent to matching of the following invariants
[
22
]
:

\det(T\mathrm{id}_{V}+g)=\det(T\mathrm{id}_{V}+\gamma)\in F[T],\quad(g^{i}u,u)=u_{2}(\gamma^{i}u_{1}),\quad 0\leq i\leq n-1.

The matching relation gives a natural bijection of orbit spaces of regular semisimple elements:

\textstyle{\bigl[(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0})\bigr]_{\mathrm{rs}}\coprod\bigl[(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})\bigr]_{\mathrm{rs}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\bigr[(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})\bigr]_{\mathrm{rs}}}

where
\mathbb{V}
is the nearby hermitian space of
V
and the action of
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})
on
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V}
is defined similarly.

Define
L_{0}^{\vee}:=L^{\vee}\cap V_{0}
. From now on, we normalize the Haar measure on
\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(F_{0})
) such that the stabilizer
\mathrm{U}(L)
(resp. the stabilizer
\mathrm{GL}(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})
of
L_{0}\subseteq L_{0}^{\vee}
) is of volume
1
.

Definition 2.2

(Semi-Lie Transfers)
.

We say
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0}))
and
f\in\mathcal{S}((\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0}))
are
transfers
to each other, if for any regular semisimple
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\Orb((g,u),\,f)&\text{if $(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})$ matches some $(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}$,}\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

2.2.
Group version transfers

Consider an orthogonal decomposition

L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
of hermitian lattices. Choose an orthogonal basis of
L^{\flat}
which gives a basis of
L
by adding
e
. Similarly, we consider the conjugacy action of
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})
) on
\mathrm{U}(V)
(resp.
S(V_{0})
).

Definition 2.3
.

We say
g\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
and
\gamma\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
match if they are conjugated by
\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})
in
\End(V)
.

The matching relation is equivalent to matching of following invariants
[
22
]
:

\det(T\mathrm{id}_{V}+g)=\det(T\mathrm{id}_{V}+\gamma)\in F[T],\quad(g^{i}e,e)=e^{*}(\gamma^{i}e),\quad 0\leq i\leq n-1.

Similarly, we formulate the matching relation and orbital integrals as in
[
22
]
. In particular, for
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})(F_{0}))
and
\gamma\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we define (
s\in\mathbb{C}
)

\Orb(\gamma,f^{\prime},s):=\omega(\gamma)\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})}f^{\prime}(h^{-1}\gamma h)\eta(h)|h|^{s}dh,

where the
transfer factor
on
S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
with respect to
L
is defined by

(2.8)

\omega(\gamma):=\eta(\det(\gamma^{i}e)_{i=0}^{n-1})\in\{\pm 1\}.

We normalize the Haar measure on
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})(F_{0})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat}_{0})(F_{0})
) such that the stabilizer
\mathrm{U}(L^{\flat})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}(L_{0}^{\flat},L_{0}^{\flat,\vee})
) is of volume
1
.

Definition 2.4

(Group Transfers)
.

We say
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})(F_{0}))
and
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0}))
are
transfers
to each other, if for any regular semisimple
\gamma\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
,

\Orb(\gamma,f^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\Orb(g,\,f)&\text{if $\gamma$ matches some $g\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}$,}\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

2.3.
Conjectures

If
L
is a hermitian lattice in
V
, define the
valuation
of
L
as

v(L):=\min_{x\in L,y\in L}v((x,y)_{V}).

We say
L
is
maximal parahoric
if
v(L)+v(L^{\vee})\geq-1
. Recall
L
is a
vertex lattice
if
L\subseteq L^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L
, and the type of
L
is
t(L):=\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}}L^{\vee}/L
. Using an orthogonal basis for
L
, we see that
L
is maximal parahoric if and only if some scalar of
L
or
L^{\vee}
is a vertex lattice.

Define
L_{0}^{\vee}:=L^{\vee}\cap V_{0}
, and the linear dual of
L_{0}^{\vee}
as
L_{0}^{\vee*}:=\{f\in V^{*}_{0}|f(L_{0}^{\vee})\subseteq O_{F_{0}}\}
. Let
S(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})
be the stabilizer of
L_{0}\subseteq L_{0}^{\vee}
inside
S(V_{0})
. We formulate the following
explicit Jacquet–Rallis transfer conjectures
(TC).

Conjecture 2.5
.

(1)

(Semi-Lie transfer for
L
) Let
L
be a maximal parahoric hermitian lattice, then

f_{L}:=1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L}
and
f_{L}^{\prime}:=1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}}

are transfers.

(2)

(Group transfer for the pair
(L,e)
) Let
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
be an orthogonal decomposition of maximal parahoric hermitian lattices. Then
f_{(L,e)}:=1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}
and
f_{(L,e)}^{\prime}:=1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}
are transfers.

By definition, the group TC for the pair
(L,e)
is equivalent to the group TC for the pair
(L^{\vee},e/(e,e)_{V})
.

Remark 2.6
.

Any
F
-basis of
V
gives a trivialization
\wedge^{n}V\cong F
hence a transfer factor
\omega(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}):=\eta(\det(\gamma^{i}u_{1})_{i=0}^{n-1})\in\{\pm 1\}
. For different
F
-basis of
V
, the associated transfer factors may differ up to a constant sign. In Conjecture
2.5
, the transfer factors are defined using a basis of
L
and depends only on
L
. We have

\omega_{L^{\vee}}=\gamma_{V}\omega_{L}

where
\gamma_{V}=\eta(\det V)\in\{\pm 1\}
is the Weil constant of
V
.

We prove the explicit Jacquet–Rallis transfer conjectures.

Theorem 2.7
.

Conjecture
2.5
holds for any maximal parahoric lattice
L
i.e.,
v(L)+v(L^{\vee})\geq-1
e.g. any vertex lattice
L
. Hence by Theorem
3.26
, the group version conjectures
2.5
also hold for any pair
(L,e)
.

For self-dual
L
, the method is similar to
[
2
]
which gives a pure local proof for the Lie algebra version Jacquet–Rallis fundamental lemma (FL). We handle the group version FL for
p=2
which is not covered by
[
2
]
. In general, our strategy is to do reductions and inductions on two boundary cases via double uncertainty principle.

3.
Reductions via relative Cayley maps

In this section, we introduce the relative Cayley map and do reductions of orbits and orbital integrals from group version to semi-Lie version. We have the reductions for
s
-variable orbital integrals, which is used for the reduction of arithmetic transfer conjectures in §
6.3
.

3.1.
The relative Cayley map

Let
V
be a
F
-vector space with a decomposition
V=V^{\flat}\oplus Fe
. For any endomorphism
A^{\prime}\in\End(V)
, we write

A^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\begin{pmatrix}\End(V^{\flat})&V^{\flat}\\
(V^{\flat})^{*}&F\end{pmatrix}=\End(V).

Definition 3.1
.

The
relative Cayley map
is the
\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})
-equivariant rational map

(3.1)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.11055pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.11055pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{c}:\End(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.03021pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.03021pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\End(V^{\flat})\times V^{\flat}\times(V^{\flat})^{*}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.15279pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.15279pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.11055pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.11055pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(A=a+\frac{bc}{1-d},\,b_{1}=\frac{b}{1-d},\,c_{1}=\frac{c}{1-d}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

3.2.
Reduction for unitary groups

Let
V
be a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space with an orthogonal decomposition
V=V^{\flat}\oplus Fe
. For any
g^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}t&u\\
w&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{U}(V),
we have
d=\frac{(g^{\prime}e,e)}{(e,e)}
and
w(x)=\frac{(g^{\prime}x,e)}{(e,e)}
for
x\in V^{\flat}
.

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume that
1-d\not=0
, consider the well-defined relative projection

g=t+\frac{uw}{1-d}:V^{\flat}V^{\flat}.

(1)

We have
g\in\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
. If
x\in V^{\flat}
such that
w(x)=0
, then
g(x)=g^{\prime}(x)
.

(2)

We have
\frac{w}{1-d}(x)=\frac{(gx,\frac{u}{1-d})}{(e,e)}
for
x\in V^{\flat}
.

Moreover, these properties uniquely characterize
g
in terms of
g^{\prime}
. Given
g\in\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
,
u\in V^{\flat}
and
d\not=1
, there exists a unique
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(V)
with these invariants.

Proof.

We have
d=\frac{(g^{\prime}e,e)}{(e,e)}
and
w(x)=\frac{(g^{\prime}x,e)}{(e,e)}
. So for
x\in V^{\flat}
we have
gx-g^{\prime}x=-\lambda(x)(g^{\prime}e-e)

where
\lambda(x):=\frac{(g^{\prime}x,e)}{(g^{\prime}e-e,e)}
. For
x,y\in V^{\flat}
, using
(g^{\prime}e,g^{\prime}y)=(e,y)=0=(g^{\prime}x,g^{\prime}e)=(x,e)
we find

(gx,gy)-(x,y)=(gx,gy)-(g^{\prime}x,g^{\prime}y)=\lambda(x)(e,g^{\prime}y)+\overline{\lambda(y)}(g^{\prime}x,e)+\lambda(x)\overline{\lambda(y)}(g^{\prime}e-e,g^{\prime}e-e)

which is
\lambda(x)\overline{\lambda(y)}(\overline{(g^{\prime}e-e,e)}+(g^{\prime}e-e,e)+(g^{\prime}e-e,g^{\prime}e-e))=0.
Hence
g\in\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
.

If
w(x)=0
, then by definition
gx=tx=g^{\prime}x
. For
x\in V^{\flat}
, we compute
(gx,u)=

(g^{\prime}x-\frac{(g^{\prime}x,e)}{(g^{\prime}e-e,e)}(g^{\prime}e-e),g^{\prime}e-de)=(g^{\prime}x,e)(0-\frac{1-\overline{d}}{d-1}-\overline{d}+\overline{d})=\frac{1-\overline{d}}{1-d}(g^{\prime}x,e).

As
w(x)=\frac{(g^{\prime}x,e)}{(e,e)}
, we get the equality
\frac{w}{1-d}(x)=\frac{(gx,\frac{u}{1-d})}{(e,e)}.
Finally, these properties determine
g^{-1}u
and
g|_{\ker w}
from
g^{\prime}
, hence
g
itself as
w(g^{-1}u)=\frac{(u,e)}{(e,e)}=1-d
is non-zero. Conversely, we can reverse above computations and reconstruct
g^{\prime}
.
∎

Definition 3.3
.

The
unitary relative Cayley map
is the rational map

(3.2)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 21.4792pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.4792pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}:\mathrm{U}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.47075pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 75.47075pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})\times V^{\flat}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.96701pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.96701pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.4792pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.4792pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g=t+\frac{uw}{1-d},\,u_{1}=\frac{u}{1-d}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

The map is equivariant with respect to the natural conjugacy action of
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
on both sides. By Proposition
3.2
, we have

Corollary 3.4
.

We have
\mathfrak{c}(g^{\prime})=(g,u_{1},w_{1})
where
w_{1}(x)=\frac{(gx,u_{1})}{(e,e)}
. The map
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}
induces an isomorphism over the locus where
d\not=1
is fixed. We have equalities of invariants (
i\in\mathbb{Z}
):

(g^{i}u_{1},u_{1})=(e,e)w_{1}(g^{i-1}u_{1}).

Proposition 3.5
.

The element
g^{\prime}
is regular semisimple if and only if
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime})
is regular semisimple.

Proof.

This follows from the
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
-equivariance of the relative Cayley map
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}
.

∎

Therefore, we have a natural reduction of
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
-orbits. We now do reduction for
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
-orbital integrals. Consider a hermitian lattice
L\subseteq V
with an orthogonal decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e_{L}\subseteq V=V^{\flat}\oplus Fe
, where
e_{L}=\lambda_{L}e
for some
\lambda_{L}\in F_{0}^{\times}
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})
with
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime})=(g,u_{1})
. Assume that
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, then for any
h\in\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
,
h^{-1}g^{\prime}h\in\mathrm{U}(L)
holds if and only if

h^{-1}gh\in\mathrm{U}(L^{\flat}),\quad h^{-1}u_{1}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat},\quad h^{-1}u_{1}\in(e,e)(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})^{\vee}.

Proof.

The map
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}
is
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
-equivariant and
d
is
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
-invariant, so we may assume
h=\mathrm{id}
. As
\det(g^{\prime})\in F^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
is a unit, the condition
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(L)
is the same as
g^{\prime}L\subseteq L
. Under the decomposition, using
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
we see that
g^{\prime}L\subseteq L
holds if and only if

t(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat},\quad u_{1}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat},\quad w_{1}(L^{\flat})\subseteq\lambda_{L}O_{F}.

The last two conditions imply
\frac{uw}{1-d}(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat}
. Using this and
g=t+\frac{uw}{1-d}\in\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})
, we may replace the first condition
t(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat}
by
g(L^{\flat})=L^{\flat}
.
By Proposition
3.2
we have
w_{1}(x)=\frac{(gx,u_{1})}{(e,e)}
, hence
w_{1}(L^{\flat})\subseteq\lambda_{L}O_{F}
is equivalent to
(L^{\flat},u_{1})\subseteq\lambda_{L}(e,e)O_{F}
i.e.,
u_{1}\in(e,e)(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})^{\vee}
.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

We do not assume that
g^{\prime}
is regular semi-simple for this proposition. We have similar reduction result for intersection numbers on unitary Rapoport–Zink spaces in §
6.3
.

Corollary 3.8
.

For
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
with
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
and any Haar measure on
\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})(F_{0})
, we have

\Orb(g^{\prime},1_{\mathrm{U}(L)})=\Orb(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime}),1_{\mathrm{U}(L^{\flat})}\times 1_{(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})\cap(e,e)(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})^{\vee}}).

Definition 3.9
.

We say the lattice
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e_{L}
is suitable for reduction if

v_{F}(e_{L},e_{L})=\min_{x,y\in L}v_{F}((x,y)).

By definition,
L
(resp.
L^{\vee}
) is suitable for reduction if and only if
\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}\subseteq(e,e)(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})^{\vee}
(resp.
(e,e)(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})^{\vee}\subseteq\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}
).

Lemma 3.10
.

If
L
is maximal parahoric, then either
L
or
L^{\vee}
is suitable for reduction.

Proof.

Otherwise, we have
v_{F}(e_{L},e_{L})\geq v(L)+1
and
v_{F}(e_{L^{\vee}},e_{L^{\vee}})=-v_{F}(e_{L},e_{L})\geq v(L^{\vee})+1
, which sum together to give
0\geq v(L)+v(L^{\vee})+2\geq 1
, a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

If
L
is suitable for reduction, then for any
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
with
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
we have

\Orb(g^{\prime},1_{\mathrm{U}(L)})=\Orb(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime}),1_{\mathrm{U}(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})}\times 1_{(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat})}).

For
\xi\in F^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
, we define the
\xi
-twisted unitary Cayley map
by

(3.3)

\mathfrak{c}_{U,\xi}(g^{\prime}):=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(\xi g^{\prime}).

For any element
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have
1-d\xi\in O_{F}^{\times}
for infinitely many
\xi\in F^{N_{F/F_{0}}=1}
because
\#\{x\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}|\Nm_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}/\mathbb{F}_{q}}(x)=1\}=q+1>1
. As
\xi
is a unit, the elements
g^{\prime}
and
\xi g^{\prime}
have same orbital integrals for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0}))
. Therefore, the reduction of orbital integrals holds for any regular semi-simple orbit in
\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})
under suitable
\xi
-twisted unitary Cayley maps.

3.3.
Reduction for symmetric spaces

Consider a decomposition
V=V^{\flat}\oplus Fe
of
F
-vector spaces. Choose a basis of
V^{\flat}
to endow
V^{\flat}
(resp.
V
) a
F_{0}
-rational structure
V_{0}^{\flat}
(resp.
V_{0}
by adding
e
), which induces a
F/F_{0}
semi-linear involution
\overline{(-)}
on
V
with fixed subspace
V_{0}
. Denote also by
\overline{(-)}
the induced involutions on
V^{*}
and
\End(V)
. We have the embedding of symmetric spaces:

S(V_{0}^{\flat})=\{\gamma\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})|\gamma\overline{\gamma}=\mathrm{id}\}\hookrightarrow S(V_{0})=\{\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GL}(V)|\gamma^{\prime}\overline{\gamma^{\prime}}=\mathrm{id}\}.

Under the decomposition
V=V^{\flat}\oplus Fe
, we write any
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})
as
\gamma^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}.

Proposition 3.12
.

Assume that
1-d\not=0
, consider the well-defined relative projection

\gamma=a+\frac{bc}{1-d}:V^{\flat}V^{\flat}.

(1)

We have
\gamma\in S(V^{\flat}_{0})
. If
x\in V^{\flat}
such that
c(x)=0
, then
\gamma(x)=\gamma^{\prime}(x)
.

(2)

We have
\gamma\overline{\frac{b}{1-d}}=\frac{b}{1-d},\quad\overline{\frac{c}{1-d}}\gamma=\frac{c}{1-d}.

Moreover, these properties uniquely characterize
\gamma
in terms of
\gamma^{\prime}
. Given
\gamma\in S(V_{0}^{\flat}),\,b\in V,\,c\in V^{*}
and
d\not=1
satisfying these properties, there exists a unique
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})
with these invariants.

Proof.

By definition, if
x\in V^{\flat}
such that
c(x)=0
, then we have
\gamma(x)=a(x)=\gamma^{\prime}(x)
. From the equality
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\overline{a}&\overline{b}\\
\overline{c}&\overline{d}\end{pmatrix}=\mathrm{id}
, we have

a\overline{a}=\mathrm{id}-b\overline{c},\quad a\overline{b}=-\overline{d}b,\quad c\overline{a}=-d\overline{c},\quad c\overline{b}=1-d\overline{d}.

We find
\gamma\overline{\gamma}=a\overline{a}+a\frac{\overline{b}\overline{c}}{1-\overline{d}}+\frac{bc}{1-d}\overline{a}+\frac{bc}{1-d}\frac{\overline{b}\overline{c}}{1-\overline{d}}
is equal to
\mathrm{id}+b\overline{c}(-1-\frac{\overline{d}}{1-\overline{d}}-\frac{d}{1-d}+\frac{1-d\overline{d}}{(1-d)(1-\overline{d})})=\mathrm{id}
.
Therefore we have
\gamma\in S(V_{0}^{\flat})
. We have

\gamma\overline{b}=a\overline{b}+c\overline{b}\frac{b}{1-d}=-\overline{d}b+(1-d\overline{d})\frac{b}{1-d}=\frac{1-\overline{d}}{1-d}b,

and similarly
\overline{c}{\gamma}=\overline{c}a+\overline{c}\frac{bc}{1-d}=-\overline{d}{c}+(1-d\overline{d})\frac{c}{1-d}=\frac{1-\overline{d}}{1-d}c
. The second proposition follows. Conversely, the uniqueness and reconstruction of
g^{\prime}
follows by reversing the above computations.
∎

Definition 3.13
.

The
untwisted symmetric relative Cayley map
is the
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
-equivariant rational map

(3.4)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.4104pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.4104pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{c}_{S}:S(V_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.52365pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.52365pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S(V^{\flat}_{0})\times V^{\flat}\times(V^{\flat})^{*}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.99142pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\gamma^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99144pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.4104pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.4104pt\raise-8.69836pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\gamma,\,b_{1}=\frac{b}{1-d},\,c_{1}=\frac{c}{1-d}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Now we modify
\mathfrak{c}_{S}
to make the image lying in
S(V^{\flat}_{0})\times V^{\flat}_{0}\times(V^{\flat}_{0})^{*}
.

Definition 3.14
.

A
twisting element
for
\gamma\in S(V_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
is any
B_{\gamma}\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})(F)
such that

\gamma=B_{\gamma}\overline{B_{\gamma}}^{-1}=\overline{B_{\gamma}}^{-1}B_{\gamma}.

By Hilbert
90
for
F/F_{0}
, there is always some
B\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})
such that
\gamma=B\overline{B}^{-1}
. Here the condition is slightly stronger. In particular, we have
\gamma B_{\gamma}=B_{\gamma}\gamma
and
\gamma\overline{B_{\gamma}}=\overline{B_{\gamma}}\gamma
.

Example 3.15
.

Choose any
\xi\in F^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
such that
\det(1+\xi\gamma)\not=0
. Write
\xi=a/\bar{a}
for some
a\in F^{\times}
, then
a^{-1}(1+\xi\gamma)
is a twisting element for
\gamma
.

Let
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})
with
1-d\not=0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{S}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,b_{1},c_{1})
. Choose a twisting element
B_{\gamma}
for
\gamma
, set

b_{2}:=B_{\gamma}^{-1}b_{1},\quad c_{2}:=c_{1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}.

Proposition 3.16
.

Choose any twisting element
B_{\gamma}
for
\gamma
as above.

(1)

We have
(b_{2},c_{2})\in V_{0}^{\flat}\times(V_{0}^{\flat})^{*}
, and we have following equalities of invariants (
i\in\mathbb{Z}
):

c_{2}\gamma^{i+1}b_{2}=c_{1}\gamma^{i}b_{1}.

(2)

The
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})
-orbit of
(\gamma,b_{2},c_{2})
doesn’t depends on the choice of
B_{\gamma}
and only depends on the
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})
-orbit of
\gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.12
, we have
\gamma\overline{b_{1}}=b_{1}
,
\overline{c_{1}}\gamma=c_{1}
. From

\gamma=B_{\gamma}\overline{B_{\gamma}}^{-1}
, we see

\overline{B_{\gamma}^{-1}b_{1}}=B_{\gamma}^{-1}b_{1},\quad\overline{c_{1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}}=c_{1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}

which shows
(b_{2},c_{2})\in V_{0}^{\flat}\times(V_{0}^{\flat})^{*}
. Since
\gamma
commutes with
B_{\gamma}
and
\overline{B_{\gamma}}
, we get

c_{2}\gamma^{i+1}b_{2}=c_{1}\gamma^{i+1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}B_{\gamma}^{-1}b_{1}=c_{1}\gamma^{i}b_{1}.

Finally, if
B_{\gamma},B_{\gamma}^{\prime}
are two twisting elements for
\gamma
, then
h_{0}:=B_{\gamma}^{-1}B_{\gamma}^{\prime}
is in
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
and commutes with
\gamma
. Hence

h_{0}.(\gamma,b_{2},c_{2})=(\gamma,b_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}).
And for any
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
, the element
h^{-1}B_{\gamma}h
is a twisting element for
h^{-1}\gamma h
. Property
(2)
holds.
∎

Definition 3.17
.

The
relative symmetric Cayley map
on
\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat}_{0})
-orbits is the map between
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
-orbits

(3.5)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.61113pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.61113pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}}:[S(V_{0})(F_{0})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.61113pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 58.61113pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[(S(V^{\flat}_{0})\times V^{\flat}_{0}\times(V^{\flat})^{*}_{0})(F_{0})]}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.99142pt\raise-9.00946pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\gamma^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99144pt\raise-9.00946pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.31822pt\raise-9.00946pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.31822pt\raise-9.00946pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\gamma,\,b_{2}:=B_{\gamma}^{-1}b_{1},\,c_{2}:=c_{1}\overline{B_{\gamma}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
B_{\gamma}
is any twisting element of
\gamma
. This is well-defined by Proposition
3.16
. We also write
\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,\,b_{2},\,c_{2})
with respect to a chosen twisting element
B_{\gamma}
.

Proposition 3.18
.

The element
\gamma^{\prime}
is regular semisimple if and only if
\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,b_{2},c_{2})
is regular semisimple.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.16
we have
c_{2}\gamma^{i+1}b_{2}=c_{1}\gamma^{i}b_{1}
. So by the same argument in Proposition
3.5
we see the equivalences:
\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,b_{2},c_{2})
is regular semisimple
\Leftrightarrow

\mathfrak{c}_{S}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,b_{1},c_{1})
is regular semisimple
\Leftrightarrow

\gamma^{\prime}
is regular semisimple.
∎

An element
\gamma\in S(V_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
is called
integral
if its characteristic polynomial
\text{char}(T\mathrm{id}_{V^{\flat}}-\gamma)
lies in
O_{F}[T]
. Recall that
F/F_{0}
is unramified and
p\geq 2
.

Proposition 3.19
.

(Integral Hilbert
90
)
Let
\gamma\in S(V_{0}^{\flat})(F_{0})
be an integral element and
R=O_{F}[\gamma]\subseteq\End(V^{\flat})
be the commutative
O_{F}
-algebra generated by
\gamma
. Then there exists an element
B_{\gamma}\in(O_{F}[\gamma])^{\times}
such that
\gamma=B_{\gamma}\overline{B_{\gamma}}^{-1}
.

Proof.

As
\gamma
is integral and
\det(\gamma)\in F^{Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
is a unit, we see
R
is finite flat over
O_{F}
. As
\overline{\gamma}=\gamma^{-1}
lies in
R
by Cayley–Hamilton theorem, we see
R
is stable under the involution
\overline{(-)}
on
\End(V^{\flat})
. Consider the finite flat commutative
O_{F_{0}}
-subalgebra

R_{0}=\{x\in R|x=\bar{x}\}
of
R
.

The ring map
R_{0}\otimes_{O_{F_{0}}}O_{F}R
is an isomorphism: we base change along the étale morphism
O_{F_{0}}O_{F}
where things are trivial. If
p>2
, we can see this by observing any
x\in R
is in the image by the formula
x=\frac{x+\bar{x}}{2}+\frac{x+\bar{x}}{2\delta}\delta
for chosen
\delta\in O_{F}^{\times}
such that
\overline{\delta}=-\delta
. We are done by the commutative algebra lemma
3.20
below. ∎

Lemma 3.20
.

For any finite flat commutative
O_{F_{0}}
-algebra
A_{0}
, consider the induced involution
\overline{(-)}
on
A=A_{0}\otimes_{O_{F_{0}}}O_{F}
given by
a\otimes ba\otimes\overline{b}
. If
x\in A^{\times}
satisfies
x\overline{x}=1
, then there exists
y\in A^{\times}
such that
x=y\overline{y}^{-1}
.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
24
, Lem. 8.6]
and is a variant of Hilbert 90 for vector bundles on the semi-local ring
A_{0}
.
∎

Now we do reduction for orbital integrals. Consider any lattice
L
in
V
with a decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e_{L}

that is stable under
\overline{(-)}
, where
e_{L}=\lambda_{L}e
for some
\lambda_{L}\in F_{0}^{\times}
. Then
L_{0}:=\{x\in L|\overline{x}=x\}
is an
O_{F_{0}}
-lattice in
V_{0}
such that
L=L_{0}\otimes_{O_{F_{0}}}O_{F}
. Consider the stabilizer for
L
:

S(L):=\{\gamma\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})\,|\,\gamma L=L\}.

Proposition 3.21
.

Let
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})
with
\mathfrak{c}_{S}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,b_{1},c_{1})
. Assume that
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, then

(1)

Assume that
\gamma
is integral. Choose the twisting element
B_{\gamma}\in O_{F}[\gamma]^{\times}
as in Proposition
3.19
, and set

\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})=(\gamma,b_{2}=B_{\gamma}^{-1}b_{1},c_{2}=c_{1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}).

For any
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat}_{0})(F_{0})
, we have
h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}h\in S(L)
if and only if

h^{-1}\gamma h\in S(L^{\flat}),\quad h^{-1}b_{2}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0},\quad c_{2}h\in(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0})^{*}.

(2)

If
\gamma
is not integral, then there is no
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat}_{0})(F_{0})
such that

h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}h\in S(L)
or
h^{-1}\gamma h\in S(L^{\flat})
.

Proof.

Because
\det(h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}h)
is a unit, the condition
h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}h\in S(L)
is equivalent to
h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}hL\subseteq L
. Under the decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
, we see (using
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
) that
h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}hL\subseteq L
if and only if

h^{-1}ah(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat},\quad h^{-1}b_{1}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat},\quad c_{1}h(L^{\flat})\subseteq\lambda_{L}O_{F}.

The latter two conditions imply
\frac{bc}{1-d}(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat}
. As
h^{-1}\gamma h=h^{-1}ah+\frac{bc}{1-d}
, above condition is equivalent to

h^{-1}\gamma h(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat},\quad h^{-1}b_{1}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat},\quad c_{1}h(L^{\flat})\subseteq\lambda_{L}O_{F}.

In particular
\gamma
must be integral, the second part follows.

Assume that
h^{-1}\gamma h(L^{\flat})\subseteq L^{\flat}
holds. By Proposition
3.19
,
B_{\gamma}
and
B_{\gamma}^{-1}
lie in
O_{F}[\gamma]
in particular
\det(B_{\gamma})\in O_{F}^{\times}
. Hence we have
h^{-1}B_{\gamma}h(L^{\flat})=L^{\flat}
and
h^{-1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}h(L^{\flat})=L^{\flat}
.

So the condition
h^{-1}b_{1}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}
(resp.
c_{1}h(L^{\flat})\subseteq\lambda_{L}O_{F}
) is equivalent to the condition
h^{-1}B_{\gamma}^{-1}h(h^{-1}b_{1})=h^{-1}b_{2}\in\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}
(resp.
c_{2}h(L^{\flat})=c_{1}h(h^{-1}\overline{B_{\gamma}}h)(L^{\flat})=c_{1}h(L^{\flat})\subseteq\lambda_{L}O_{F}
). As
(h^{-1}b_{2},\,c_{2}h)\in V_{0}^{\flat}\times(V_{0}^{\flat})^{*}
, the result follows.

∎

From now on, we choose and fix the twisting element
B_{\gamma}\in O_{F}[\gamma]^{\times}
as in Proposition
3.19
.

Corollary 3.22
.

For
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
with
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, we have
(
s\in\mathbb{C}
)

\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},1_{S(L)},s)=\Orb(\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}),1_{S(L^{\flat})}\times 1_{\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0}}\times 1_{(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0})^{*}},s).

In general, for any collection of lattices
\{L_{i}\}
of the form
L_{i}=L^{\flat}_{i}\oplus O_{F}\lambda_{L_{i}}e
, let
S(\{L_{i}\})
be the stabilizer of all lattices
L_{i}
inside
S(V_{0})
. Then we have

\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},1_{S(\{L_{i}\})},s)=\Orb(\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}),1_{S(\{L^{\flat}\})}\times 1_{\cap_{i}\lambda_{L_{i}}^{-1}(L^{\flat}_{i})_{0}}\times 1_{(\sum_{i}\lambda_{L_{i}}^{-1}(L^{\flat}_{i})_{0})^{*}},s).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.21
, both integrals are zero unless
\gamma
is integral. Assume that
\gamma
is integral and choose
B_{\gamma}
as in Proposition
3.21
, then two integrals have same supports. So we only need to show the equality of transfer factors
\omega(\gamma,b_{2},c_{2})=\omega(\gamma^{\prime}).
From
\gamma^{i}b_{2}=B_{\gamma}^{-1}\gamma^{i}b_{1}
and
\det(B_{\gamma})\in O_{F}^{\times}
, we have

\omega(\gamma,b_{2},c_{2})=\omega(\gamma,b_{1},c_{1})=\omega(\gamma,b,c).

By induction on
i\geq 0
, we see
\gamma^{i}b-a^{i}(b)
is in the
F
-span of
\{b,ab,\dots,a^{i-1}b\}
so
\omega(\gamma,b,c)=\omega(a,b,c)
. As
\gamma^{\prime}(x)=a(x)+c(x)e

(x\in V^{\flat})
, we have

\omega(\gamma^{\prime})=\det(e\wedge\gamma^{\prime}e\dots\wedge\gamma^{\prime n-1}e)=\det(e\wedge b\wedge\gamma b\dots\wedge\gamma^{n-2}b)=\omega(\gamma,b,c).

The result for
L
follows. The reduction for any collection of lattices follows along the same line as in Proposition
3.21
and above arguments.
∎

For any
\xi\in F^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
, define the
\xi
-twisted symmetric Cayley map by

\mathfrak{c}_{S,\xi}(\gamma^{\prime}):=\mathfrak{c}_{S}(\xi\gamma^{\prime}),\quad\mathfrak{c}_{S,\xi}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):=\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\xi\gamma^{\prime}).

For
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have
1-d\xi\in O_{F}^{\times}
for infinitely many
\xi\in F^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
as
\#\{x\in k_{F}|\Nm_{k_{F}/k_{F_{0}}}(x)=1\}=q+1>1
. The orbital integral is the same for
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\xi\gamma^{\prime}
. Therefore, the reduction of orbital integrals holds for any regular semi-simple
\gamma^{\prime}
, using suitable
\xi
-twisted symmetric Cayley maps.

3.4.
Matching and the equivalence of two transfer conjectures

Return to our transfer Conjecture
2.5
. Choose an orthogonal
F
-basis
\{e_{i}\}
of
V^{\flat}
, and extend it to an orthogonal basis of
V
by adding
e
.

Proposition 3.23
.

If
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matches
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, then
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime})=(g,u_{1})\in(\mathrm{U}(V^{\flat})\times V^{\flat})(F_{0})
matches

(e,e).\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):=(\gamma,b_{2},(e,e)c_{2})\in(S(V^{\flat}_{0})\times V^{\flat}_{0}\times(V^{\flat}_{0})^{*})(F_{0}).

Proof.

We only need to check invariants of
(g,u_{1})
and
(\gamma,b_{2},(e,e)c_{2})
match. If
g^{\prime}
matches
\gamma^{\prime}
, then there exist
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})
such that
h^{-1}g^{\prime}h=\gamma^{\prime}
. This gives

h^{-1}u=b,\quad wh=c,\quad h^{-1}th=a,\quad d_{g^{\prime}}=d_{\gamma^{\prime}}=d.

As
\gamma=a+\frac{bc}{1-d}
,
g=t+\frac{uw}{1-d}
, we see
h^{-1}gh=\gamma
. So we have

\text{char}(T\text{id}_{V^{\flat}}-g)=\text{char}(T\text{id}_{V^{\flat}}-\gamma)

and
c_{1}\gamma^{i}b_{1}=w_{1}g^{i}u_{1}
for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Proposition
3.2
, we have
(g^{i}u_{1},u_{1})=(e,e)w_{1}(g^{i-1}u_{1})
. By Proposition
3.16
,
c_{2}\gamma^{i}b_{2}=c_{1}\gamma^{i-1}b_{1}
, so
(e,e)c_{2}\gamma^{i}b_{2}=(e,e)c_{1}\gamma^{i-1}b_{1}
. Therefore we have
(g^{i}u_{1},u_{1})=(e,e)c_{2}\gamma^{i}b_{2}
for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. So
(g,u_{1})
matches
(\gamma,b_{2},(e,e)c_{2})
.
∎

Consider any
\overline{(-)}
-stable hermitian lattice
L
in
V
with an orthogonal decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e_{L}
,
e_{L}=\lambda_{L}e
for some
\lambda_{L}\in F_{0}^{\times}
. We have
L=L_{0}\otimes_{O_{F_{0}}}O_{F}
for the lattice
L_{0}=\{x\in L|\overline{x}=x\}
. Apply Corollary
3.22
to the collection of
L
and
L^{\vee}
, we see

Proposition 3.24
.

For
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{rs}
with
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, we have

\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},1_{S(L,L^{\vee})})=\Orb((e,e).\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}),f^{\prime}).

where
(e,e).\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):=(\gamma,b_{2},(e,e)c_{2})
and

f^{\prime}=1_{S(L^{\flat},(L^{\flat})^{\vee})}\times 1_{\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0}\cap(e,e)(\lambda^{-1}_{L}L^{\flat}_{0})^{\vee}}\times 1_{(e,e)(\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0})^{*}\cap((\lambda_{L}^{-1}L^{\flat}_{0})^{\vee})^{*}}.

Corollary 3.25
.

If
L
is suitable for reduction (Definition
3.9
), then for
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{rs}
with
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
we have

\Orb(\gamma^{\prime},1_{S(L,L^{\vee})})=\Orb((e,e).\mathfrak{c}_{S}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}),f^{\prime}).

where
