f^{\prime}:=1_{S(\lambda^{-1}_{L}L^{\flat},(\lambda^{-1}_{L}L^{\flat})^{\vee})}\times 1_{\lambda^{-1}_{L}L^{\flat}_{0}}\times 1_{((\lambda^{-1}_{L}L^{\flat})^{\vee}_{0})^{*}}.

Theorem 3.26
.

(1)

If
L
is suitable for reduction, then the group transfer conjecture
2.5
for
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e_{L}
is equivalent to the semi-Lie transfer conjecture
2.5
for
L_{{\mathrm{new}}}:=\lambda^{-1}_{L}L^{\flat}
.

(2)

If
L^{\vee}
is suitable for reduction, then the group transfer conjecture
2.5
for
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e_{L}
is equivalent to the semi-Lie transfer conjecture
2.5
for
L_{{\mathrm{new}}}:=\lambda^{-1}_{L^{\vee}}L^{\vee,\flat}
.

Proof.

By duality, we only need to show the first part. By Corollary
3.25
, Corollary
3.11
and Proposition
3.23
, semi-Lie version TC implies group version TC. Moreover, as relative Cayley maps are surjective on orbits (for given
d
) by Proposition
3.6
and
3.12
, we can reverse the process.
∎

In particular, if
L
is maximal parahoric then either
(1)
or
(2)
holds. The new lattice
L_{\mathrm{new}}
is still maximal parahoric, so we can continue the process once we could relate (boundary) cases of semi-Lie version transfer conjectures to lower rank group version transfer conjectures.

4.
Transfers for all vertex lattices

In this section, we give the proof of explicit transfer Conjecture
2.5
for any
p
-adic field
F_{0}
. Consider the space of regular semisimple
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
-orbits

\textstyle{B=\bigr[S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}\bigr]_{\mathrm{rs}}(F_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{U}(V)\times V]_{\mathrm{rs}}(F_{0})\coprod\bigl[\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V}\bigr]_{\mathrm{rs}}(F_{0}).}

where
\mathbb{V}
is the nearby hermitian space of
V
.

Endow the
n
-dimensional hermitian space
V
with the
F_{0}
-valued quadratic form
q(u):=(u,u)_{V}
, and the space
V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
with the
F_{0}
-valued quadratic form
q^{\prime}(b,c):=c(b)
. They induce a map on orbit space
B
:

q=q^{\prime}:BF_{0},\quad[(\gamma,b,c)]c(b).

Fix an unramified quadratic character
\psi
of
F_{0}
. We denote by

•

\mathcal{F}_{U}
the Fourier transform on
V
with respect to the additive character
\psi\circ q
,

•

\mathcal{F}_{S}
the Fourier transform on
V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
with respect to the additive character
\psi\circ q^{\prime}
.

We always use self-dual Haar measure on
V
and
V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
for the Fourier transforms. Then for a
\overline{(-)}
-stable hermitian lattice
L\subseteq V
of type
t
, we have

\vol(L)=q^{-t/2}_{F}=1\times q^{-t}=\vol(L_{0}\times(L_{0}^{\vee})^{*}).

4.1.
Double uncertainty principle on distributions

We have the Weil representation of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})
and
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)
respectively, by acting on the linear factors
V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
and
V
respectively. Concretely, the action of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)
is given by

(4.3)

\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.f(g,u)=\psi(t(u,u)_{V})f(g,u),

(4.4)

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.f(g,u)=\gamma_{V}\mathcal{F}_{U}f(g,u),

where
\gamma_{V}:=\eta(\det(V))\in\{\pm 1\}
is the Weil constant of
V
.
The action of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})
is given by

(4.5)

\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.f^{\prime}(\gamma,b,c)=\psi(t(cb))f^{\prime}(\gamma,b,c),

(4.6)

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.f^{\prime}(\gamma,b,c)=\mathcal{F}_{S}f^{\prime}(\gamma,b,c).

For
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)
, define the function

\Orb((\gamma,b,c),f)=\begin{cases}\Orb(g,u,f)&\text{ if $(\gamma,b,c)$ matches $(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)_{\mathrm{rs}}(F_{0})$}\\
0,\,\,\text{else}.&\end{cases}

By pushforward, we define two
space of orbital integral functions

\Orb_{U},\Orb_{S}\subseteq C^{\infty}(B)
:

\Orb_{S}:=\{\Orb(-,f^{\prime}):(\gamma,b,c)\Orb((\gamma,b,c),f^{\prime})|f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})\},

\Orb_{U}=\{\Orb(-,f):(\gamma,b,c)\Orb((\gamma,b,c),f)|f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)\}.

By
[
67
, Theroem A.1]
, the Weil representations of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})
and
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)
descend to
\Orb_{U}
and
\Orb_{S}
, and agree on the intersection
\Orb_{U}\cap\Orb_{S}
. In other words,

Proposition 4.1
.

There is an action of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on the linear subspace

\Orb_{U}+\Orb_{S}\subseteq C^{\infty}(B)

compatible with the Weil representation on
V
and
V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
. And
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)\times V),f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})
are transfers, if and only if
\Orb(-,f)-\Orb(-,f^{\prime})\in\Orb_{U}+\Orb_{S}
is zero.

Remark 4.2
.

The proof of
[
67
, Theroem A.1]
is via local trace formula, which is eventually reduced to the fact that orbital integral function is locally integrable and Fourier transform on a quadratic space preserves the
L^{2}
-norm. Therefore, it also holds for
p=2
.

Using an orthogonal basis for
L
, we see the valuation

v(L):=\min_{u\in L}v((u,u)_{V})
is equal to the valuation

v(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee}):=\min_{b\in L_{0},\,c\in(L_{0}^{\vee})^{*}}v(c(b)).
We introduce the difference function

(4.7)

\Phi_{L}=\Orb(-,1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L})-\Orb(-,1_{S(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{(L_{0}^{\vee})^{*}})\in C^{\infty}(B).

By Proposition
4.1
, to prove Theorem
2.7
for
L
, we only need to show
\Phi_{L}=0
. In other words, both matching part and vanishing part of orbital integrals follow from
\Phi_{L}=0
.

The following double uncertainty principle says the support of a function and its “Fourier transform” can’t be both too small, unless the function is zero.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
a_{1},a_{2}
be two integers with
a_{1}+a_{2}\geq-1
. Assume that
\Phi\in\Orb_{U}+\Orb_{S}
satisfies
\Phi(x)=0
for all
x\in B
with
v(q(x))\leq a_{1}
, and
\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.\Phi(x)=0
for all
x\in B
with
v(q(x))\leq a_{2}
.
Then
\Phi=0
.

Proof.

Let
k=-a_{1}-1
and
k^{\prime}=-a_{2}-1
so
k+k^{\prime}\leq-1
. By assumption, we have

\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.\Phi=\Phi
for all
t\in\varpi^{k}O_{F_{0}}
and
\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.\Phi=\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\Phi
for all
t\in\varpi^{k^{\prime}}O_{F_{0}}
.
The subgroups

\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
\varpi^{k}O_{F_{0}}&1\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi^{k^{\prime}}O_{F_{0}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
generates the whole group
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
. Hence
\Phi
is fixed by
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
. As
\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.\Phi([\gamma,b,c])=\psi(t(cb))\Phi([\gamma,b,c])
for any
t\in F_{0}
, we see
\Phi=0
.
∎

Proposition 4.4
.

We have

(1)

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.\Phi_{L}=\vol(L)\Phi_{L^{\vee}}.

(2)

\Phi_{L}(x)=0
for all
x\in B
with
v(q(x))\leq v(L)-1
. And
\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.\Phi_{L}(x)=0
for all
x\in B
with
v(q(x))\leq v(L^{\vee})-1
.

Proof.

The first part follows from that
q(u)=q^{\prime}(u_{1},u_{2})
is an invariant for an orbit
x=(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in B
, and that
v(L)=v(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})
. By definition,
\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.\Phi_{L}
is given by

\vol(L)\Orb(1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L^{\vee}},-)-\vol(L_{0}\times(L_{0}^{\vee})^{*})\gamma_{V}\Orb(1_{S(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee}}\times 1_{L_{0}^{*}},-).

By Remark
2.6
, we have

\omega_{L^{\vee}}=\gamma_{V}\omega_{L}.

And
\vol(L)=\vol((L_{0}\times L_{0}^{\vee})^{*})=|L^{\vee}/L|^{1/2}
, hence

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\Phi_{L}=\vol(L)\Phi_{L^{\vee}}
. Then the second part follows directly.
∎

4.2.
Proof of TC assuming inductions on boundary cases

We now prove Theorem
2.7
.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
L
be a rank
n
maximal parahoric hermitian
O_{F}
-lattice i.e.,
v(L)+v(L^{\vee})\geq-1
. Then the semi-Lie transfer Conjecture
2.5
for
L
holds.

Proof.

We do induction on the rank of
L
. The case
n=1
is standard and works for any hermitian lattice
L
(see also the computation in §
9.1
). Now assume that Conjecture
2.5
holds for all maximal parahoric lattices of rank
\leq n-1
.

As
v(L)+v(L^{\vee})\geq-1
, by Proposition
4.4
and Proposition
4.3
, to show the conjecture for
L
we just need to prove

\Phi_{L}(x)=0
if
v(q(x))=v(L)
, and
\Phi_{L^{\vee}}(x)=0
if
v(q(x))=v(L^{\vee})
. By symmetry, we only need to deal with
L
. By Lemma
4.6
below, we see that
\Phi_{L}(x)=0
is implied by the group version transfer conjecture for
(L,e)
, which is equivalent to the semi-Lie version conjecture for the rank
n-1
lattice
L^{\flat}
by Proposition
3.26
, hence is true by induction. The result follows.
∎

Let
e\in L
be a vector with maximal length i.e.,
v((e,e)_{V})=v(L)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Semi-Lie transfer conjecture for
L
in the boundary case
v(q(x))=v(L)
is implied by the group version transfer conjecture for the pair
(L,e)
.

In the following three subsections, we prove Lemma
4.6
.

4.3.
Integral transitivity on maximal length vectors

We establish two integral transitivity lemmas for the induction on boundary cases. Recall that
F/F_{0}
is unramified, the norm map is surjective on units i.e.,
\Nm_{F/F_{0}}(O_{F}^{\times})=O_{F_{0}}^{\times}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

For any
O_{F}
-hermitian lattice
L
,
v(L)=\min_{x\in L}v_{F}((x,x))=\min_{x,y\in L}v_{F}((x,y)).

Proof.

We have an orthogonal basis of
L
(
p\geq 2
) by
[
23
, Section 7]
. The result follows directly.
∎

Let
L
be an
O_{F}
-hermitian lattice. Let
e\in L
be a vector with maximal length i.e.,
v((e,e))=v(L)
. By Lemma
4.7
, we have
\frac{(x,e)}{(e,e)}\in O_{F}
for all
x\in L
. Hence there is an orthogonal decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
for
L^{\flat}:=\{x\in L|(x,e)=0\}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

(Integral transitivity)
Choose a vector
x_{0}\in L
with maximal length. Then the compact open subgroup
\mathrm{U}(L)
of
\mathrm{U}(V)
acts transitively on the sphere

S_{x_{0}}=\{x\in L|(x,x)=(x_{0},x_{0})\}.

Proof.

For any
x\in S_{x_{0}}
, we get a splitting
L=L_{1}\oplus O_{F}x_{0}
. Choose a maximal vector of
L_{1}
and keep doing splitting, finally we extend
x
to an orthonormal basis
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n-1},x\}
of
L
. For any other
x^{\prime}\in S_{x_{0}}
, we get another orthonormal basis
\{e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{n-1}^{\prime},x^{\prime}\}
of
L
. By classifications of hermitian lattices, we can assume that
(e_{i},e_{i})=(e_{i}^{\prime},e_{i}^{\prime})\in\varpi^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
1\leq i\leq n-1
after multiplying
e_{i}
by units in
O_{F_{0}}^{\times}
and rearrange the order. Then the endomorphism
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V)
sending
e_{i}
to
e_{i}^{\prime}
and
x
to
x^{\prime}
is inside the compact open subgroup
\mathrm{U}(L)
. The result follows.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

For integral transitivity on “smaller spheres” and integral Witt’s theorem, see
[
44
]
.

Now let
L_{1},L_{2}
be two lattices inside a
F_{0}
-vector space
V_{0}
. We say a pair
(u_{1},u_{2})\in L_{1}\times L_{2}^{*}
is of maximal length if we have
v(u_{2}u_{1})=v(L_{1},L_{2}):=\min_{x\in L_{1},y\in L_{2}^{*}}v(y(x))
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Choose a pair
(u_{1},u_{2})\in L_{1}\times L_{2}^{*}
of maximal length. Let
V_{0}^{\flat}=\mathrm{Ker}(u_{2})
, and
(L_{i})^{\flat}
be the projection of
L_{i}
to
V_{0}^{\flat}
(
i=1,2
). Then
V_{0}=V_{0}^{\flat}\oplus F_{0}u_{1}
, and

L_{1}=L_{1}^{\flat}\oplus O_{F_{0}}u_{1}\,\,L_{2}=L_{2}^{\flat}\oplus O_{F_{0}}\varpi^{-v(L_{1},L_{2})}u_{1}.

And
L_{2}^{*}=(L_{2}^{\flat})^{*}\oplus O_{F_{0}}u_{2}
.

Proof.

For any
x\in V_{0}
, we have
x=(x-\frac{u_{2}(x)}{u_{2}(u_{1})}u_{1})+\frac{u_{2}(x)}{u_{2}(u_{1})}u_{1}\in V_{0}^{\flat}\oplus F_{0}u_{1}
.
The first property follows. Now set
k=-v(L_{1},L_{2})
. For any
f_{2}\in L_{2}^{*}
, we have
f_{2}(\varpi^{k}u_{1})\in\varpi^{k+v(L_{1},L_{2})}O_{F_{0}}=O_{F_{0}}
, hence
\varpi^{k}u_{1}\in L_{2}
. For
x\in L_{2}
, there exists
a\in O_{F_{0}}
such that
u_{2}(x-a\varpi^{k}u_{1})=0
. So we have the decomposition
L_{2}=L_{2}^{\flat}\oplus O_{F_{0}}\varpi^{k}u_{1}
. Taking its dual, we see
L_{2}^{*}=L_{2}^{\flat*}\oplus O_{F_{0}}u_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

(Integral transitivity)
Choose a pair
(u_{1},u_{2})\in L_{1}\times L_{2}^{*}
of maximal length. Then the compact open subgroup
\mathrm{GL}(L_{1},L_{2})
(stabilizer of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
) of
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
acts transitively on the sphere

S_{u_{1},u_{2}}=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in L_{1}\times L_{2}^{*}|u_{2}u_{1}=x_{2}x_{1}\}.

Proof.

Apply the Lemma
4.10
several times to extend
u_{1}
to a basis
\{e_{i}\}
of
L_{0}
(hence of
V_{0}
) such that
u_{2}
is a multiple of the dual basis of
u_{1}
and
L_{2}
has a basis
\{\varpi^{k_{i}}e_{i}^{*}\}
. Similarly, we extend
(x_{1},x_{2})
to another basis
\{e_{i}^{\prime}\}
of
L_{0}
. Then the automorphism
h
sending the basis
\{e_{i}^{\prime}\}
to
\{e_{i}\}
lies in
S(L_{1},L_{2})
and we have
h^{-1}u_{1}=x_{1},u_{2}h=x_{2}.

∎

4.4.
Induction on boundary cases: unitary groups

Recall that
e
is a fixed vector in
L
of maximal length, and we have a decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
. On the unitary side, we now show orbital integrals for
L
in the boundary case
v(q(x))=v(L)
can be reduced to orbital integrals for
(L,e)
.

Consider
y=(g_{0},u_{0})\in[\mathrm{U}(V)\times V]_{rs}(F_{0})
such that
v((u_{0},u_{0})_{V})=v(L)
. Conjugate
(g_{0},u_{0})
by an element in
\mathrm{U}(V)
, we can assume that
u_{0}\in L_{0}\subseteq V_{0}
. By Lemma
4.8
, we can even assume
u_{0}=e
.

Proposition 4.12
.

Assume that
u_{0}\in L_{0}
and
v((u_{0},u_{0})_{V})=v(L)
, we have

\Orb((g_{0},u_{0}),1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L})=\Orb(g_{0},1_{\mathrm{U}(L)})

where the right hand side is group version orbital integral for the pair
(L,u_{0})
.

Proof.

Let
h\in\mathrm{U}(V)
be any element with
1_{L}(h^{-1}u_{0})\not=0
. By Lemma
4.8
, there exists
h_{1}\in\mathrm{U}(L)
such that
h^{-1}u_{0}=h_{1}u_{0}.
Hence the support of such
h
is in
\mathrm{U}(L)\mathrm{U}(V_{0}^{\flat})
. Recall that the volume of
\mathrm{U}(L)
is normalized to be
1
. As
1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L}
is invariant under
\mathrm{U}(L)
,
\Orb((g_{0},u_{0}),1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L})
is equal to

\int_{h\in\mathrm{U}(V_{0}^{\flat})}1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}(h.g_{0})1_{L}(h^{-1}u_{0})dh=\int_{h\in\mathrm{U}(V_{0}^{\flat})}1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}(h.g_{0})1_{L}(u_{0})dh=\Orb(g_{0},1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}).

∎

4.5.
Induction on boundary cases: symmetric spaces

Consider
x=(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in[S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}]_{\mathrm{rs}}(F_{0})
such that
v(u_{2}u_{1})=v(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})=v(L)
. Conjugate
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})
by an element in
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
, we can assume that
u_{1}\in L_{0},u_{2}\in(L_{0}^{\vee})^{*}
. By Lemma
4.8
, we can and do assume
u_{1}=e,\,u_{2}=(u_{2}u_{1})e^{*}.

Proposition 4.13
.

For all
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}},s)=\Orb(\gamma,1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})},s),

where the right hand side is the group version orbital integral for the pair
(L,u_{1})
.

Proof.

Let
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
be any element with
1_{L_{0}}(h^{-1}u_{1})1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}}(u_{2}h)\not=0
. By Lemma
4.11
, there exists
h_{1}\in\mathrm{GL}(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})
such that
h^{-1}u_{1}=h_{1}u_{1},u_{2}h=u_{2}h_{1}.

Hence the support of such
h
is in
\mathrm{GL}(L_{1},L_{2})\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})
. Recall that the volume of
\mathrm{GL}(L_{1},L_{2})
is normalized to be
1
. As
1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}}
is invariant under
\mathrm{GL}(L_{1},L_{2})
and
\eta(\det(\mathrm{GL}(L_{1},L_{2})))=\{1\}
,
\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}},s)
is equal to

\displaystyle\omega(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})}1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}(h.\gamma)1_{L_{0}}(h^{-1}u_{1})1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}}(u_{2}h)\eta(h)h|^{s}dh

\displaystyle=\omega(\gamma)\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})}1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}(h.\gamma)\eta(h)|h|^{s}dh=\Orb(\gamma,1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee})},s).

Here the transfer factor
\omega(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})
for
L
and
\omega(\gamma)
for
(L,u_{1})
agree by Definition (
2.2
) and (
2.8
).
∎

We finish the proof of Lemma
4.6
. Assume that
y=(g_{0},e)
matches
x=(\gamma,e,(u_{2}u_{1})e^{*})
, where
e^{*}
is the dual basis of
e
under the chosen basis of
V
. Then there exists
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V)
such that
h.(g_{0},e)=(\gamma,e,(u_{2}u_{1})e^{*})
. So
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V^{\flat})
and
g
matches
\gamma
.

By induction, the transfer conjecture for
(L,e)
holds, sos

\\
\ \Orb((g_{0},u_{0}),1_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\times 1_{L})=\Orb(g_{0},1_{\mathrm{U}(L)})

=\Orb(\gamma,1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})})=\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),1_{S(L_{0},L^{\vee}_{0})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee*}}).

Lemma
4.6
is proved. Therefore, on two boundary cases (for
L
and
L^{\vee}
), the semi-Lie TC reduces to two lower rank group TCs. Hence
\Phi_{L}
(c.f.
4.7
) satisfies the conditions in Corollary
4.3
. Theorem
2.7
is proved.

Part II
Arithmetic transfer conjectures

Let
p
be an odd prime. Let
F/F_{0}
be an unramified quadratic extension of
p
-adic local fields. Fix a uniformizer
\varpi
of
F_{0}
and an embedding
O_{F}O_{\breve{F}}
. Denote the residue field of
F_{0}
(resp.
\breve{F}
) by
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(resp.
\mathbb{F}
). Let
\sigma=\overline{(-)}\in\Gal(F/F_{0})
be the non-trivial Galois involution.

Let
V
be a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n\geq 1
, and
L
be a vertex lattice in
V
i.e.,
L\subseteq L^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L
. Let
0\leq t\leq n
be the type of
L
. The space
V
is split if and only if
t
is even.

5.
Special cycles and modified derived fixed points

In this section, we study the relative Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}
for the lattice
L
and two kinds of Kudla–Rapoport cycles. We use the dual isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
as the tool of “taking dual” on the geometric side. We introduce the formal Balloon–Ground stratification on the special fiber and use it to resolve the singularity of
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
, which is an arithmetic analog of the Atiyah flop
[
1
]
. We define a (derived) modification of the fixed point locus of an automorphism on
\mathcal{N}
.

For any
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
-scheme
S
,
a basic triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
of dimension
n
and type
t
(and signature
(1,n-1)
) over
S
is the following data:

•

X
is a strict formal
O_{F_{0}}
-module over
S
of relative height
2n
and dimension
n
. Strictness means the induced action of
O_{F_{0}}
on
\Lie X
is via the structure morphism
O_{F_{0}}\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

•

\iota:O_{F}\End(X)
is an action of
O_{F}
on
X
that extends the action of
O_{F_{0}}
. We require that the
Kottwitz signature condition
of signature
(1,n-1)
holds for all
a\in O_{F}
:

(5.1)

\charac(\iota(a)\mid\Lie X)=(T-a)(T-\overline{a})^{n-1}\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[T].

•

\lambda:XX^{\vee}
is a polarization on
X
as strict
O_{F_{0}}
-module, which is
O_{F}/O_{F_{0}}
semi-linear in the sense that the Rosati involution
\Ros_{\lambda}
induces the non-trivial involution
\overline{(-)}\in\Gal({F}/{F_{0}})
on
\iota:O_{F}\End(X)
.

•

We require that the finite flat group scheme
\text{Ker}\,\lambda
over
S
lies in
X[\varpi]
and is of order
q^{2t}
.

Note that here we endow the dual formal
O_{F_{0}}
-module
X^{\vee}
with the
O_{F}
-action
\iota^{\vee}(a):=\iota(\bar{a})^{\vee}
and the dual polarization
\lambda^{\vee}
(such that
\lambda^{\vee}\circ\lambda=[\varpi]
). The dual of a basic triple
(X,\iota,\lambda)
(of dimension
n
and type
t
) is the basic triple
(X^{\vee},\iota^{\vee},\lambda^{\vee})
(of dimension
n
and type
n-t
).

The Kottwitz signature condition gives a decomposition
\Lie X=(\Lie X)_{0}\oplus(\Lie X)_{1}
, where
O_{F}
acts on the rank
1
piece
(\Lie X)_{0}
by structure map, and on the rank
n-1
piece
(\Lie X)_{1}
by
\sigma
-conjugate of the structure map.

An isomorphism
(X_{1},\iota_{1},\lambda_{1})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(X_{2},\iota_{2},\lambda_{2})
between two such triples is an
O_{F}
-linear isomorphism
\varphi\colon X_{1}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X_{2}
such that
\varphi^{*}(\lambda_{2})=\lambda_{1}
. Up to
O_{F}
-linear quasi-isogeny compatible with the polarization, there exists a unique such triple
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
over
\mathbb{F}
. Fix one choice of
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
as the
framing object
.

Definition 5.1
.

The Rapoport–Zink space for
L
is the functor

\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}

sending
S
to the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
, where

•

(X,\iota,\lambda)
is a basic triple of dimension
n
and type
t
over
S
.

•

\rho:X\times_{S}{\overline{S}}\mathbb{X}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S}
an
O_{F}
-linear quasi-isogeny of height
0
over the reduction
\overline{S}:=S\times_{O_{\breve{F}}}\mathbb{F}
such that
\rho^{*}(\lambda_{\mathbb{X},\overline{S}})=\lambda_{\overline{S}}
.

From
[
52
, Thm. 2.16]
, the functor
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}
is representable by a formal scheme locally formally of finite type over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
of relative dimension
n-1
.

Let
\mathbb{E}
be a formal
O_{F_{0}}
-module over
\mathbb{F}
of relative height
2
and dimension
1
. Complete it into a triple
(\mathbb{E},\iota_{\mathbb{E}},\lambda_{\mathbb{E}})
of
signature
(0,1)
, dimension
1
and type
0
over
\mathbb{F}
, and still denote it by
\mathbb{E}
. Changing the
O_{F}
-action on
\mathbb{E}
by Galois involution
\overline{(-)}
, we obtain a framing object
(\mathbb{E},\iota_{\mathbb{E}}\circ\sigma,\lambda_{\mathbb{E}})
for
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[0]}
, which we denote by
\overline{\mathbb{E}}
. The theory of canonical lifting
[
13
]
gives an isomorphism
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[0]}\simeq\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
, where the universal triple
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
over
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[0]}\simeq\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
is the canonical lifting of
\overline{\mathbb{E}}
. Denote by
\mathcal{E}
the basic triple over
O_{\breve{F}}
obtained from the canonical lifting of
\mathbb{E}
.

Let
the space of special quasi-homomorphisms
be the
F
-vector space

(5.2)

\mathbb{V}=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X})

equipped with the hermitian form (
x,\,y\in\mathbb{V}
):

(5.3)

(x,y)_{\mathbb{V}}:=\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}^{-1}\circ y^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ x\in\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{E})\cong F.

The automorphism group of quasi-isogenies
\Aut^{\circ}(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
acts on
\mathbb{V}
naturally. By Dieudonne theory, we have
\Aut^{\circ}(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})\cong\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
. The group
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
acts on
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}
by

(5.4)

g.(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)=(X,\iota,\lambda,g\circ\rho).

From now on, we fix
n
and
t
and write
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}
for simplicity.

Remark 5.2
.

The framing object may be defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}
, and we obtain a descent of
\mathcal{N}
to
\Spf O_{F}
by the same moduli interpretation. We will still denote it by
\mathcal{N}\Spf O_{F}
.

5.1.
The dual isomorphism
\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}

By taking dual, the framing object
(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})
gives a framing object
(\mathbb{X}^{\vee},\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}})
of dimension
n
and type
n-t
, where
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}}:\mathbb{X}^{\vee}\mathbb{X}
is the dual polarization of
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
such that
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}=[\varpi]:\mathbb{X}\mathbb{X}
. And
\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}}:=\iota_{\mathbb{X}}\circ\overline{(-)}
.

Let
\mathbb{V}^{\vee}:=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X}^{\vee})
be the space of special quasi-homomorphisms for
(\mathbb{X}^{\vee},\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}})
. The polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
induces a
F
-linear map
\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}:\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}^{\vee}
sending
x
to
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ x
.

Proposition 5.3
.

The map
\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}:\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}^{\vee}
is a bijection. For any
x,y\in\mathbb{V}
, we have

(5.5)

(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ x,\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ y)_{\mathbb{V}^{\vee}}=-\varpi(x,y)_{\mathbb{V}}.

Proof.

Any polarization is an anti-symmetric isogeny. Unraveling the definitions we obtain the comparison between hermitian forms on
\mathbb{V}
and
\mathbb{V}^{\vee}
.
∎

Let
V^{\vee}
be the hermitian space with same underlying
F
-vector space as
V
, but equipped with the hermitian form
(x,y)_{V^{\vee}}:=-\varpi(x,y)_{V}
. Denote by
\lambda_{V}:V\cong V^{\vee}
the identification of underlying vector spaces. Then
\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee})
is a vertex lattice in
V^{\vee}
of type
n-t
.

The dual Rapoport–Zink space
of
\mathcal{N}
is the Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}^{\vee}:=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee}))}=\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[n-t]}
for the framing object
(\mathbb{X}^{\vee},\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}})
. We have
the dual isomorphism

\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}

(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})(X^{\vee},\iota_{X^{\vee}},\lambda_{X^{\vee}},\rho_{X^{\vee}})

where
\lambda_{X^{\vee}}
is the dual polarization of
\lambda_{X}
, and
\rho_{X^{\vee}}
is the quasi-isogeny
((\rho_{X})^{\vee})^{-1}:X^{\vee}\times_{S}{\overline{S}}\mathbb{X}^{\vee}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S}
. By definition, we have

(\mathcal{N}^{\vee})^{\vee}=\mathcal{N},\quad\lambda_{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}\circ\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}=id_{\mathcal{N}},\quad\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}\circ\lambda_{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}=id_{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}.

5.2.
Kudla–Rapoport cycles

The dual
\overline{\mathbb{E}}^{\vee}
of
\overline{\mathbb{E}}
gives a framing object for
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[1]}
. For
i\in\{0,1\}
, denote by
\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{[i]}
the universal triple over
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[i]}\simeq\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
, and by
\mathcal{E}^{[i]}
the triple over
O_{\breve{F}}
obtained from
\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{[i]}
by twisting the
O_{F}
-action using the Galois involution
\sigma
. So
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{[0]}
.

Definition 5.4
.

For any non-zero
u\in\mathbb{V}
, the Kudla–Rapoport cycle
\mathcal{Z}(u)\mathcal{N}
is the closed formal subscheme of
\mathcal{N}
sending each
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
-scheme
S
to the subset
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{N}(S)
such that

(\rho_{X})^{-1}\circ u:\mathbb{E}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S}X\times_{S}\overline{S}

lifts to a homomorphism from
\mathcal{E}
to
X
over
S
.

Similarly, the Kudla–Rapoport cycle
\mathcal{Y}(u)\mathcal{N}
is the closed formal subscheme of
\mathcal{N}
where

(\rho_{X^{\vee}})^{-1}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ u:\mathbb{E}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S}X^{\vee}\times_{S}\overline{S}

lifts to a homomorphism from
\mathcal{E}
to
X^{\vee}
over
S
.

Proposition 5.5
.

[
6
, Prop. 5.9]

The functors
\mathcal{Z}(u)
and
\mathcal{Y}(u)
are representable by Cartier divisors of
\mathcal{N}
.

Remark 5.6
.

Unlike the case
t=0

[
30
, Prop. 3.5]
, in general
\mathcal{Z}(u)
and
\mathcal{Y}(u)
may not be flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
nor relative Cartier divisors on
\mathcal{N}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

(1)

We have natural inclusions:

(5.6)

\mathcal{Z}(u)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}(u)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}(\varpi u).

(2)

Under the dual isomorphism
\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
, we have

(5.7)

\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{Y}(u))=\mathcal{Z}(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ u),\quad\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{Z}(u))=\mathcal{Y}(\varpi^{-1}(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ u)).

(3)

\mathcal{Z}(u)
is empty unless
v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}})\geq 0
;
\mathcal{Y}(u)
is empty unless
v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}})\geq-1
.

Proof.

We use the moduli interpretation. If
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(S)
, then
\lambda_{X}\circ\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u:\mathbb{E}\rightarrow X^{\vee}\times_{S}\overline{S}
lifts to a homomorphism from
\mathcal{E}
to
X^{\vee}
over
S
. By compatibility of
\lambda_{X}
and
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
under the quasi-isogeny
\rho_{X}
, we have
\lambda_{X}\circ\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u=\rho_{X}^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ u
hence
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{Y}(u)(S)
. Similarly, from
\lambda_{X}^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{X}\circ\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u=[\iota_{X}(\varpi)]\circ\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u
we see that
\mathcal{Y}(u)\subseteq\mathcal{Z}(\varpi u)
. The second property holds by the definition of
\mathcal{Y}(u)
and
\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}
.

For the last property, if
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})\in\mathcal{Z}(u)(S)
, then

\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}^{-1}\circ(\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u)^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{X}\circ(\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u)\in\Hom_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{E}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S},\mathbb{E}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S})\simeq O_{F}

which exactly gives
(u,u)_{\mathbb{V}}\in O_{F}
by the compatibility
\lambda_{X}\circ\rho_{X}^{-1}\circ u=\rho_{X}^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}\circ u
. Using the dual isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
and Proposition
5.3
, the assertion for
\mathcal{Y}(u)
follows by duality.
∎

5.3.
Formal Balloon-Ground stratification

Proposition 5.8
.

The formal scheme
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\Spf O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
is formally smooth if
t=0
or
n
, and is of strictly semi-stable reduction if
0<t<n
. More precisely, the completed local ring of
\mathcal{N}
at any closed point is isomorphic to
either
O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi)

or

O_{\breve{F}}[[t_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1}]]
.

Proof.

This follows from the computation local models as in
[
6
, Prop 3.33]
, which reduces to the
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
case in
[
10
, §4.4.5]
.
∎

Let
S
be any
\mathbb{F}
-scheme. Choose any point
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(S)
. Consider the
covariant
relative Dieudonne crystal
\mathbb{D}(X)
of
X
over
S
, and the cotangent sheaf

\omega_{X}:=(\Lie X)^{\vee}.

We have short exact sequences of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

(5.8)

0\omega_{X^{\vee}}\mathbb{D}(X)(S)\Lie X0,

(5.9)

0\omega_{X}\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)\Lie X^{\vee}0.

We have a natural identification
(\mathbb{D}(X)(S))^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)
via the perfect pairing

(5.10)

(-,-):\mathbb{D}(X)(S)\times\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)\mathcal{O}_{S}.

We obtain an antisymmetric bilinear form

(-,-)_{\lambda}:\mathbb{D}(X)(S)\times\mathbb{D}(X)(S)\mathcal{O}_{S},\,\,(x,y)_{\lambda}:=(x,\mathbb{D}(\lambda)(y)).

The
O_{F}
-action on
X
induces a decomposition

\mathbb{D}(X)=\mathbb{D}(X)_{0}\oplus\mathbb{D}(X)_{1}
such that
O_{F}
acts on
\mathbb{D}(X)_{0}
(resp.
\mathbb{D}(X)_{1}
) by the structure map (resp. the structure map composed with the Galois involution on
O_{F}/O_{F_{0}}
).
And
\mathbb{D}(X)_{i}
(
i=0,1
) is isotropic under the form
(-,-)_{\lambda}
. From the Kottwitz signature condition
(1,n-1)
,
(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{0}
(resp.
(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{1}
) is locally free of rank
n-1
(resp.
1
). The polarizations induce maps between line bundles

\Lie\lambda:(\Lie X)_{0}(\Lie X^{\vee})_{0},\quad\Lie\lambda^{\vee}:(\Lie X^{\vee})_{0}(\Lie X)_{0}.

We introduce
the formal Balloon–Ground stratification
on the special fiber
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{F}}
:

•

The formal Balloon stratum

\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
is the vanishing locus of universal
\Lie\lambda
.

•

The formal Ground stratum

\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
is the vanishing locus of universal
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}
.

•

The formal Linking stratum

\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
is the intersection
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\cap\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
.

As
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}\circ\Lie\lambda=[\varpi]
, we have the stratification
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{F}}=\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
.

Proposition 5.9
.

The formal subschemes
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
are Cartier divisors of
\mathcal{N}
and are formally smooth over
\mathbb{F}
. The structure map
\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\rightarrow O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
is formally smooth. And for any closed point
x
of
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
, the completed local ring of
\mathcal{N}
at
x
is isomorphic to
O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi)
, and the local equation of
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
) may be taken to be
x_{0}=0
(resp.
y_{0}=0
) .

Proof.

By Proposition
5.8
,
\mathcal{N}
is regular and
\varpi
is locally non-zero. As vanishing locus of sections of line bundles,
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
are Weil divisors of
\mathcal{N}
. As
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}\circ\Lie\lambda=[\varpi]
, the local generators of
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
are also locally non-zero, hence
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
are in fact Cartier divisors of
\mathcal{N}
.

We show that
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
are formally smooth over
\mathbb{F}
. For any closed point
x
, we pass to the completed local ring
\hat{O}_{\mathcal{N},x}
of
\mathcal{N}
at
x
. As
\Lie\lambda\circ\Lie\lambda^{\vee}=[\varpi]
, the local generator
x^{\circ}
(resp.
x^{\bullet}
) of
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
) can be chosen such that
x^{\circ}x^{\bullet}=\varpi
inside
\hat{O}_{\mathcal{N},x}
. By the description of completed local rings in Proposition
5.8
, there are two cases: firstly, if
\hat{O}_{\mathcal{N},x}
is isomorphic to
O_{\breve{F}}[[t_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1}]]
which is a UFD, then the equation
x^{\circ}x^{\bullet}=\varpi
implies that either
x^{\circ}
or
x^{\bullet}
is a unit and the other generator agrees with
\varpi
(up to a unit). In particular both of them are formally smooth over
\mathbb{F}
and we are done; second, if
\hat{O}_{\mathcal{N},x}
is isomorphic to
O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi)
, solving the equation
x^{\circ}x^{\bullet}=\varpi
shows that
x^{\circ}
(resp.
x^{\bullet}
) can be arranged as local generators
x_{0}
(resp.
y_{0}
) in
5.8
after applying an automorphism on
\widehat{O}_{\mathcal{N},x}
. We are done as well.

To see the structure map
\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\rightarrow O_{\breve{F}}
is formally smooth, we apply Grothendieck–Messing theory. We only need to deform the Hodge filtration. As
(\omega_{X})_{1}=(\Lie X)^{\vee}_{1}
and
(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{0}=(\Lie X^{\vee})^{\vee}
are orthogonal complements under the perfect pairing
(-,-)
hence determine each other, we only need to deform the quotient lines
(\Lie X)_{0}
and
(\Lie X^{\vee})_{0}
in the Hodge filtration. If
x\in\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
, then one of
(\Lie X)_{0}
and
(\Lie X^{\vee})_{0}
determines the other using the non-zero section
\Lie\lambda
or
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}
, so we only need to deform one quotient line, which forms a unobstructed deformation problem.
∎

Definition 5.10
.

Let
f:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
be the formal blow up of
\mathcal{N}\times_{O_{\breve{F}}}\mathcal{N}
along the Weil divisor
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
.

Theorem 5.11
.

If
0<t<n
, the formal scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
is of semi-stable reduction over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
and is in particular regular. The map
f:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
is an isomorphism away from
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
. The geometric fiber of
f
is either a point or a projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}}
. In particular
f
is a small resolution.

Proof.

By formal smoothness of
\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\rightarrow O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
, we know that
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
is regular away from
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
or
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times\mathcal{N}
. Hence the center
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
is a Cartier divisor of
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
away from
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
, where blow up changes nothing.

Blow up commutes with flat base change in particular open immersions, so we can pass to completed local rings. By Proposition
5.9
, we can choose local generators of
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
) as
x^{0}
(resp.
y^{0}
) in the isomorphism

\widehat{O}_{\mathcal{N},x}\simeq O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi)

for any closed point
x\in\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
. By direct computation, the blow up of

O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},x_{1},y_{1},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1},s_{1},\ldots,s_{n-1}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi,x_{1}y_{1}-\varpi)

along
(x_{0},x_{1})
is of strictly semi-stable reduction over
O_{\breve{F}}
. As the ideal
(x_{0},x_{1})
is generated by two elements, the fiber of
f
at
x\in\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
can be embedded into
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}}
. By local computations, it is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}}
. Hence the resolution
f
has only
\dim\leq 1
fibers and is an isomorphism outside the
\text{codim}\geq 3
locus
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
, hence a small resolution.
∎

The following proposition gives another definition of Balloon–Ground stratification in the spirit of
[
35
, Defn. 5.2.3]
.

Proposition 5.12
.

The closed formal subscheme
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{F}}
is defined by the condition

(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0},(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{1})_{\lambda}=0\in\mathcal{O}_{S}.

Similarly, the space
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{F}}
is defined by the condition

(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1})^{\perp}\subseteq(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{0}.

Here
(-)^{\perp}
means the (right) orthogonal complement under
(-,-)_{\lambda}
inside
\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}
.

Proof.

We always have
((\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{0},(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{1})_{\lambda}=0
, so
(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0},(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{1})_{\lambda}=0
is equivalent to

((\Lie X)_{0},(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{1})_{\lambda}=0
i.e.,
((\Lie X)_{0},(\Lie X^{\vee})_{1})_{\lambda}=0
i.e.,
\Lie\lambda=0
.

On the other hand,
(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1})^{\perp}
is the same as the orthogonal complement of
\Im(\mathbb{D}(\lambda))(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1})\subseteq\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)_{0}
under the perfect pairing
(-,-)
between
\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{0}
and
\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)_{0}
. By perfectness, we see the condition
(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1})^{\perp}\subseteq(\omega_{X^{\vee}})_{0}
is equivalent to the following inclusion inside
\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)_{0}
:

(\Lie X)_{0}^{\vee}=(\omega_{X})_{0}\subseteq\Im(\mathbb{D}(\lambda))(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1}).

As
S
is of characteristic
p
, we have
\Im(\mathbb{D}(\lambda))(\mathbb{D}(X)(S)_{1})=\text{Ker}(\mathbb{D}(\lambda^{\vee}))|_{\mathbb{D}(X^{\vee})(S)_{0}}
by
[
35
, Lem. 3.4.12]
. So the inclusion is the same as the condition that

\mathbb{D}(\lambda^{\vee})((\Lie X)_{0}^{\vee})=0

which is exactly
(\Lie\lambda^{\vee})^{\vee}=0
i.e.,
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}=0
.
∎

Remark 5.13
.

As in Remark
5.2
, the definition of Kudla–Rapoport cycles and formal Balloon–Ground stratification descends to
O_{F}
by the same moduli interpretations.

5.4.
Modifying derived fixed points

We recall some well-known facts about blow-ups.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
Z\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion of formal schemes that are locally formally of finite type over
O_{\breve{F}}
. Consider the blow up morphism
\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}XX
along
Z
. For any closed formal subscheme
Y\rightarrow X
, denote by
\widetilde{Y}
the strict transform of
Y
in
X
. Then

(1)

There is a natural isomorphism
\widetilde{Y}\cong\mathrm{Bl}_{Z\cap_{X}Y}Y
over
Y
. Here
Z\cap_{X}Y
is the fiber product of
Y
and
Z
in
X
.

(2)

Either
\widetilde{Y}
is empty, or
\dim_{\widetilde{y}}\widetilde{Y}=\dim_{y}Y
for any closed point
\widetilde{y}
of
\widetilde{Y}
with image
y\in Y
.

(3)

Blow up commutes with flat base change
X^{\prime}X
of the base.

For a closed formal scheme
Y\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
, write
\widetilde{Y}
its strict transform under the resolution (
5.10
).

Remark 5.15
.

For the embedding
\delta_{(L,e)}:\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}
in §
5.5
, we have
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\cap\mathcal{N}^{\flat}=(\mathcal{N}^{\flat})^{\circ}
. Hence for closed formal subscheme
Y\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
, the strict transform
\widetilde{Y}
is independent of whether the strict transform is taken in
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
or
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
.

Remark 5.16
.

The resolution
f
has a simple model. Consider the quadratic cone
X:=V(x_{0}y_{0}=x_{1}y_{1})\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{4}_{\mathbb{F}}=\Spec{\mathbb{F}[x_{0},y_{0},x_{1},y_{1}]}
which is singular at the origin. The resolution
f
above is similar to the classical Atiyah flop
[
1
]

f_{x_{0}x_{1}}:X_{x_{0}x_{1}}X
obtained by blowing up along
V(x_{0}=x_{1}=0)\subseteq X
. Consider similar resolutions
f_{y_{0}y_{1}},\,f_{x_{0}y_{1}},\,f_{x_{1}y_{0}},\,f_{x_{0}y_{0}x_{1}y_{1}}
of
X
where
f_{x_{0}y_{0}x_{1}y_{1}}
means blowing up of
X
along the origin. Then we have identifications
f_{x_{0}x_{1}}=f_{y_{0}y_{1}}
,
f_{x_{0}y_{1}}=f_{x_{1}y_{0}}
and a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{x_{0}y_{0}x_{1}y_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{x_{0}x_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{x_{0}y_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

whose base change to the origin
\Spec\mathbb{F}X
is isomorphic to projections of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
to two factors
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. The above resolution
f
agrees with the blow up of
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
along products of Ground strata
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
. We do not consider the blow up along
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
as the diagonal
\Delta
doesn’t lift to it via strict transforms.

Proposition 5.17
.

For any automorphism
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
, the strict transform of its graph
\Gamma_{g}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
is an isomorphism
\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}\simeq\Gamma_{g}
.

Proof.

The formal Balloon stratum
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
is stable under
g
by moduli interpretation. So the pullback of
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
to
\Gamma_{g}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}
via projection to the first factor
\mathcal{N}
. As
\Gamma_{g}\cong\mathcal{N}
is regular, the Weil divisor
i_{g}:\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\mathcal{N}
is Cartier hence the blow up changes nothing.
∎

Therefore, we have a natural lifting
\Gamma_{g}=\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
. Take
g=\mathrm{id}
, we get a natural lifting of the diagonal embedding
\Delta:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
:

\widetilde{\Delta}:\mathcal{N}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}.

Definition 5.18
.

For
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{rs}
, the
fixed point locus
of
g
inside
\mathcal{N}
is the formal scheme
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g):=\Gamma_{g}\cap_{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\Delta
. The
modified fixed locus
of
g
is the fibered product

\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g):=\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Delta}

in the category of formal schemes over
O_{\breve{F}}
. The
modified derived fixed locus
of
g
is the derived tensor product

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.06427pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.06427pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}

viewed as an element in the Grothendieck
K
-group
K_{0}^{\prime}(\mathcal{N})_{\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)}
(
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients) of coherent sheaves on
\mathcal{N}
supported on
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)
.

As
\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
is a closed immersion,
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}=\mathcal{N}
is a closed immersion. We have a map
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g){\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
, which is an isomorphism if
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)\cap\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}=\emptyset
.

5.5.
Embeddings

Let
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
be an orthogonal decomposition of vertex lattices where
v_{F}((e,e)_{V})=i\in\{0,1\}
. We have
t(L^{\flat})=t(L)-i
. Choose a decomposition of framing objects:

(5.11)

(\mathbb{X},\iota_{\mathbb{X}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}})=(\mathbb{X}^{\flat},\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\flat}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\flat}})\times(\mathbb{E}^{[i]},\iota_{\mathbb{E}^{[i]}},\lambda_{\mathbb{E}^{[i]}}).

Set
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}:=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L^{\flat})}
and
\mathcal{N}:=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}
with above framing objects. There is a natural embedding:

(5.12)

\displaystyle\delta=\delta_{(L,e)}:\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle(X^{\flat},\iota^{\flat},\lambda^{\flat},\rho^{\flat})\longmapsto(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho):=(X^{\flat},\iota^{\flat},\lambda^{\flat},\rho^{\flat})\times(\mathcal{E}^{[i]},\iota_{\mathcal{E}^{[i]}},\lambda_{\mathcal{E}^{[i]}},\rho_{\mathcal{E}^{[i]}}).

Taking dual, we have decompositions of vertex lattices and framing objects:

\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee})=\lambda_{V^{\flat}}((L^{\flat})^{\vee})\oplus O_{F}\lambda_{V}\frac{e}{(e,e)_{V}},

(\mathbb{X}^{\vee},\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee}})=(\mathbb{X}^{\vee,\flat},\iota_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee,\flat}},\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\vee,\flat}})\times(\mathbb{E}^{[i^{\vee}]},\iota_{\mathbb{E}^{[i^{\vee}]}},\lambda_{\mathbb{E}^{[i^{\vee}]}}).

Here
i^{\vee}:=v((\lambda_{V}\frac{e}{(e,e)_{V}},\lambda_{V}\frac{e}{(e,e)_{V}})_{V^{\vee}})=1-i
.
We have an embedding

\delta_{(L,e)}^{\vee}:=\delta_{(\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee}),\lambda_{V}\frac{e}{(e,e)_{V}})}:\mathcal{N}^{\vee,\flat}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}.

From the moduli interpretation, the embeddings are compatible under dual isomorphisms:

(5.13)

\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}\circ\delta_{(L,e)}=\delta_{(L,e)}^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}.

Define the vector
e^{[i]}\in\mathbb{V}=\Hom^{\circ}_{O_{E}}(\mathbb{E}^{[0]},\mathbb{X})
by

(5.14)

e^{[i]}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}^{[0]}\mathbb{X}^{\flat}\times\mathbb{E}^{[0]}=\mathbb{X},&i=0.\\
\mathbb{E}^{[0]}\overset{\lambda_{\mathbb{E}^{[0]}}}{\cong}\mathbb{E}^{[1]}\mathbb{X}^{\flat}\times\mathbb{E}^{[1]}=\mathbb{X},&i=1.\end{cases}

Proposition 5.19
.

(1)

If
i=0
, then
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}=\mathcal{Z}(e^{[0]})
under the embedding
\delta_{(L,e)}
.

(2)

If
i=1
, then
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}=\mathcal{Y}(\varpi^{-1}e^{[1]})
under the embedding
\delta_{(L,e)}
.

Proof.

If
i=0
, then the valuation of
e^{[0]}
is a unit. Hence for any point
(X,\iota_{X},\lambda_{X},\rho_{X})
of
\mathcal{Z}(e^{[0]})(S)
, the lifting of
\rho^{-1}_{X}\circ e^{[0]}
to
X
gives a splitting of
X
to identify it with a point in
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}(S)
. The first property follows. The second property follows by duality using the dual isomorphism (
5.13
).
∎

6.
Arithmetic transfer conjectures and reductions

6.1.
Semi-Lie version

For any bounded complex
\mathcal{F}
in the
K
-group of coherent sheaves on the regular formal scheme
\mathcal{N}
with proper support, recall the Euler characteristic of
\mathcal{F}
is

\chi(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{F}):=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i}\length_{O_{\breve{F}}}H^{i}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{F}).

Definition 6.1
.

For any regular semisimple pair
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{rs}
, we consider the following
derived intersection numbers

(6.1)

\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u):=\chi(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}(u)}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)).

(6.2)

\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,u):=\chi(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}(u)}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)).

Note that
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)
has bounded degrees by the regularity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
. By Proposition
6.2
below,
\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u)
and
\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,u)
are well-defined and finite.

Proposition 6.2
.

(1)

The intersections
\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}
and
\mathcal{Y}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}
are
proper scheme
over
O_{\breve{F}}
.

(2)

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}(u)}\cap\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}
are both proper schemes over

O_{\breve{F}}
.

(3)

\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}(u)}\cap\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}
are both proper schemes over

O_{\breve{F}}
.

Proof.

We prove the first property firstly. Using the dual isomorphism, it is sufficient to prove that
\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}
is a proper scheme. Note
\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}
is a closed subscheme of the Kudla–Rapoport cycle
\mathcal{Z}(L_{g,u})
where
L_{g,u}
is the lattice spanned by
\{u,gu,\ldots,g^{n-1}u\}
. We only need to show
\mathcal{Z}(L_{g,u})
is a proper scheme. By regular semi-simpleness,
L_{g,u}
is a full lattice in
\mathbb{V}
. We follow the argument of Li–Zhang
[
37
, Lemma 2.10.1]
via vertical and horizontal parts. The horizontal part of
\mathcal{Z}(L_{g,u})
is empty: otherwise we may find a
O_{K}
-point
\mathcal{X}
of
\mathcal{Z}(L_{g,u})
for some extension
K
of
\breve{F}
, and get an isogeny
\mathcal{E}^{n}\mathcal{X}
induced by
\{u,gu,\ldots,g^{n-1}u\}
, contradict to the assumption that
\mathcal{X}
has Kottwitz signature
(1,n-1)
. The vertical part of
\mathcal{Z}(L_{g,u})
is a scheme by the argument in
[
37
, Lemma 5.1.1]
, and is contained in finitely many (proper) irreducible components of
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
using the Bruhat–Tits stratification in our set up (see §
7
) hence proper.

Now the first property holds. As the blow up morphism is proper, the natural map
f:\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}(u)}\cap\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}
is proper with image being a proper scheme. Hence
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}(u)}\cap\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}
is also proper scheme, so is its closed formal subscheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}(u)\cap\Gamma_{g}}
. The second property holds, and the third property follows by duality.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

Finiteness of intersection numbers for regular semi-simple orbits appear in other contexts, see e.g.
[
41
, §9.2]
,
[
66
, Lemma 2.8]
via basic uniformization.

Recall that in §
2
we have set up orbital integrals on the analytic side. Choose an orthogonal basis of
L
to endow
L
(resp.
V
) with an
O_{F_{0}}
(resp.
F_{0}
)-structure
L_{0}
(resp.
V_{0}
). Consider the symmetric space over
F_{0}

S(V_{0})=\{\gamma\in\mathrm{GL}(V)|\gamma\overline{\gamma}=id\,\}.

The general linear group
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
acts on
S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*}
by

h.(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})=(h^{-1}\gamma h,h^{-1}u_{1},u_{2}h).

For
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0}))
, recall the derived orbital integral (
2.5
).

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f^{\prime}):=\omega_{L}(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})}f^{\prime}(h.(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}))\eta(h)|h|^{s}dh

We consider the following test functions on
(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})
:

f_{\text{std}}:=1_{S(L,L^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{(L^{\vee}_{0})^{*}},\quad f_{\text{std}}^{\prime}=1_{S(L,L^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}^{\vee}}\times 1_{(L_{0})^{*}}.

Now we introduce the explicit arithmetic transfer conjectures at maximal parahoric levels.

Conjecture 6.4

(Semi-Lie ATC for
L
)
.

For any regular semisimple pair
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matching
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have equalities in
\mathbb{Q}\log q
:

(1)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f_{\text{std}})=-\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u)\log q.

(2)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f_{\text{std}}^{\prime})=-(-1)^{t}\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,u)\log q.

Proposition 6.5
.

The part
(2)
of Conjecture
6.4
for
L\subseteq V
is equivalent to part
(1)
of Conjecture
6.4
for the dual vertex lattice
\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee})\subseteq V^{\vee}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.7
, we have
\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathcal{N}}(g,u)=\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathcal{N}^{\vee}}(g,\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ u)
. On the analytic side, we have

f_{\text{std},L}^{\prime}(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})=f_{\text{std},\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee})}(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}).

We are done by noting the transfer factor
\omega_{L}
and
\omega_{\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee})}
for
L
and
\lambda_{V}(L^{\vee})
differ by the multiplication of the Weil constant
\gamma_{V}=(-1)^{t}
.
∎

6.2.
Group version

We have group version arithmetic transform conjectures for any orthogonal decomposition of vertex lattices
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
where
v((e,e)_{V})=i\in\{0,1\}
.

Definition 6.6
.

With respect to the embedding
\delta_{(L,e)}:\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\mathcal{N}
(
5.12
), we consider the
derived intersection number
of
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
as the following Euler characteristic number

(6.3)

\widetilde{\Int}(g):=\chi(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g)).

By Proposition
5.19
, we have

(6.4)

\widetilde{\Int}(g)=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,e^{[0]}),&\text{if $i=0$}.\\
\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,\varpi^{-1}e^{[1]}),&\text{if $i=1$}.\\
\end{cases}

The GGP type embedding
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}
lifts to an embedding of formal schemes

\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}

via strict transforms .

Proposition 6.7
.

The natural commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}

is cartesian. Moreover, the commutative diagram for the embedding
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
over
{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
is also cartesian.

Proof.

Denote the fiber product by
X
, then we have a closed immersion
i:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}X
over
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
. It is sufficient to show
i
is an isomorphism when localizing at every closed point
P\in\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
. The blow up is an isomorphism outside
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
, we can assume
P\in\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
. The formulation of Balloon–Ground strata is compatible with the embedding
\delta_{(L,e)}
. By Proposition
5.9
and Theorem
5.11
, we can choose local generators such that the embedding

\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
at
P
is isomorphic to the standard embedding from

O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},x_{1},y_{1},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-1},s_{1},\ldots,s_{n-1}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi,x_{1}y_{1}-\varpi)

\twoheadrightarrow O_{\breve{F}}[[x_{0},y_{0},x_{1},y_{1},t_{1},\ldots,t_{n-2},s_{1},\ldots,s_{n-2}]]/(x_{0}y_{0}-\varpi,x_{1}y_{1}-\varpi)

where
x_{0}
(resp.
y_{0}
) is a local generator for
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
). Then the compatibility follows from direct computations. The claim for the embedding
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
follows similarly.
∎

The formal scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}
is regular by compatibility of blow ups in Proposition
6.7
.

Proposition 6.8
.

For
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have

\widetilde{\Int}(g)=\chi(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}_{(1\times g)\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}).

Proof.

By definition,
\widetilde{\Int}(g)
is the derived intersection number between
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
and
(1\times g)\mathcal{N}
inside the resolution
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
. By projection formula, we only need to show the (derived) pullback of
(1\times g)\mathcal{N}
along the embedding
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
agrees with
(1\times g)\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
. This is true before taking blow ups, and we are done by the compatibility of blow ups in Proposition
6.7
.
∎

Consider the conjugacy action of
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})
on
S(V_{0})
. For
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(S(V_{0})(F_{0}))
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, recall the derived orbital integral

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma^{\prime},f^{\prime})=\omega_{L}(\gamma^{\prime})\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0}^{\flat})}f^{\prime}(h^{-1}\gamma^{\prime}h)\eta(\det(h))|\det h|^{s}dh

where
\omega_{L}(\gamma^{\prime}):=\eta(\det(\gamma^{\prime i}e)_{i=0}^{n-1})\in\{\pm 1\}
is the transfer factor for the basis of
L
(
2.8
).

Conjecture 6.9
.

(Group ATC for
(L,e)
)
For regular semisimple element
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{rs}
matching
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{rs}
, we have an equality in
\mathbb{Q}\log q

(6.5)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma^{\prime},1_{S(L,L^{\vee})})=-\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})\log q.

The group version arithmetic transfer conjecture includes the case
[
50
, Section 10]
as a special case i.e.,
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
where
L^{\flat}
is self dual and
(e,e)_{V}=\varpi
.

6.3.
Reduction of intersection numbers

Consider any orthogonal decomposition of vertex lattices
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
where
v_{F}((e,e)_{V})=i\in\{0,1\}
. In this subsection, we show the reduction from Conjecture
6.9
for
(L,e)
to part
(i+1)
of Conjecture
6.4
for
L^{\flat}
.

Assume that
i=0
firstly. Consider the map
\ast:\Hom_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X}^{\flat})\Hom_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{X}^{\flat},\mathbb{E})
defined by
f\lambda_{\mathbb{E}}^{-1}\circ f^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{\mathbb{X}^{\flat}}
. It induces an isomorphism of
F
-vector spaces

(6.6)

\ast:\mathbb{V}^{\flat}=\Hom^{\circ}_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X}^{\flat})\simeq(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})^{*}=\Hom^{\circ}_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{X}^{\flat},\mathbb{E}).

Write any element
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
in the matrix form

\textstyle{\mathbb{X}^{\flat}\times\mathbb{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}=\left(\begin{matrix}a&u\\
w&d\end{matrix}\right)}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{X}^{\flat}\times\mathbb{E}}

where

a\in\End^{\circ}_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{X}^{\flat})=\End(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})
,
u\in\mathbb{V}^{\flat}
,
w\in(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})^{*}
and
d\in\End^{\circ}_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{E})=F
.

For the decomposition
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}^{\flat}\oplus Fe^{[i]}
, consider its unitary relative Cayley map (
3.2
):

(6.9)

\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}:\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})\times\mathbb{V}^{\flat},

(6.10)

g^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&u\\
w&d\end{pmatrix}(g=a+\frac{uw}{1-d},\,u_{1}=\frac{u}{1-d}).

For
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{rs}
with
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime})=(g,u_{1})
, consider the graph
\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}
(resp.
\Gamma_{g}
) of
g^{\prime}
(resp.
g
) inside
\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
).

Lemma 6.10
.

If
i=0
and
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, then we have

(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u_{1}))\cap_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\Gamma_{g}=(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat})\cap_{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}.

Proof.

As
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, we have
\mathcal{Z}(u)=\mathcal{Z}(u_{1})
. Set
\epsilon_{d}:=\frac{1-\overline{d}}{(1-d)(e,e)}\in F^{\times}
which is an unit by our assumption. By Proposition
3.2
, for the element
w^{\vee}:=g^{-1}\epsilon_{d}u
we have

(w^{\vee})^{*}=w\in(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})^{*}.

For any test scheme
S
, consider a
S
-point
(X_{1},X_{2})
of
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
. On the one hand, if
(X_{1},X_{2})
is on the graph
\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}
via the embedding
\delta:\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\mathcal{N}
, then we have a homomorphism
\varphi^{\prime}:X_{1}\times\mathcal{E}X_{2}\times\mathcal{E}
lifting
g^{\prime}
. Write
\varphi^{\prime}
in the matrix form

\textstyle{X_{1}\times\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}=\left(\begin{matrix}\varphi&\psi_{u}\\
\psi_{w}&d\end{matrix}\right)}
\textstyle{X_{2}\times\mathcal{E}}

lifting the diagram (
6.3
) (after applying framings of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
). Here
\varphi:X_{1}\rightarrow X_{2}
,
\psi_{u}:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow X_{2}
,
\psi_{w}:X_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
are all homomorphisms, in particular
X_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}(u)
. As
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
, the quasi-isogeny

\widetilde{\varphi}=\varphi+(1-d)^{-1}\psi_{u}\circ\psi_{w}

is a homomorphism lifting
g
(
6.10
). Hence
(X_{1},X_{2})
is on the intersection
(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))\cap_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\Gamma_{g}
.

On the other hand, we start with homomorphisms
\psi_{u}:\mathcal{E}X_{2}
(resp.
\varphi_{g}:X_{1}X_{2}
) lifting
u
(resp.
g
). By the formula
w^{\vee}=g^{-1}\epsilon_{d}u
, the homomorphism (
\epsilon_{d}
is a unit)

\psi_{w^{\vee}}:=[\epsilon_{d}]\circ\varphi^{-1}_{g}\circ\psi_{u}:\mathcal{E}X_{2}

lifting the vector
w^{\vee}\in\mathbb{V}^{\flat}
. As
i=0
, the polarization
\lambda_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
is an isomorphism. Then the homomorphism
\psi_{w}:=\lambda_{\mathcal{E}}^{-1}\circ(\psi_{w^{\vee}})^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{X}

gives the desired lifting of
w
. The homomorphism
\varphi:=\varphi_{g}-(1-d)^{-1}\psi_{u}\circ\psi_{w}
lifts
a
, hence we get the desired lifting of
g^{\prime}
. Therefore,
(X_{1},X_{2})
is on the intersection
(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat})\cap_{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}
.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

This generalizes the reduction lemma
[
67
, Lem. 4.4]
to maximal parahoric levels.

Proposition 6.12
.

We have an identification of two fiber products as closed formal subschemes of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}
:

(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat})\cap_{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}=(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}.

Proof.

The strict transform of
\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}
equals to itself by Proposition
5.17
. Then
T=(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}
is the fiber product of the diagram:

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\ \widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}=\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}

The bottom square is cartesian by Proposition
6.7
. So
T=(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat})\cap_{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}
.
∎

Consider the small resolution
f^{\flat}:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
introduced before for
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
.

Proposition 6.13
.

We have an identification of two fiber products as closed formal subschemes of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}
:

(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u_{1}))\cap_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\Gamma_{g}=(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u_{1}))\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}},

Proof.

By Proposition
5.17
, the graph
\Gamma_{g}=\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
factors through
f^{\flat}
. The equality follows by base change.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

Assume that
u\in\mathbb{V}^{\flat}
is non-zero, then we have equalities in the
K
-group of
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}

(6.11)

(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}=(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\cap^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}},

(6.12)

(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}=(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))\cap^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.5
,
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is a Cartier divisor of
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
.
By Lemma
6.10
, Proposition
6.12
and Proposition
6.13
, the intersection
(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}=(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat})\cap_{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}
has expected dimension
\dim\mathcal{Z}(u)=n-2
, and
(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))\cap_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}
has expected dimension
\dim\mathcal{Z}(u)=n-2
.

As
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
are regular, we conclude by applying
[
67
, Lem. B.2]
. The assumptions in
loc. cit.
are satisfied:
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u)
is a Cartier divisor of
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
, so the pullback
(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))
is a Cartier divisor in the regular formal scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}
. Hence
(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))
is Cohen-Macaulay and of pure dimension
2n-4
.
∎

Now we finish the reduction of group version ATCs to semi-Lie version ATCs.

Theorem 6.15
.

Consider any regular semi-simple element
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{rs}
. Let
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{U}}(g^{\prime})=(g,u_{1})\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}^{\flat})\times\mathbb{V}^{\flat})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
.

(1)

If
i=0
, then we have

\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})=\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u_{1}).

(2)

If
i=1
, then we have

\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})=\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Y}}(g,u_{1}).

(3)

Conjecture
6.9
for
(L,e)
is implied by part
(i+1)
of Conjecture
6.4
for
L^{\flat}
.

Proof.

Assume that
i=0
firstly. Twisting
g^{\prime}
by elements in
F^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}
, we can assume
1-d\in O_{F}^{\times}
. Then
\mathcal{Z}(u_{1})=\mathcal{Z}(u)
. To simplify the notations, we write the space to mean its coherent structure sheaves in the K-groups. By Proposition
6.14
, we have

(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}=(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}}.

Apply
-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}
to both sides, as elements in
K_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)\cap\mathcal{Z}(u))
we obtain

\displaystyle(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g^{\prime}),\mathcal{N}^{\flat})_{\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}((f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{\Gamma_{g}})

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u)))\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g).

We have

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))=\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\otimes_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))=\mathcal{Z}(u)

which is of the expected dimension
n-1+2n-4-(2n-3)=n-2
. By
[
67
, Lem. B.2]
, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}}(f^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\times\mathcal{Z}(u))=\mathcal{Z}(u).

Therefore,
\widetilde{\Int}(g^{\prime})=(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g^{\prime}),\mathcal{N}^{\flat})_{\mathcal{N}}=(\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(g),\mathcal{Z}(u))_{\mathcal{N}^{\flat}}=\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u)
.

If
i=1
, we apply the dual isomorphism
\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
to reduce to the case
i=0
using the compatibility (
5.13
).

For the last part, consider matching elements
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in S(V_{0})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
. Apply relative Cayley maps to
g^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime}
. By local constancy of intersection numbers
[
42
]
, we can always assume
u
is non-zero by approximation. We have the reduction on the analytic side by Corollary
3.22
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.16
.

Although
[
42
]
only lists as examples the unitary Rapoport–Zink spaces in the self-dual case, the argument in
loc. cit.
on local constancy of intersection numbers is quite general and applies to our current situation.

7.
Bruhat–Tits strata

In this section, we study Bruhat–Tits stratification on the reduced locus
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
based on
[
6
,
62
,
63
]
, which is useful for explicit computations. We redefine Bruhat–Tits strata via the Kudla–Rapoport cycles, which is used later to prove AT conjectures in the unramified maximal order case and some local modularity results.

Our group is not quasi–split in general, and the geometry of the reduced locus
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
is more involved in particular
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
may be not equidimensional. See
[
21
]
for some general results on top dimensional irreducible components and their stabilizers (which are special parahorics) of the reduced locus of Rapoport-Zink spaces for quasi–split groups.

For a vertex lattice
L\subseteq V
of rank
n
and type
t
, consider the associated
Rapoport–Zink space

\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
. Recall we introduce the nearby hermitian space
\mathbb{V}=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X})
.

Definition 7.1
.

For a vertex lattices
L^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{V}
, the closed
Bruhat-Tits stratum

\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})
is the closed subscheme of
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
over
\mathbb{F}
defined by

(1)

if
L^{\prime}=L^{\bullet}
has type
\geq t+1
, then
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime}):=\mathcal{Z}(L^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{red}}
.

(2)

If
L^{\prime}=L^{\circ}
has type
\leq t-1
, then
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime}):=\mathcal{Y}((L^{\circ})^{\vee})^{\mathrm{red}}
.

Proposition 7.2
.

Consider an orthogonal decomposition
L=L^{\flat}\oplus O_{F}e
with
v((e,e)_{V})=i\in\{0,1\}
, with induced decomposition
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}^{\flat}\oplus Fe^{[i]}
and the embedding
\delta_{(L,e)}:\mathcal{N}^{\flat}\mathcal{N}
. If
L^{\prime}
is a vertex lattice in
\mathbb{V}
containing
e^{[i]}
, then

(7.1)

\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{N}^{\flat}=\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime\flat}).

Proof.

We may assume
i=0
using the dual isomorphism. Identify
\mathcal{N}^{\flat}
with
\mathcal{Z}(e^{[0]})
. The result follows from moduli definition of
\mathcal{Z}(L^{\prime})
or
\mathcal{Y}(L^{\prime\vee})
.
∎

7.1.
Relative Dieudonne theory

Recall
\sigma
is the
F_{0}
-linear Frobenius automorphism on
\breve{F}_{0}
. We have
O_{F}\otimes_{O_{F_{0}}}O_{\breve{F}_{0}}\simeq O_{\breve{F}_{0}}\times O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
where
O_{F}
acts on the first (resp. second) factor
O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
by the fixed embedding
i_{0}:O_{F}\hookrightarrow O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
(resp.
\sigma\circ i_{0}
).

Let
\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{E})
(resp.
\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{X})
) be the (covariant)
relative Dieudonne module
of the framing object
\mathbb{E}
(resp.
\mathbb{X}
), which is a free
O_{\breve{F}_{0}}
-module of rank
2
(resp. rank
2n
) with the relative Frobenius morphism
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{M}}
) and the relative Verschiebung
\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{E}}
(resp.
\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{M}}
).

Remark 7.3
.

See
[
52
, Prop 3.56, Defn. 3.57]
for backgrounds on relative Dieudonne theory. Let
f
be the inertial degree of
F_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The absolute Dieudonne module of
\mathbb{X}
is a free
W(\mathbb{F})
module of rank
2n[F_{0}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]
, which decomposes into direct summands under the action of
O_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}W(\mathbb{F})\cong\prod_{i=1}^{f}O_{\breve{F}}
. The
0
-th summand has
O_{\breve{F}}
-rank
\frac{2n[F_{0}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]}{f}\times\frac{1}{\text{rk}_{W(\mathbb{F})}O_{\breve{F}}}=2n
and is exactly the relative Dieudonne module
\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{X})
of
\mathbb{X}
.

Denote by
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})=\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{X})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
the associated isocrystal. The
O_{F}
-action on
\mathbb{X}
induces
\mathbb{Z}/2
-gradings:

(7.2)

\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{X})=\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{X})_{0}\oplus\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{X})_{1},\quad\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})=\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}\oplus\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{1}.

The polarization
\lambda_{\mathbb{X}}
induces a nondegenerate
\breve{F}
-bilinear alternating form
\left<-,-\right>_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})
. For
x,y\in\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})
and
a\in O_{F}
, we have

(7.3)

\left<\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{X}}x,y\right>_{\mathbb{X}}=\sigma(\left<x,\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{X}}y\right>_{\mathbb{X}}),\quad\left<\iota(a)x,y\right>_{\mathbb{X}}=\left<x,\iota(\overline{a})y\right>_{\mathbb{X}}.

Definition 7.4
.

Let
\{-,-\}
be the nondegenerate form on
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
given by

\{x,y\}:=\left<x,\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{X}}y\right>_{\mathbb{X}}

which is linear in the first variable and
\sigma
-linear in the second variable. The restriction of the form
\{-,-\}|_{C(\mathbb{X})}
to
C(\mathbb{X})
is skew-hermitian.

For any lattice
\Lambda_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{1}
, write
(\Lambda_{1})^{\perp}\subseteq\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
as its dual inside
\mathbb{N}(X)_{0}
under the pairing
\left<-,-\right>
. We use
(-)^{\perp}
to distinguish it with the dual under the natural form
\{-,-\}
on
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
.

Consider the
\sigma^{2}
-linear operator
\tau=\mathcal{V}^{-1}_{\mathbb{X}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{X}}
on
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
. Let
C(\mathbb{X})=\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}^{\tau=1}
be the
F
-vector space of invariant elements in
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
.

By the (relative) supersingular assumption on
\mathbb{X}
, we have

(7.4)

\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}\simeq C(\mathbb{X})\otimes_{F}\breve{F}.

We have similar decompositions and forms on the isocrystal
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{E})=\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{E})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Choose a unit
\delta\in O_{F}^{\times}
such that
\bar{\delta}=-\delta
. By
[
30
, Remark. 2.5]
(the case of signature
(0,1)
), we can find
\tau
-invariant generators
1_{i}
of
\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{E})_{i}\,(i=0,1)
such that

(7.5)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}1_{1}=1_{0},\quad\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}1_{0}=\varpi 1_{1},\quad\left\{1_{0},1_{0}\right\}_{\mathbb{E}}=\varpi\delta.

A vector
x\in\mathbb{V}=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X})
induces a
F\otimes_{F_{0}}\breve{F}
-linear map from
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{E})
to
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})
. As
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}(1_{0})=\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{E}}(1_{0})
, we have
x(1_{0})\in C(\mathbb{X})
. From
[
30
, Lem. 3.9]
we obtain:

Proposition 7.5
.

The association
xx(1_{0})
gives a bijection

\mathbb{V}\cong C(\mathbb{X})

under which the hermitian form
(-,-)_{\mathbb{V}}
on
\mathbb{V}
is identified with
\frac{1}{\varpi\delta}\{-,-\}
on
C(\mathbb{X})
.

Consider the following non-degenerate
\sigma
-sesquilinear form on
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
and
C(\mathbb{X})
:

(7.6)

(x,y)_{\mathbb{N}}:=\frac{1}{\varpi\delta}\{x,y\}.

For
x,y\in\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
, we have

\sigma((x,y)_{\mathbb{N}})=(\tau(y),x)_{\mathbb{N}},\quad(\tau(x),\tau(y))_{\mathbb{N}}=\sigma^{2}((x,y)_{\mathbb{N}}).

7.2.
\mathbb{F}
-points of
\mathcal{N}

For any
O_{\breve{F}}
lattice
A
in
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
, denote by
A^{\vee}
its dual under
(-,-)_{\mathbb{N}}
:

(7.7)

A^{\vee}=\{x\in\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}|(x,A)_{\mathbb{N}}\subseteq O_{\breve{F}}\}.

And denote by
(-)^{\perp}
the dual with respect to
\left<-,-\right>_{\mathbb{X}}
. For two
O_{\breve{F}}
lattices
A_{1},A_{2}
in
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
and
m\geq 0
, write
A_{1}\overset{m}{\subseteq}A_{2}
if
A_{1}\subseteq A_{2}
and
A_{2}/A_{1}
is a
\mathbb{F}
-vector space of dimension
m
. Note we have

(7.8)

(A^{\vee})^{\vee}=\tau(A).

We regard
O_{F}
lattices in
\mathbb{V}\simeq C(\mathbb{X})
the same as
\tau
-stable
O_{\breve{F}}
lattices in
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
. For any
O_{F}
lattice
L^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{V}\simeq C(\mathbb{X})
, we denote by
L^{\prime\vee}
its dual under the form
(-,-)_{\mathbb{V}}\simeq(-,-)_{\mathbb{N}}
. We have
(L^{\prime\vee})^{\vee}=L^{\prime}
.

Consider a point
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})
. The framing gives an isomorphism
\mathbb{N}(X)\simeq\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})
. Hence the relative Dieudonne module of
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)
gives an
O_{\breve{F}}
-lattice
M=M_{0}\oplus M_{1}
inside
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})
. Consider two
O_{\breve{F}}
-lattices inside
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
given by

(7.9)

A=M_{0},\quad B=M_{1}^{\perp}.

By definition, we have
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{X}}M_{1}=((M_{1})^{\perp})^{\vee}
. A pair of
O_{\breve{F}}
-lattices
(A,B)
in
\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}
is call
special
if we have

B^{\vee}\overset{1}{\subseteq}A,\quad A^{\vee}\overset{1}{\subseteq}B,\quad A\overset{t}{\subseteq}B.

Proposition 7.6
.

We have the following description of
\mathbb{F}
-points of
\mathcal{N}
:

(7.10)

\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})=\{\text{special pairs of $O_{\breve{F}}$-lattices \,}(A,B)\subseteq\mathbb{N}(\mathbb{X})_{0}\}.

Proof.

This follows from
[
62
, Lem. 1.5]

[
6
, Prop. 2.4]
. The condition on the polarization is equivalent to
A\overset{t}{\subseteq}B
, and the Kottwitz signature condition is equivalent to
B^{\vee}\overset{1}{\subseteq}A,\,A^{\vee}\overset{1}{\subseteq}B
. Hence we conclude by relative Dieudonne theory.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

By Proposition
5.3
, we have
(-,-)_{\mathbb{V}^{\vee}}=-\varpi(-,-)_{\mathbb{V}}
under the dual isomorphism
