\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}:\mathbb{V}\cong\mathbb{V}^{\vee}
. On
\mathbb{F}
-points, the dual isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
(
5.1
) is given by

\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})\mathcal{N}^{\vee}(\mathbb{F}),\,\,(A,B)(A^{\prime},B^{\prime}):=(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}(B),\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}(\varpi^{-1}A)).

Given a special pair
(A,B)
, denote by
L_{A}
(resp.
L_{B}
) the smallest
\tau
-stable lattice containing
A
(resp.
B
). By definition,

(7.11)

A\subseteq L_{A},\quad L_{A}^{\vee}\subseteq A^{\vee},

(7.12)

L_{B}^{\vee}\subseteq B^{\vee},\quad B\subseteq L_{B}.

Proposition 7.8
.

[
6
, Lem. 2.7]

For a special pair
(A,B)
, at least one of the two following cases holds:

•

\varpi L_{A}^{\vee}\subseteq L_{A}\subseteq L_{A}^{\vee}
, in particular
A\overset{t-1}{\subseteq}A^{\vee}
. Hence
L^{\circ}=L_{A}
is a vertex lattice of type
\leq\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(A^{\vee}/A)=t-1
.

•

\varpi L_{B}\subseteq L_{B}^{\vee}\subseteq L_{B}
, in particular
B^{\vee}\overset{t+1}{\subseteq}B
. Hence
L^{\bullet}=L_{B}^{\vee}
is a vertex lattice of type
\geq\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(B/B^{\vee})=t+1
.

Remark 7.9
.

For the Balloon–Ground stratification,
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red},\circ}(\mathbb{F})\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{F})
is the locus
A\subseteq A^{\vee}
(
A^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}A
is automatic). And
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red},\bullet}(\mathbb{F})
is the locus
B\subseteq\varpi^{-1}B^{\vee}
(
B^{\vee}\subseteq B
is automatic). Any automorphism
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})
preserves
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red},\bullet}(\mathbb{F})
and
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red},\circ}(\mathbb{F})
. By Proposition
7.8
, we have a stratification

\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}(\mathbb{F})=\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red},\bullet}(\mathbb{F})\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red},\circ}(\mathbb{F}).

7.3.
KR cycles and BT strata

Consider a non-zero vector
u\in\mathbb{V}
and a point
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})
. By Grothendieck–Messing theory,
\rho^{-1}\circ u
lifts to
\Hom_{O_{F}}(\mathbb{E},X)
if and only if

(7.13)

u(1_{0})\in M_{0},\quad u(1_{1})\in M_{1}.

We have
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}1_{1}=1_{0},\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}1_{0}=\varpi 1_{1}
by (
7.5
). Hence the condition (
7.13
) is equivalent to that
u(1_{0})\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{X}}M_{1}=B^{\vee}
, and we have the following description of
\mathcal{Z}(u)(\mathbb{F})
(
[
6
, Prop. 5.5.]
):

(7.14)

\mathcal{Z}(u)(\mathbb{F})=\{(A,B)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})|u\in B^{\vee}\}.

The relative Dieudonne module of
X^{\vee}
can be identified with
M^{\perp}=B\oplus A^{\perp}
. We obtain similar description for
\mathcal{Y}(u)
:

(7.15)

\mathcal{Y}(u)(\mathbb{F})=\{(A,B)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})|u\in A^{\vee}\}.

The inclusion
\mathcal{Z}(u)\subseteq\mathcal{Y}(u)\subseteq\mathcal{Z}(\varpi^{-1}u)
on
\mathbb{F}
-points corresponds to the inclusion
B^{\vee}\subseteq A^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}B^{\vee}
.

For a vertex lattice
L^{\bullet}\subseteq\mathbb{V}
of type
\geq t+1
, consider the
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}
-vector space

\mathbb{V}(L^{\bullet})_{0}:=(L^{\bullet})^{\vee}/L^{\bullet}
of dimension
t(L^{\bullet})
. Here we regard
L^{\bullet}
as a finite rank
O_{F}
-lattice. Equip
\mathbb{V}(L^{\bullet})_{0}
with the
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}
-valued perfect
\sigma
-hermitian form
(x,y):=\overline{\varpi(x,y)_{\mathbb{V}}}
. From above we have
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\bullet})(\mathbb{F})=\mathcal{Z}(L^{\bullet})(\mathbb{F})=

\{(A,B)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})|L^{\bullet}\subseteq B^{\vee}\subseteq A\subseteq B\subseteq(L^{\bullet})^{\vee},L^{\bullet}\subseteq B^{\vee}\subseteq A^{\vee}\subseteq B\subseteq(L^{\bullet})^{\vee}\}.

Set
U_{1}
(resp.
U_{2}
) as the reduction of
B^{\vee}
(resp.
A
) inside
\mathbb{V}(L^{\bullet})_{0}\otimes\mathbb{F}
. Then
\mathcal{Z}(L^{\bullet})(\mathbb{F})
can be identified with

(7.16)

\{(U_{1},U_{2})\subseteq\mathbb{V}(L^{\bullet})_{0}\otimes\mathbb{F}|U_{1}\subseteq U_{2},U_{2}\subseteq U_{1}^{\perp},U_{1}\subseteq U_{2}^{\perp},\dim U_{1}=\frac{t(L^{\bullet})-t-1}{2},\dim U_{2}=\dim U_{1}+1\}.

Here by definition
U^{\perp}
is the subspace
\{x\in\mathbb{V}(L^{\bullet})_{0}\otimes\mathbb{F}|(x,y)=0,\,\forall y\in U\}.

For a vertex lattice
L^{\circ}\subseteq\mathbb{V}
of type
\leq t-1
, consider the
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}
-vector space

\mathbb{V}(L^{\circ})_{0}:=L^{\circ}/\varpi(L^{\circ})^{\vee}
of dimension
n-t(L^{\circ})
. Here we regard
L^{\circ}
as a finite rank
O_{F}
-lattice. Equip
\mathbb{V}(L^{\circ})_{0}
with the perfect
\sigma
-hermitian form
(x,y):=\overline{(x,y)_{\mathbb{V}}}
. Similarly we have
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\circ})=\mathcal{Y}((L^{\circ})^{\vee})(\mathbb{F})=

\{(A,B)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})|\varpi(L^{\circ})^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi A^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi B\subseteq A\subseteq L^{\circ},\varpi(L^{\circ})^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi A^{\vee}\subseteq B^{\vee}\subseteq A\subseteq L^{\circ}\}

Set
U_{3}
(resp.
U_{4}
) as the reduction of
\varpi A^{\vee}
(resp.
\varpi B
) inside
\mathbb{V}(L^{\circ})_{0}\otimes\mathbb{F}
. Then
\mathcal{Y}((L^{\circ})^{\vee})(\mathbb{F})
can be identified with the set

(7.17)

\{(U_{3},U_{4})\subseteq\mathbb{V}(L^{\circ})_{0}\otimes\mathbb{F}|U_{3}\subseteq U_{4},U_{3}\subseteq U_{4}^{\perp},U_{4}\subseteq U_{3}^{\perp},\dim U_{3}=\frac{t-1-t(L^{\circ})}{2},\dim U_{4}=\dim U_{3}+1\}.

Here
U^{\perp}
is by definition the subspace
\{x\in\mathbb{V}(L^{\circ})_{0}\otimes\mathbb{F}|(x,y)=0,\,\forall y\in U\}.

We now work with the descent of
\mathcal{N}
to
\Spf O_{F}
by Remark
5.2
, the definition of Bruhat–Tits strata also descends to
\Spf O_{F}
by Remark
5.13
.
For any perfect field
k
over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}
, we have natural bijections of
k
points of BT strata as above by applying relative Dieudonne theory over
k
. In conclusion, we have (see also
[
6
, Defn. 2.9, Prop. 2.18]
):

Proposition 7.10
.

(1)

For two vertex lattices
L^{\circ}_{1},L^{\circ}_{2}
of type
\leq t-1
, we have

\mathrm{BT}(L_{1}^{\circ})\cap\mathrm{BT}(L_{2}^{\circ})=\begin{cases}\mathrm{BT}(L^{\circ}_{1}\cap L^{\circ}_{2})&\text{if $L^{\circ}_{1}\cap L^{\circ}_{2}$ is a vertex lattice}\\
\emptyset&\text{else}\\
\end{cases}

(2)

For two vertex lattices
L^{\bullet}_{1},L^{\bullet}_{2}
of type
\geq t+1
, we have

\mathrm{BT}(L_{1}^{\bullet})\cap\mathrm{BT}({L_{2}^{\bullet}})=\begin{cases}\mathrm{BT}(L^{\bullet}_{1}+L^{\bullet}_{2})&\text{if $L^{\bullet}_{1}+L^{\bullet}_{2}$ is a vertex lattice}\\
\emptyset&\text{else}\\
\end{cases}

Theorem 7.11
.

The collection
\{\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})\}_{L^{\prime}}
indexed by vertex lattices
L^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{V}
gives a natural stratification on
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
. Each stratum
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})
is projective, smooth, and irreducible over
\mathbb{F}
, and isomorphic to a (closed) Deligne–Lusztig variety for
\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}(L^{\prime})_{0})
which is not of Coxeter type in general.

(1)

We have

\dim\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\frac{t-1-t(L^{\prime})}{2}+n-t&\text{if $t(L^{\prime})\leq t-1$}\\
\frac{t(L^{\prime})-t-1}{2}+t&\text{if $t(L^{\prime})\geq t+1$}\\
\end{cases}

(2)

\mathrm{BT}(L^{\bullet})
is a union of two classical Deligne–Lusztig varieties, depending on whether
U_{2}\subseteq U_{2}^{\perp}
. And
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\circ})
is a union of two classical Deligne–Lusztig varieties, depending on whether
U_{4}\subseteq U_{4}^{\perp}
.

(3)

\mathrm{B}T(L^{\circ})\cap\mathrm{B}T(L^{\bullet})
is non-empty if and only if
L^{\bullet}\subseteq L^{\circ}
. In this case,

(7.18)

\mathrm{B}T(L^{\circ})\cap\mathrm{B}T(L^{\bullet})(\mathbb{F})=\{(A,B)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})|L^{\bullet}\subseteq B^{\vee}\subseteq A\subseteq L^{\circ}\}.

is equal to
\mathbb{F}
-points of the flag variety
Fl_{(L^{\bullet},L^{\circ})}
parameterizing flags
(\overline{B^{\vee}},\overline{A})
of dimension
(\frac{t(L^{\bullet})-t-1}{2},\frac{t(L^{\bullet})-t+1}{2})
in the
\frac{t(L^{\bullet})-t(L^{\circ})}{2}
dimensional
\mathbb{F}
-vector space
L^{\circ}/L^{\bullet}
.

(4)

In particular if
t(L^{\bullet})=t+1
and
t(L^{\circ})=t-1
, then
\mathrm{B}T(L^{\circ})(\mathbb{F})\cong\mathbb{P}^{n-t}(\mathbb{F})
and
\mathrm{B}T(L^{\bullet})(\mathbb{F})\cong\mathbb{P}^{t}(\mathbb{F})
. And
\mathrm{B}T(L^{\circ})\cap\mathrm{B}T(L^{\bullet})(\mathbb{F})
is exactly one point if
L^{\bullet}\subseteq L^{\circ}
, and empty otherwise.

Proof.

This is
[
6
, Thm. 1.1]
after identifying the strata in
loc. cit.
with our definitions via special cycles. Proposition
7.8
shows the collection
\{\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})\}_{L^{\prime}}
covers
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
which forms a stratification. In
loc.cit.
, we identify
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})
with (closed) Deligne–Lusztig varieties (
7.16
,
7.17
). By the identification, we obtain dimensions, projectivity, smoothness and geometrical irreducibility of
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\prime})
.
∎

Remark 7.12
.

We can describe the induced Balloon-Ground stratification on the reduced locus
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
using Bruhat–Tits strata. For example, if
t=1
, then
(\mathcal{N}^{\circ})^{\mathrm{red}}
is the union of all type
0
Bruhat–Tits strata
\mathcal{Y}((L^{\circ})^{\vee})^{\mathrm{red}}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{L^{\circ}}
in
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
, and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}\subseteq(\mathcal{N})^{\mathrm{red}}
is the disjoint union of Fermat hypersurfaces in
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{L^{\circ}}
of degree
q+1
.

8.
Local modularity

In this section, we prove some local modularity results, which is used in §
13.4
to show (double) modularity of arithmetic theta series over the basic locus, see also Proposition
15.4
.

Let
L
be a vertex lattice of rank
n
and type
t
. Consider the intersection pairing between divisors
\mathcal{Z}
on
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{U}(L)}
and
1
-cycles
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
:

(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{C}):=\chi(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}}).

Consider the following functions on
u\in\mathbb{V}-0
:

f_{L}(\mathcal{C})(u):=(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathcal{C}),\quad f_{L^{\vee}}(\mathcal{C})(u):=(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathcal{C}).

Consider the Weil representation of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{V})
with respect to a fixed unramified non-trivial additive character
\psi:F_{0}\mathbb{C}
. Set
\psi_{F}=\psi\circ\tr_{F/F_{0}}
. Then for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{V})
, we have

(8.1)

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.f(u)=\gamma_{\mathbb{V}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}}(f)(u),

where
\gamma_{\mathbb{V}}=\eta(\det(\mathbb{V}))\in\{\pm 1\}
is the Weil constant of
\mathbb{V}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}}
is the Fourier transform on
\mathbb{V}
with respect to
\psi\circ q_{\mathbb{V}}
:

(8.2)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}}(f)(y):=\int_{x\in\mathbb{V}}f(x)\psi_{F}((x,y)_{\mathbb{V}})dx,\quad y\in\mathbb{V}.

Here the Haar measure is the self-dual one with respect to
\psi_{F}
.

A
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
is called
very special
, if it is a finite linear combination of
1
-cycles from standard embeddings of the form

\delta:\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}=\mathcal{N}

X^{\flat}X^{\flat}\times(\mathcal{E}^{[1]})^{t-1}\times\mathcal{E}^{n-t-1}.

induced by a decomposition of framing objects
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}^{\flat}\times(\mathbb{E}^{[1]})^{t-1}\times(\mathbb{E}^{[0]})^{n-t-1}
.

Theorem 8.1
.

Choose any
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
, and assume it is very special if
0<t<n
. Then
f_{L}(\mathcal{C})
and
f_{L^{\vee}}(\mathcal{C})
extend to Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{V}
. Moreover, we have the dual relation

(8.3)

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.f_{L}(\mathcal{C})=(-q)^{-t}f_{L^{\vee}}(\mathcal{C}).

Conjecture 8.2

( local modularity conjecture )
.

Theorem
8.1
holds for any
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
.

In the rest of this section, we prove the theorem. The case
t=0
follows from
[
37
, Lem. 6.2.1, Thm. 6.4.9]
. Consider the dual isomorphism
\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
. By Proposition
5.7
, we have

\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{Y}(u))=\mathcal{Z}(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ u),\quad\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{Z}(u))=\mathcal{Y}(\varpi^{-1}(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ u)).

We have
(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}-,\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}-)_{\mathbb{V}^{\vee}}=-\varpi(-,-)_{\mathbb{V}}
by Proposition
5.3
. Hence the self-dual Haar measure on
\mathbb{V}^{\vee}
is
|\varpi|^{n/2}_{F}=q^{-n}
times the Haar measure on
\mathbb{V}
. And for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{V})
, we have

(8.4)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}^{\vee}}(f\circ\lambda^{-1}_{\mathbb{V}})=q^{-n}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}}(f)\circ(-\varpi\lambda^{-1}_{\mathbb{V}})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{V}^{\vee}).

From this we may compute
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}^{\vee}}(\widetilde{f}_{\widetilde{L}^{\vee}}(\lambda_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{C})))(x)=q^{n}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}}(f_{L}(\mathcal{C}))(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}^{-1}\circ x)
for
x\in\mathbb{V}^{\vee}-0
, where
\widetilde{f}_{\widetilde{L}^{\vee}}
means
f_{L^{\vee}}
on the dual Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
.
Hence the case
t=t_{0}
follows from the case
t=n-t_{0}
.

So we may assume that
0<t<n
from now on. We could assume the very special
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
lives on a chosen embedding
\delta:\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}=\mathcal{N}
. Write
u\in\mathbb{V}
as

(8.5)

u=u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}\in\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}^{\flat}\oplus(\oplus_{i=1}^{t-1}Fe^{[1]}_{i})\oplus(\oplus_{j=1}^{n-t-1}Fe^{[0]}_{j}).

Proposition 8.3
.

After restriction to the embedding
\delta
, for any
u\in\mathbb{V}
we have

(1)

\mathcal{Z}(u)|_{\delta}=\mathcal{Z}(u_{1})1_{O^{t-1}_{F_{0}}}(u_{2})1_{O^{n-t-1}_{F_{0}}}(u_{3}).

(2)

\mathcal{Y}(u)|_{\delta}=\mathcal{Y}(u_{1})1_{\varpi^{-1}O^{t-1}_{F_{0}}}(u_{2})1_{O^{n-t-1}_{F_{0}}}(u_{3}).

Proof.

We can decompose the embedding into
\delta:\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}\overset{\delta_{2}}{}\mathcal{N}_{t+1}^{[t]}\overset{\delta_{1}}{}\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[t]}=\mathcal{N}
, then the result follows from moduli definitions of Kudla–Rapoport cycles.
∎

So by the projection formula for
\delta
, Theorem
8.1
for the very special
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
is reduced to the base case
n=2
and
t=1
.

8.1.
The base case
n=2,\,t=1
.

We now show Theorem
8.1
is true for
n=2
and
t=1
. As the starting vertex lattice in
V
is fixed, we use the notation
L
instead of
L^{\prime}
for a vertex lattice in
\mathbb{V}
, and write
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}_{2}
. By the main theorem in
[
29
]
, The space
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
is isomorphic to the Drinfeld half plane
\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{F_{0}}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
. Any Bruhat–Tits stratum
\mathrm{BT}(L)
in
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
is a projective line (see also
7.11
):

\mathbb{P}_{L}=\mathbb{P}(L^{\vee}/\varpi L^{\vee}).

We work with non-isotropic vector
u\in\mathbb{V}
i.e.,
(u,u)_{\mathbb{V}}\not=0
. So
m=v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}})
is finite, and we set
u_{1}=\varpi^{-\lfloor\frac{m+1}{2}\rfloor}u
. Note

(8.6)

v((u_{1},u_{1})_{\mathbb{V}})=\begin{cases}0&\text{$m$ is even},\\
-1&\text{$m$ is odd}.\\
\end{cases}

By
[
58
, Lem. 3.8]
(the notion of vertex lattices in
loc.cit.
is dual to us) which works for any
F_{0}
, there exists a unique vertex lattice
L_{u}
such that
u_{1}\in L^{\vee}_{u}-\varpi L^{\vee}_{u}
. Moreover,
L_{u}
is of type
0
(resp. type
2
) if
m
is even (resp. odd). We call
L^{\vee}_{u}
the
central lattice
of
u
.

Let
B
denote the Bruhat–Tits tree for
{\mathrm{SU}}(\mathbb{V}_{2})(F_{0})
, which is a graph with the following description
[
58
, Defn. 3.3]
. The vertices are vertex lattices in
\mathbb{V}_{2}
, and edges can only occur between vertex lattices of differing type. Two vertex lattices
L_{1},L_{2}
are joined by an edge if and only if
L_{1}\subseteq L_{2}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L_{1}
or
L_{2}\subseteq L_{1}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L_{2}
.This graph is a
q+1
-regular tree.

Let
d(L_{1},L_{2})
be the distance of two vertex lattices
L_{1},L_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{2}
. For a vector
u\in\mathbb{V}_{2}
and a vertex lattice
L\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{2}
, define the multiplicity

m(u,L)=\max(\max\{r\in\mathbb{Z}|\varpi^{-r}u\in L\},0).

Theorem 8.4
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{V}_{2}
with
m=v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}_{2}})\geq 0
, then we have a decomposition

(8.7)

\mathcal{Z}(u)=\mathcal{Z}(u)^{h}+\sum_{u\in L}m(u,L)\mathbb{P}_{L}

in the K-group of
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
supported on
\mathcal{Z}(u)
where

(1)

\mathcal{Z}(u)^{h}\cong\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
is the horizontal part of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
. It meets
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
at an ordinary point
x_{u}
on
\mathbb{P}_{L_{u}}
, corresponding to the non-isotropic line given by
u_{1}
in
L_{u}^{\vee}/\varpi L_{u}^{\vee}
.

(2)

For a vertex lattice
L
,
u\in L
if and only if
d(L,L_{u})\leq m
, in which case we have

(8.8)

m(u,L)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(m-d(L,L_{u})),&m=d(L,L_{u})\mod 2,\\
\frac{1}{2}(m+1-d(L,L_{u})),&m+1=d(L,L_{u})\mod 2.\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

The decomposition of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
is
[
58
, Thm. 3.14]
by computing explicit equations of
\mathcal{Z}(u)
on each Bruhat–Tits stratum. The multiplicity formula is
[
58
, Lem. 3.12]
. The proof works over any
F_{0}
.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

The case
m=0
and
m=1
form the base shapes of
\mathcal{Z}
-cycles. Note
m(u,L)=0
if
u\in L-\varpi L
. If
v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}})\geq 0
, we have
\mathcal{Z}(\varpi u)-\mathcal{Z}(u)=\sum_{u\in L}\mathbb{P}_{L}
.

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{V}_{2}
with
v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}_{2}})=m\geq-1
. Then we have a decomposition in the K-group of
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
with supported on
\mathcal{Y}(u)
:

(8.9)

\mathcal{Y}(u)=\mathcal{Y}(u)^{h}+\sum_{u\in L}m^{\vee}(u,L)\mathbb{P}_{L}

(1)

Here
\mathcal{Y}(u)^{h}\cong\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
is the horizontal part of
\mathcal{Y}(u)
. It meets
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
at an ordinary point
x_{u}
on
\mathbb{P}_{L_{u}}
, corresponding to the non-isotropic line given by
u_{1}
in
L_{u}^{\vee}/\varpi L_{u}^{\vee}
.

(2)

If
u\in L
, the multiplicity
m^{\vee}(u,L)
is equal to

(8.10)

m^{\vee}(u,L)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(m+2-d(L,L_{u})),&m=d(L,L_{u})\mod 2,\\
\frac{1}{2}(m+1-d(L,L_{u})),&m+1=d(L,L_{u})\mod 2.\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

We are done by applying the dual isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
(
5.1
) to Theorem
8.4
. Note if
d(L,L_{u})=m+1
, then
m^{\vee}(u,L)=0
. Hence we can write the summation
\sum m^{\vee}(u,L)\mathbb{P}_{L}
over vertex lattices
L
such that
d(L,L_{u})\leq m
i.e.,
u\in L
instead of
d(L,L_{u})\leq m+1
.
∎

Theorem 8.7
.

Consider a vertex lattice
L\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{2}
.

(1)

If
L
has type
0
, then for any
u\not=0
we have

(8.11)

(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L})=1_{L}(u),\,\,(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L})=-q1_{L}(u).

(2)

If
L
has type
2
, then for any
u\not=0
we have

(8.12)

(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L})=-q1_{L}(u),\,\,(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L})=1_{L^{\vee}}(u).

Proof.

The number
(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathbb{P}_{L})
for non-isotropic vector
u
is computed in
[
59
, Lem 2.10]
explicitly. The result depends on the parity of
d(L,L_{u})-v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}})
. But it is equivalent to our formulation, as if
L
is of type
0
(resp.
2
), then
d(L,L_{u})-v((u,u)_{\mathbb{V}})
is always even (resp. odd). We could also (re)prove the theorem using the Remark
8.5
and the case
m=0
and
m=1
. For isotropic vectors
u
, we reduce to non-isotropic case by the local constancy of intersection numbers
[
42
]
.

Applying the dual isomorphism
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
, we have
(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L})=(\mathcal{Z}(\lambda_{\mathbb{V}}\circ u),\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\lambda(L)})
. The result for
\mathcal{Y}
-cycles follows.
∎

The following corollary finishes the proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Corollary 8.8
.

Assume that
n=2,\,t=1
, in particular
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}_{2}
is split and
\gamma_{\mathbb{V}}=1
. Then for any
1
-cycle
C\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
, we have

(8.13)

\begin{pmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}.(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathcal{C})=-q^{-1}(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathcal{C}).

Proof.

Any vertical
1
-cycle in
(\mathcal{N}^{1}_{2})_{red}
is a finite linear combination of
\mathrm{BT}(L)
for vertex lattices
L\subseteq\mathbb{V}_{2}
. By self-duality of the Haar measure on
\mathbb{V}
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{V}}1_{L}=\vol(L)1_{L^{\vee}}
where
\vol(L)=(\#(L^{\vee}/L))^{-1/2}=q^{-t(L)}
. So the result follows from Theorem
8.7
.
∎

9.
ATCs for unramified maximal orders

In this section, we show Conjecture
6.4
for
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
holds in the case
O_{F}[g]
is a maximal order that is étale over
O_{F}
. By duality, we only need to work with Part
(1)
i.e., for
\mathcal{Z}
-cycles. Then we generalize the thesis of Mihatsch
[
40
, Section 9]
on AFL in the maximal order case to our set up assuming
F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is unramified.

9.1.
Semi-Lie ATC for
n=1

Assume that
n=1
, and the type of
L\subseteq V
is
t_{0}\in\{0,1\}
. Recall the standard function (
1.2
)

f_{\mathrm{std}}:=1_{S(L,L^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{(L^{\vee}_{0})^{*}}.

and a regular semisimple pair
(\gamma,b,c)\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times(V_{0})^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
.

Proposition 9.1
.

(1)

If
v(cb)=t_{0}\mod 2
, then

(9.1)

\Orb((\gamma,b,c),f_{\mathrm{std}})=\begin{cases}1&v(cb)\geq t_{0},\\
0&\text{else.}\\
\end{cases}

(2)

If
v(cb)=t_{0}+1\mod 2
, then
\Orb((\gamma,b,c),f_{\mathrm{std}})=0
and

(9.2)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,b,c),f_{\mathrm{std}})=\begin{cases}-(\log q)\frac{v(cb)-t_{0}+1}{2}&v(cb)\geq t_{0}+1,\\
0&\text{else.}\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

Choose a generator of
L
, then we are computing orbital integrals for
f_{\mathrm{std}}=1\times 1_{O_{F_{0}}}\times 1_{\varpi^{t_{0}}O_{F_{0}}^{*}}
. And the transfer factor is
\omega_{L}(\gamma,b,c)=(-1)^{v(b)}
. So
\Orb((\gamma,b,c),f_{\mathrm{std}},s)
is

(-1)^{v(b)}\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}_{1}(F_{0})}1_{O_{F_{0}}}(h^{-1}b)1_{\varpi^{t_{0}}O_{F_{0}}^{*}}(ch)(-1)^{v_{F}(h)}|h|^{s}dh=(-1)^{v(b)}\sum^{v(b)}_{k=-v(c)+t_{0}}(-1)^{k}q^{-ks}.

Take
s=0
, then the first part follows. Now assume that
v(cb)=t_{0}+1\mod 2
. Then we obtain

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl(\gamma,b,c,0)=(-1)^{v(b)}\sum_{k=-v(c)+t_{0}}^{v(b)}(-1)^{k}(\log q)(-k)=-\log q\frac{v(cb)-t_{0}+1}{2}.

∎

Proposition 9.2
.

For any regular semisimple pair
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have identifications

{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)=\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[t_{0}]}
and
\mathcal{Z}(u)\simeq\Spf O_{\breve{F}}/\varpi^{k}

where
k:=\max\{0,\frac{v((u,u))-t_{0}+1}{2}\}
.

Proof.

By the theory of canonical lifting
[
13
]
, we have
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[t_{0}]}\cong\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
(by duality we may assume
t_{0}=0
). As
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[t_{0}]}\times_{O_{\breve{F}}}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[t_{0}]}=\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[t_{0}]}
,
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
is the whole space
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[t_{0}]}
. The lifting condition for
\mathcal{Z}(u)
doesn’t depend on the polarization. Only the hermitian form on
\mathbb{V}
changes when we change
t_{0}=0
to
t_{0}=1
by scaling. So we may assume
t_{0}=0
. Again by the theory of canonical lifting, we have
\mathcal{Z}(u)\simeq\Spf O_{\breve{F}}/\varpi^{k}
.
∎

9.2.
Reduction in the maximal order case

Return to the semi-Lie Conjecture
6.4
for a vertex lattice
L\subseteq V
of type
t
and rank
n
. Recall the hermitian space
\mathbb{V}=\Hom_{O_{F}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{X})
.

Consider a maximal order
O_{F}[g]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}O_{F_{i}}\hookrightarrow\End(\mathbb{V})
which is étale over
O_{F}
i.e., all
F_{i}
are
unramified
field extensions of
F
. Then each
F_{i}
is an unramified quadratic extension of its fixed subfield
F_{i,0}
. Let
f_{i}
be the residual degree of
F_{i,0}
over
F_{0}
. As
F_{i}=F_{i,0}\otimes_{F_{0}}F
is a field, the degree
f_{i}
is odd for any
i
. We have a decomposition of
F/F_{0}
-hermitian spaces

(9.3)

\mathbb{V}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{V}_{i},\quad\mathbb{V}_{i}\cong F_{i}.

Lift the
F/F_{0}
-hermitian form on
\mathbb{V}_{i}
to a
F_{i}/F_{i,0}
-hermitian form on
\mathbb{V}_{i}
along the trace morphism
\tr:F_{i}F
. As
F_{i}
is unramified over
Fs
, the hermitian dual of any
O_{F_{i}}/O_{F_{i,0}}
hermitian lattice agrees with the hermitian dual of its underlying
O_{F}/O_{F_{0}}
hermitian lattice.

9.3.
Fixed points via Bruhat–Tits strata

We can compute
\mathbb{F}
-points of
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
for
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
using the Bruhat–Tits strata in §
7
.

Proposition 9.3
.

Assume that
O_{F}[g]
is an étale maximal order. Then there exists a unique vertex lattice
L\subseteq\mathbb{V}
that is
O_{F}[g]
-stable. Moreover, its type
t(L)=\sum_{i}f_{i}
where the index runs over
i
such that
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is non-split as a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space.

Proof.

Consider any
O_{F}[g]
-stable vertex lattice
L\subseteq\mathbb{V}
. Then
L=\prod_{i}L_{i}
as
O_{F}/O_{F_{0}}
-hermitian lattices. As
L\subseteq L^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L
, we have inclusions of
O_{F_{i}}
-stable lattices

L_{i}\subseteq L^{\vee}_{i}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L_{i}.

We know that there is a unique
O_{F_{i}}
-stable lattices in
\mathbb{V}_{i}
up to
O_{F_{i}}
-scaling. As
F_{i}/F_{0}
is unramified,
\varpi
is a uniformizer for
F_{i}
. We have two cases: either
L_{i}^{\vee}=L_{i}
thus
t(L_{i})=0
, which happens if and only if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is a split
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space; or
L_{i}^{\vee}=\varpi^{-1}L_{i}
thus
t(L_{i})=f_{i}
, which happens if and only if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is a non-split
F/F_{i,0}
-hermitian space.

Hence
L_{i}
are all uniquely determined. Conversely, we can construct an
O_{F}[g]
-stable lattice by reversing the above process.
∎

If
x\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})
is a fixed point of
g
, then the associated special pair
(A,B)
by Proposition
7.6
is also fixed by
g
. Recall
L_{A}
(resp.
L_{B}
) the smallest
\tau
-stable lattice containing
A
(resp.
B
) (
7.11
) is generated by
\tau^{i}(A)
(resp.
\tau^{i}(B)
) (
i=0,1,2,\dots
). So
L_{A},L_{B}
are
O_{F}[g]
-stable. Therefore, from Proposition
7.8
and Proposition
9.3
we see that

Corollary 9.4
.

Assume that
O_{F}[g]
is an étale maximal order. Let
L_{g}
be the unique
O_{F}[g]
-stable vertex lattice in
\mathbb{V}
. Then we have an inclusion

{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)(\mathbb{F})\subseteq\mathrm{BT}(L_{g}).

Hence
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)(\mathbb{F})
agrees with fixed points of
\overline{g}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}(L_{g})_{0})(\mathbb{F}_{q})
on the smooth projective variety
\mathrm{BT}(L_{g})
.

Example 9.5
.

Consider the case
\sum_{\text{$\mathbb{V}_{i}$ is not quasi-split}}f_{i}=t-1
. Then
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)(\mathbb{F})
is equal to the fixed points of
g\in\mathrm{U}(L_{g}/\varpi L_{g}^{\vee})
on
\mathcal{Y}(L^{\vee}_{g})=\mathbb{P}(L_{g}/\varpi(L^{\vee}_{g}))
. They corresponds to
O_{F}[g]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}O_{F_{i}}
-stable lines in
L_{g}/\varpi L^{\vee}_{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}}\mathbb{F}\cong\prod_{\text{$\mathbb{V}_{i}$ is quasi-split}}k_{F_{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}}\mathbb{F}
. Hence the number of fixed points of
g
is the number of index
i
such that
f_{i}=1
and
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is split.

If
n=2
and
t=1
, then
F[g]=F\times F
and
\mathbb{V}=\mathbb{V}_{1}\times\mathbb{V}_{2}
. We see any unramified maximal order
g\in\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{2})(F_{0})
has exactly two fixed points on
(\mathcal{N}^{[1]}_{2})^{\mathrm{red}}
, which lies in a type
0
(resp.
2
) Bruhat–Tits stratum
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L}
if
\mathbb{V}_{1}
and
\mathbb{V}_{2}
are both split (resp. non-split).

9.4.
Fixed points via moduli interpretation

By definition, the subscheme
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)\mathcal{N}
parameterizes tuples
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in\mathcal{N}
with an
O_{F}
-linear isomorphism
\varphi:XX

such that
\varphi^{*}\lambda=\lambda
and that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X\times_{S}\overline{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{X\times_{S}\overline{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathbb{X}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbb{X}\times_{\mathbb{F}}\overline{S}}

For any point
(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)\in{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)(S)
, using the idempotents in
O_{F}[g]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}O_{F_{i}}
we have a decomposition

(9.4)

(X,\iota,\lambda,\rho)=\prod_{i=1}^{m}(X_{i},\iota_{i},\lambda_{i},\rho_{i})

with induced decomposition
\ker\lambda=\prod_{i}\ker\lambda_{i}
Here
X_{i}
is a supersingular hermitian
O_{F_{i}}
-
O_{F_{0}}
module of dimension
n_{i}=\dim_{F}\mathbb{V}_{i}
and
O_{F_{0}}
-type
t_{i}
for some integer
t_{i}
. See
[
40
, Defn. 3.2]
,
[
6
, Defn. 3.21]
) for the notion. In particular, the
O_{F_{i}}
-
O_{F_{0}}
rank of
X_{i}
is
1
and
\ker\lambda_{i}\subseteq X_{i}[\varpi]
has order
q^{2t_{i}}
. By our assumption,
n_{i}=\dim_{F}\mathbb{V}_{i}=f_{i}
.

As
X
satisfies Kottwitz condition of signature
(1,n-1)
, there is a unique index
i_{0}
such that
X_{i_{0}}
has signature
(1,n_{i_{0}}-1)
and other
X_{i}
(
i\not=i_{0}
) has signature
(0,n_{i})
. Fix
i_{0}
and
t_{i}

(i=1,\ldots,m)
. The
O_{F}[g]
action on
\mathbb{X}
induces a decomposition (depending on
i_{0}
and
t_{i}
)

\mathbb{X}=\prod_{i}\mathbb{X}_{i}

where
\mathbb{X}_{i}
is a supersingular hermitian
O_{F_{i}}-O_{F_{0}}
module of
O_{F_{i}}/O_{F_{0}}
rank
1
and
O_{F_{0}}
-type
t_{i}
.

Consider the universal object
X
on
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
. As
F_{i}/F_{0}
is unramified, we have the comparison theorem between hermitian
O_{F_{i}}
-
O_{F_{0}}
modules and hermitian
O_{F_{i}}
modules, see
[
6
, Prop. 3.29]
. We obtain the following generalization of
[
40
, Prop. 4.14]
.

Theorem 9.6
.

There is a natural identification of formal schemes over
O_{\breve{F}}
:

(9.5)

{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)=\coprod_{(i_{0},\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m})}(\mathcal{N}_{F_{i_{0}},(1,0)}^{[t_{i_{0}}/f_{i_{0}}]}\times\prod_{i\not=i_{0}}\mathcal{N}^{[t_{i}/f_{i}]}_{F_{i},(0,1)})

where
(i_{0},\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m})
runs over integers
1\leq i_{0}\leq m,\,0\leq t_{i}\leq m
such that

(1)

f_{i}|t_{i}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}=t
.

(2)

t_{i_{0}}
is odd if
\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}
is split, and is even if
\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}
is non split.

(3)

For
i\not=i_{0}
,
t_{i}
is even if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is split, and is odd if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is non split.

Note
\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}=n\geq t=\sum_{i=1}t_{i}=t
, so condition
(1)
implies
t_{i}=0
or
f_{i}
. From this, we see

t_{i}=\begin{cases}f_{i}&\text{if $i=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}$ is split,}\\
0&\text{if $i=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}$ is not split,}\\
0&\text{if $i\not=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i}$ is split,}\\
f_{i}&\text{if $i\not=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i}$ is not split.}\\
\end{cases}

Corollary 9.7
.

Assume that
O_{F}[g]
is an étale maximal order. Then

(1)

{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
is formally smooth over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
.

(2)

We have
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)={}^{\mathbb{L}}\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)
.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[1]}
and
\mathcal{N}_{1}^{[1]}
are isomorphic to
\Spf O_{\breve{F_{i}}}=\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
,
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
is formally smooth over
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
.

For any fixed point
(A,B)\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbb{F})
of
g
, both
L_{A}
and
L_{B}
in Proposition
7.8
are
O_{F}[g]
-stable, so can’t both be vertex lattices by the uniqueness in Proposition
9.3
. So
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
doesn’t intersect with the link stratum
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}
, hence
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)=\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)
. As
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)
is purely
1
-dimensional of the expect dimension, we apply
[
67
, Lem. B.2.]
(note that
\mathcal{N}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times\mathcal{N}}
are regular) to see that

{}^{\mathbb{L}}\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)=\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}(g)
.
∎

Example 9.8
.

Assume that
t_{0}=1
,
f_{i}=1
and
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is split for all
i
, then
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)=\coprod_{i=1}^{n}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
which matches Example
9.5
.

9.5.
Geometric side

Consider a non-zero vector
u=(u_{i})\in\mathbb{V}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{V}_{i}
.

Proposition 9.9
.

On the
(i_{0},\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m})
-th copy of
\Spf O_{\breve{F}}
of
{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)
, we have

\mathcal{Z}(u)|_{\Spf O_{\breve{F}}}\cong\mathcal{Z}(u_{i_{0}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{F_{i_{0}},(1,0)}^{[t_{i_{0}}/f_{i_{0}}]}.

The
O_{\breve{F}}
-length of
\mathcal{Z}(u)|_{\Spf O_{\breve{F}}}
is

\begin{cases}\max\{0,\frac{v_{F}((u_{i},u_{i})_{\mathbb{V}})}{2}\}&\text{if $\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}$ is split,}\\
\max\{0,\frac{v_{F}((u_{i},u_{i})_{\mathbb{V}}+1}{2}\}&\text{if $\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}$ is not split.}\\
\end{cases}
.

Proof.

We observe that

\Hom_{O_{F}}(\mathcal{E},X)=\prod_{i}\Hom_{O_{F_{i}}}(\mathcal{E}\otimes_{O_{F}}O_{F_{i}},X_{i}).

Therefore, the lifting of
u
corresponds to liftings of each
u_{i}
on the right hand side of (
9.5
).

If
i\not=i_{0}
then
X_{i}
has signature
(0,1)
, hence
u_{i}
lifts to the whole space
\mathcal{N}^{[t_{i}/f_{i}]}_{F_{i},(0,1)}
by Lubin–Tate theory. So we have
\mathcal{Z}(u)|_{\Spf O_{\breve{F}}}\cong\mathcal{Z}(u_{i_{0}})
. The computation of
\length(\mathcal{Z}(u)|_{\Spf O_{\breve{F}}})
follows from the case
n=1,t\in\{0,1\}
, see Proposition
9.2
.
∎

Theorem 9.10
.

For any regular semisimple pair
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
such that
O_{F}[g]
is an étale maximal order, we have

\widetilde{\Int}^{\mathcal{Z}}(g,u)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\max\{0,\frac{v_{F}((u_{i},u_{i})_{\mathbb{V}})+a_{i}}{2}\}.

where
a_{i}=0
if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is split and
a_{i}=1
if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is not split.

Proof.

As
(u,u)_{\mathbb{V}}=\sum_{i}(u_{i},u_{i})_{\mathbb{V}}
, the result follows from Corollary
9.7
and Proposition
9.2
. ∎

9.6.
Analytic side via counting lattices

Recall that
L\subseteq V
is a vertex lattice of rank
n
and type
t=t_{0}
. Choose an orthogonal basis of
L
to endow
L
(resp.
V
) with an
O_{F_{0}}
(resp.
F_{0}
)-structure
L_{0}
(resp.
V_{0}
), which induces a
F/F_{0}
semi-linear involution
\tau=\overline{(-)}
on
V
with fixed subspace
V_{0}
. Recall the Haar measure on
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(F_{0})
is normalized so that the volume of
\mathrm{GL}(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})
is
1
.

Under the basis, we identify
L_{0}
(resp.
L_{0}^{\vee}
) with
O_{F_{0}}^{n}
(resp.
\varpi^{-1}O_{F_{0}}^{t_{0}}\oplus O_{F_{0}}^{n-t_{0}}
). The
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
-orbit of
(L,L^{\vee})
in the set of pairs of
O_{F}
-lattices
(L_{1},L_{2})\subseteq V
can be identified with the set

\mathrm{Lat}_{n,F}^{[t]}:=\{(L_{1},L_{2})\subseteq V|L_{1}\subseteq L_{2}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}L_{1},\quad[L_{2}:L_{1}]=t,\quad\tau(L_{1})=L_{1},\quad\tau(L_{2})=L_{2}\}.

Set

M_{n,F}^{[t]}:=\{(L_{1},L_{2})\in\mathrm{Lat}_{n,F}^{[t]}|u_{1}\in L_{1},\quad u_{2}\in L_{2}^{*},\quad\gamma L_{1}=L_{1},\quad\gamma L_{2}=L_{2}\},

M_{n,F,i}^{[t]}:=\{(L_{1},L_{2})\in M_{n,F}^{[t]}|[L_{1}:L]=i\}.

For the function
f_{\mathrm{std}}=1_{S(L,L^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{0}}\times 1_{(L^{\vee}_{0})^{*}}
and a regular semisimple pair
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
, write
(s\in\mathbb{C})

\Orb_{F}((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),s,t):=\Orb((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),f_{\mathrm{std}}).

Consider the lattice
L_{\gamma,u_{1}}=\sum_{i}O_{F}\gamma^{i}u_{1}.
Write
\ell=[L_{\gamma,u_{1}}:L]
.

Proposition 9.11
.

We have

\Orb_{F}((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),s,t)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i}(\#M_{n,F,i}^{[t]})q^{-(i+\ell)s}.

Proof.

By our choice of Haar measure, we have

\Orb_{F}((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),s,t)=\omega_{L}(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\int_{h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})/\mathrm{GL}(L_{0},L_{0}^{\vee})}f_{\mathrm{std}}(h.(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}))\eta(h)|h|^{s}dh.

By definition,
f_{\mathrm{std}}(h.(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}))
has value
1
if
(hL,hL^{\vee})\in M_{n,F}^{[t]}
and value
0
else. For
(L_{1},L_{2})=(hL,hL^{\vee})\in M_{n,F}^{[t]}
, we have

v(\det(h))=[hL:L]=[L_{1}:L]=[L_{1}:L_{\gamma,u_{1}}]+\ell
. We conclude by noting that the parity of
[L_{1}:L_{\gamma,u_{1}}]
is equal to the transfer factor
\omega_{L}(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})
.
∎

Now we assume
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})
matches
(g,u)
such that
O_{F}[g]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}O_{F_{i}}
is an étale maximal order. As the characteristic polynomial of
\gamma
agrees with
g
, the embedding
O_{F}[\gamma]\subseteq\End(V)
induces a compatible decomposition
V=\prod_{i=1}^{m}V_{i}
.
Here
V_{i}
is a
F_{i}/F_{0}
hermitian space such that
V_{i}\not\simeq\mathbb{V}_{i}
and
\dim_{F}V_{i}=f_{i}
. Consider the induced decomposition

(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}(\gamma_{i},u_{1i},u_{2i}).

Proposition 9.12
.

We have

(9.6)

\Orb_{F}((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),s,t)=\sum_{\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}}\prod\Orb_{F_{i}}((\gamma_{i},u_{1i},u_{2i}),s/f_{i},t_{i}/f_{i})

where
\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
runs over integers
0\leq t_{i}\leq n
such that
t_{i}\in\{0,f_{i}\}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}=t
.

Proof.

From definition, we have

M_{n,F}^{[t]}=\coprod_{\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}M_{1,F_{i}}^{[t_{i}/f_{i}]}.

where
\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
runs over integers
0\leq t_{i}\leq n
such that
f_{i}|t_{i}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}=t
. Note
\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}=n\geq t
, we see
t_{i}\leq f_{i}
hence
t_{i}\in\{0,f_{i}\}
. By definition,
\omega_{L}(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})=\ell=\prod_{i}\ell_{i}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\omega_{L_{i}}(\gamma_{i},u_{1i},u_{2i})
. So the result follows from Proposition
9.11
.
∎

Corollary 9.13
.

We have an equality in
\mathbb{Q}\log q
:

\operatorname{\partial Orb}_{F}\bigl((\gamma,u_{1},u_{2}),t)=\sum_{i_{0},\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}}\frac{1}{f_{i_{0}}}\operatorname{\partial Orb}_{F_{i_{0}}}((\gamma_{i_{0}},u_{1i_{0}},u_{2i_{0}})),t_{i_{0}}/f_{i_{0}})\prod_{i\not=i_{0}}\Orb_{F_{i}}((\gamma_{i},u_{1i},u_{2i}),t_{i}/f_{i}).

where
i_{0}
runs over integers
1\leq i_{0}\leq n
with

t_{i}=\begin{cases}f_{i}&\text{if $i=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}$ is split,}\\
0&\text{if $i=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i_{0}}$ is not split,}\\
0&\text{if $i\not=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i}$ is split,}\\
f_{i}&\text{if $i\not=i_{0}$, $\mathbb{V}_{i}$ is not split.}\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

The equality follows from above proposition by taking derivative. We only need to explain the conditions on
(i_{0},\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m})
. The pair
(\gamma_{i},u_{1i},u_{2i})
matches the pair
(g_{i},u_{i})\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V}_{i})\times\mathbb{V}_{i})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
.

Consider any
i\not=i_{0}
. From §
9.1
, we have the vanishing of
\Orb_{F_{i}}((\gamma_{i},u_{1i},u_{2i}),0,t_{i}/f_{i})
unless
t_{i}=0
if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is split and
t_{i}=f_{i}
if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is non-split.

Compare
\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}=t
and
\mathbb{V}=\oplus_{i=1}\mathbb{V}_{i}
(using
f_{i}
is odd) , we see
t_{i_{0}}=f_{i_{0}}
(resp.
t_{i_{0}}=0
) if
\mathbb{V}_{i}
is split (resp. not split).
∎

Theorem 9.14
.

Let
L
be a vertex lattice of dimension
n
and type
t
. Consider a regular semisimple pair
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(\mathbb{V})\times\mathbb{V})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
matching
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
. Assume that
O_{F}[g]
is an étale maximal order, then Conjecture
6.4
holds for
(g,u)
and
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
9.10
and Corollary
9.13
and the computation in the case
n=1
in §
9.1
.
∎

Part III
Modularity of arithmetic theta series

10.
Integral models and Balloon–Ground stratification

In this section, we introduce the RSZ-variant of unitary Shimura varieties
[
51
,
48
]
for parahoric hermitian lattices as a globalization of Rapoport–Zink spaces studied in Part
II
, which admits a natural integral model
\mathcal{M}
with PEL type moduli interpretation. Then we globalize the formal Balloon–Ground stratification in §
5.3
to the Balloon–Ground stratification on mod
p
fiber of
\mathcal{M}
.

10.1.
The Shimura data

Let
F/F_{0}
be a totally imaginary quadratic extension of a totally real number field. We always assume that
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
.

Write
\Sigma_{F}:=\Hom(F,\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
and choose a CM type
\Phi\subseteq\Sigma_{F}
of
F
with a distinguished element
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi
. Let
V
be a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n\geq 1
with signature
\{(r_{\varphi},s_{\varphi})_{\varphi\in\Phi}\}=\{(n-1,1)_{\varphi_{0}},(n,0)_{\varphi\in\Phi-\{\varphi_{0}\}}\}
under a fixed embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

Consider the reductive group
G:=\mathrm{U}(V)
over
F_{0}
, and the following reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}
:

Z^{\mathbb{Q}}:=\{x\in\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}|\,\Nm_{F/F_{0}}x\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\},

G^{\mathbb{Q}}:=\{g\in\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{GU}(V)|\,c(g)\in\mathbb{G}_{m}\},

where
c:\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{GU}(V)\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
denotes the similitude character.

For any
\varphi\in\Phi
, choose a basis of
V_{\varphi}
over
\mathbb{C}
under which the hermitian form is given by
\diag\{1_{r_{\varphi}},-1_{s_{\varphi}}\}
. It induces a homomorphism

h_{G^{\mathbb{Q}},\varphi}:\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\mathrm{GU}(V_{\varphi}),\quad z\diag\{z1_{r_{\varphi}},\overline{z}1_{s_{\varphi}}\}.

The homomorphism

(10.1)

h_{G^{\mathbb{Q}}}:\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}G^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathbb{R}}\hookrightarrow\prod_{\varphi\in\Phi}\mathrm{GU}(V_{\varphi}),\quad z(h_{G^{\mathbb{Q}},\varphi}(z))_{\varphi\in\Phi}

gives a Shimura datum
(G^{\mathbb{Q}},\{h_{G^{\mathbb{Q}}}\})
. The homomorphism

(10.2)

h_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}:\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G)_{\mathbb{R}}\hookrightarrow\prod_{\varphi\in\Phi}\mathrm{U}(V_{\varphi}),\quad z(\diag\{1_{r_{\varphi}},\overline{z}/z1_{s_{\varphi}}\})_{\varphi\in\Phi}

gives a Shimura datum
(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G,\{h_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}\})
(see
[
51
, Section 2.3]
).

The homomorphism

(10.3)

h_{\Phi}:\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathbb{R}}\hookrightarrow\prod_{\varphi\in\Phi}\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m},\quad z(\varphi(z))_{\varphi\in\Phi}

gives a Shimura datum
(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},h_{\Phi})
whose reflex field
F^{\Phi}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
is the subfield fixed by
\{\sigma\in\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})|\sigma\circ\Phi=\Phi\}
. Consider the fibre product

\widetilde{G}=Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}G^{\mathbb{Q}}

along projection maps
\Nm_{F/F_{0}}:Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
c:G^{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The homomorphism

h_{\widetilde{G}}=(h_{\Phi},h_{G^{\mathbb{Q}}}):\Res_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\widetilde{G}_{\mathbb{R}}

gives a Shimura datum
(\widetilde{G},\{h_{\widetilde{G}}\})
whose reflex field
E
is the composition
\varphi_{0}(F)F^{\Phi}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Note there is a natural isomorphism

(10.4)

Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}G^{\mathbb{Q}}\cong Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\times\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G,\quad(z,g)(z,z^{-1}g).

For neat compact open subgroups
K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\subseteq Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
K_{G}\subseteq G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
, the
RSZ unitary Shimura variety

[
51
]
with level
K_{\widetilde{G}}=K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\times K_{G}
is the Shimura variety associated to
(\widetilde{G},\{h_{\widetilde{G}}\})
(over
\mathbb{C}
):

(10.5)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\widetilde{G}}}(\widetilde{G},\{h_{\widetilde{G}}\})(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},h_{\Phi})(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G}}(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G,\{h_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}\})(\mathbb{C}).

Remark 10.1
.

By
[
48
, Rem. 3.1]
, We have
E=\varphi_{0}(F)
, if
F
is Galois over
\mathbb{Q}
or
F=F_{0}K
for an imaginary quadratic field
K
over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Denote by

M=M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\Spec E

the canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(\widetilde{G},h_{\widetilde{G}})
, which is a
n-1
dimensional smooth
projective
(as
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
) variety over
E
.

10.2.
The level for
L
and
\Delta

Choose a finite collection
\Delta
of finite places for
F_{0}
such that
F/F_{0}
is unramified outside
\Delta
and all
2
-adic places are in
\Delta
.

Let
L
be a hermitian lattice in
V
. If
v\not\in\Delta
is a finite place of
F_{0}
such that the localization
L_{v}
is not self-dual, then we assume
v
is inert in
F
and moreover

•

L_{v}\subseteq L_{v}^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi_{v}^{-1}L_{v}
and
L_{v}^{\vee}/L_{v}
has size
q_{v}^{2t_{v}}
for some
0\leq t_{v}\leq n
i.e.,
L_{v}
is a vertex lattice in
V_{v}
of type
t_{v}
.

•

If
0<t_{v}<n
, then
E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p_{v}}
decomposes into unramified extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p_{v}}
. Here
p_{v}>2
is the underlying prime of
v
. In particular,
F_{v}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p_{v}}
.

Let
K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\Delta}
(resp.
K_{G,\Delta}
) be a compact open subgroup of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(F_{0,\Delta})
(resp.
\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0,\Delta})
). Let
K_{G}(L)^{\Delta}
be the (completed) stabilizer of
L
in
\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{\Delta})
, and
K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Delta}
be the unique maximal compact open subgroup of

Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{\Delta})
. We call

(10.6)

{K_{\widetilde{G}}=K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\Delta}\times K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Delta}\times K_{G,\Delta}\times K_{G}(L)^{\Delta}\subseteq\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}).}

a level structure for
L
and
\Delta
.

10.3.
The PEL type moduli

Now we define the integral model for
M
with level for
L
and
\Delta
generalizing the construction in
[
37
, Section 14.1]
,
[
51
, Section 6.1]
. Consider the functor

\mathcal{M}_{0}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]

sending a locally noetherian
O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
-scheme
S
to the groupoid
\mathcal{M}_{0}(S)
of tuples
(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\overline{\eta}_{0})
where

•

A_{0}
is an abelian scheme over
S
of dimension
[F_{0}:\mathbb{Q}]
.

•

\iota_{0}:O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]\End(A_{0})[\Delta^{-1}]
is an
O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]
-action satisfying the
Kottwitz condition
of signature
\{(1,0)_{\varphi\in\Phi}\}
:

(10.7)

\charac(\iota_{0}(a)\mid\Lie A_{0})=\prod_{\varphi\in\Phi}(T-\varphi(a))\in\mathcal{O}_{S}[T].

•

\lambda_{0}:A_{0}A_{0}^{\vee}
is an away-from-
\Delta
principal polarization such that for all
a\in O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]
we have
\lambda^{-1}_{0}\circ\iota_{0}(a)^{\vee}\circ\lambda_{0}=\iota_{0}(\overline{a})
.

•

\overline{\eta}_{0}
is a
K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\Delta}
-level structure as in
[
33
, Section C.3]
.

An isomorphism between two tuples is a quasi-isogeny preserving the polarization and
K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\Delta}
-level structure. The functor
\mathcal{M}_{0}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
is representable, finite and étale
[
17
, Prop. 3.1.2]
.

From now on, we assume that
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is non-empty which holds if
F/F_{0}
is ramified at some place, see
[
48
, Rem. 3.5 (ii)]
. The generic fiber
M_{0}
of
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is a disjoint union of copies of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},h_{\Phi})
, see
[
48
, Lem 3.4]
. We work with one copy and still denote its étale integral model by
\mathcal{M}_{0}
.

Remark 10.2
.

We allow
L
to be non-self dual at some
v\not\in\Delta
, so
\mathcal{M}_{0}
can be non-empty even if
F/F_{0}
is unramified everywhere.

Definition 10.3
.

The integral RSZ Shimura variety with level
K
for
L
and
\Delta
is the functor

\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]

sending a locally noetherian
O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
-scheme
S
to the groupoid of tuples

(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\overline{\eta}_{0},A,\iota,\lambda,\overline{\eta}_{\Delta})
where

•

(A_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\overline{\eta}_{0})
is an object of
\mathcal{M}_{0}(S)
.

•

A
is an abelian scheme over
S
of dimension
n[F_{0}:\mathbb{Q}]
.

•

\iota:O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]\End(A)[\Delta^{-1}]
is an
O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]
-action satisfying the
Kottwitz condition
of signature
\{(n-1,1)_{\varphi_{0}},(n,0)_{\varphi\in\Phi-\{\varphi_{0}\}}\}
.

•

\lambda:AA^{\vee}
is a polarization such that for all
a\in O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]
we have
\lambda^{-1}\circ\iota(a)^{\vee}\circ\lambda=\iota(\overline{a}).

•

For any finite place
v\not\in\Delta
of
F_{0}
, the induced map
\lambda_{v}:A[\varpi_{v}^{\infty}]A^{\vee}[\varpi_{v}^{\infty}]
has kernel
\text{Ker}\lambda_{v}\subseteq A[\varpi_{v}]
which has order
\#L^{\vee}_{v}/L_{v}=q_{v}^{2t_{v}}
.

•

\overline{\eta}_{\Delta}
is a
K_{G,\Delta}
-orbit of isometries of hermitian modules

\eta_{\Delta}:V_{\Delta}(A_{0},A)=\prod_{v\in\Delta}V_{v}(A_{0},A)\cong V(F_{0,\Delta})

as smooth
F_{0,\Delta}
-sheaves on
S
. Here

V_{v}(A_{0},A)=\Hom_{F\otimes_{F_{0}}F_{0,v}}(V_{v}A_{0},V_{v}A)
is the Hom space between rational Tate modules of
A_{0}
and
A
, with the hermitian form

(x,y):=\lambda_{0}^{-1}\circ y^{\vee}\circ\lambda\circ x\in F_{v}.

•

For any finite place
v\not\in\Delta
of
F_{0}
, we put the sign condition and Eisenstein condition at
v
as in
[
48
, Section 4.1]
. By
[
48
, Section 4.1]
and
[
51
, Rem. 6.5 (i)]
, the Eisenstein condition is automatic when the places of
F
above
v
are unramified over
p
, and the sign condition is automatic when
v
is split in
F
.

An isomorphism between two tuples is a pair of quasi-isogenies
(\phi_{0},\phi):(A_{0},A)(A_{0}^{\prime},A^{\prime})
preserving polarizations and the
K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\Delta}\times K_{G,\Delta}
-orbit of level structures.

Theorem 10.4
.

[
67
, Thm. 6.2]

The functor
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
is representable by a separated scheme flat and of finite type over
\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
is smooth over
O_{E}\otimes_{O_{F}}O_{F,v}
for any finite place
v\not\in\Delta
such that
L_{v}
is self-dual.

From now on, for simplicity we write
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
and write
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})
for a
S
-point of
\mathcal{M}
. By the assumption
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathcal{M}
is in fact projective over
O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
.

10.4.
The singularity and Balloon–Ground stratification

Let
v\not\in\Delta
be a finite place of
F_{0}
such that
L_{v}
is not self-dual. Choose a place
\nu|v
of the reflex field
E
. Denote by
k_{v}=\mathbb{F}_{q_{v}}
(resp.
k_{\nu}
) the residue field of
F_{0,v}
(resp.
E_{\nu}
). Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
the special fiber of
\mathcal{M}
over
k_{\nu}
.

Theorem 10.5
.

[
6
, Prop. 4.2.]

If
t_{v}=n
, then
\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}
is smooth. If
0<t_{v}<n
, then
\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}
is of strictly semi-stable reduction.

The proof is based on computations of local models. For our need, we give a different proof using Grothendieck–Messing theory, which contains finer information and relates the singularity of
\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E,\nu}
with the Balloon-Ground stratification on
\mathcal{M}
.

Let
S
be a locally noetherian scheme over
k_{\nu}
. For any point
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\in\mathcal{M}(S)
, consider the bundle
\omega_{A^{\vee}}:=(\Lie A^{\vee})^{\vee}
. The Hodge filtration induces a short exact sequence of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules:

(10.8)

0\omega_{A^{\vee}}H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)\Lie A0.

which decomposes under the
O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}]
-action into
\varphi
-parts for
\varphi\in\Phi\cup\Phi^{c}=\Hom(F,\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
:

0(\omega_{A^{\vee}})_{\varphi}H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi}(\Lie A)_{\varphi}0.

By the Kottwitz signature condition,
(\omega_{A^{\vee}})_{\varphi_{0}}
(resp.
(\omega_{A^{\vee}})_{\varphi_{0}^{c}}
) is a vector bundle on
S
of rank
1
(resp.
n-1
).

Definition 10.6
.

(1)

The
Balloon stratum

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
is the vanishing locus of the universal section
\Lie\lambda:(\Lie A)_{\varphi_{0}^{c}}(\Lie A^{\vee})_{\varphi_{0}}
inside
\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}
.

(2)

The
Ground stratum

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
is the vanishing locus of the universal section
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}:(\Lie A^{\vee})_{\varphi_{0}}(\Lie A)_{\varphi_{0}^{c}}
inside
\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}
. Here
\lambda^{\vee}
is the dual polarization of
\lambda
at
v
such that
\lambda^{\vee}\circ\lambda=[\varpi_{v}]
.

(3)

The
Linking stratum

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\dagger}:=\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}\cap\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}^{\bullet}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}
.

As
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}\circ\Lie\lambda=[\varpi_{v}]
, we have the
Balloon–Ground stratification

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}=\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}\cup\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
, which globalizes the

formal Balloon–Ground stratification
in §
5.3
. Consider the bilinear form

(-,-)_{\varphi}:H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi}\times H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi^{c}}O_{S}

induced by the polarization
\lambda:AA^{\vee}
. As the kernel
\text{Ker}\,\lambda[\varpi_{v}]
has order
q^{2t_{v}}
, the orthogonal complements
(H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi_{0}^{c}})^{\perp}\subseteq H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi_{0}}
and
(H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi_{0}})^{\perp}\subseteq H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi_{0}^{c}}
are vector bundles of rank
t_{v}
over
S
.

Proposition 10.7
.

The Balloon stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
(resp. Ground stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
) is the closed locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
where
(\omega_{A^{\vee}})_{\varphi_{0}}\subseteq(H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi_{0}^{c}})^{\perp}
(resp.
(H^{\mathrm{dR}}_{1}(A)_{\varphi_{0}})^{\perp}\subseteq(\omega_{A^{\vee}})_{\varphi_{0}^{c}}
).

Proof.

This is proved in the same way as Proposition
5.12
.
∎

Theorem 10.8
.

If
0<t_{v}<n
, then
\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}
is of strictly semi-stable reduction. Moreover,

(1)

For a geometric point
x
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\dagger}
, the completed local ring of
\mathcal{M}
at
x
admits a smooth map to
W(k_{\nu})[[x_{1},y_{1}]]/(x_{1}y_{1}-p)
such that
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
) is the pullback of the Cartier divisor
x_{1}=0
(resp.
y_{1}=0
).

(2)

The Balloon stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
and the Ground stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
are smooth projective varieties of dimension
n-1
over
k_{\nu}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Proposition
5.9
and
[
35
, Thm. 5.2.5]
. We compute the completed local rings via Grothendieck–Messing theory. By our assumption on
L
, the local ring
O_{E_{\nu}}\simeq W(k_{\nu})
is the ring of Witt vectors for
k_{\nu}
. Let
k
be any perfect field over
k_{\nu}
and
A
be a
k
-point of
\mathcal{M}
.

Denote by
\mathbb{D}(A)
the covariant Dieudonne crystal of
A
. Consider the Dieudonne module
M=\mathbb{D}(A)(W(k))
which is finite free over
W(k)
of rank
2n[F_{0}:\mathbb{Q}]
. By the signature condition, we only need to consider deformations
\varphi_{0}
and
\varphi_{0}^{c}
part of the Hodge Filtration of
A
. Now
M_{\varphi_{0}}
and
M_{\varphi^{c}_{0}}
are finite free over
W(k)
of rank
n
. The short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\Lie(A^{\vee})^{\vee}\rightarrow H_{1}^{\mathrm{dR}}(A)\rightarrow\Lie(A)\rightarrow 0

can be identified by Dieudonne theory with (
V_{M}
is the Verschiebung operator on
M
)

0\rightarrow V_{M}M/\varpi_{v}M\rightarrow M/\varpi_{v}M\rightarrow M/V_{M}M\rightarrow 0.

Let
V=K^{n}
be a
n
-dimensional quadratic space over
K:=W(k)[1/p]
with the bilinear form

(x,y)=\sum_{i=1}^{t_{v}}\varpi_{v}x_{i}y_{i}+\sum_{i=t_{v}+1}^{n}x_{i}y_{i}.

Let
Ker
be the radical of this bilinear form. We can identify
M_{\varphi_{0}}\otimes K
and
M_{\varphi^{c}_{0}}\otimes K
with
V
such that the pairing between them induced by
\lambda
can be identified with the quadratic form above. By Grothendieck–Messing theory we see that the local model for
\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}
is the following moduli problem
(\ell,H)
for
W(k_{\nu})
-algebras
R
where
p\in R
is locally nilpotent:

\ell
is a line in
V\otimes R
and
H
is a hyperplane in
V\otimes R
such that
(\ell,H)=0
.

The Hodge filtration of
A
defines such a pair
(\ell_{0},H_{0})
for
R=k
. And we can compute the completed local ring of
\mathcal{M}
at
x
by deforming
(\ell_{0},H_{0})
in the local model. If
\text{Ker}\not\subseteq H
, then
\text{Ker}+H=V\otimes R
, hence
(\ell,V\otimes R))=0
i.e.,
\ell\subseteq\text{Ker}
. So we have the following three cases:

•

\ell\subseteq\text{Ker}
, and
\text{Ker}\subseteq H
, which corresponds to
x\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}
.

•

\ell\not\subseteq\text{Ker}
, and
\text{Ker}\subseteq H
, which corresponds to
x\in\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}-\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}
.

•

\ell\subseteq\text{Ker}
, and
\text{Ker}\not\subseteq H
, which corresponds to
x\in\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}-\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}
.

Standard computations show the semi-stable reduction results
(1)
and
(2)
.
∎

Remark 10.9
.

If
t_{v}=1
i.e,
L_{v}
is almost-self dual, the Balloon–Ground stratification is introduced in
[
35
, Section 5]
under the assumption that
p
is a special inert prime in particular
F_{0,v_{0}}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. In this case, the Balloon stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
lies in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. Hence the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
contains some irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. Such phenomenon also appears in Stamm’s example
[
57
]
for Hilbert modular surfaces at Iwahori levels.

11.
Kudla–Rapoport cycles and modified Hecke CM cycles

In this section, we globalize the construction in §
5
. Fix a finite place
v_{0}\not\in\Delta
of
F_{0}
such that
L_{v_{0}}
is not self-dual. We introduce two kinds of Kudla–Rapoport divisors on the integral model with testing functions
1_{L_{v_{0}}}
and
1_{L_{v_{0}}^{\vee}}
at
v_{0}
respectively. Then we desingularize the self-product
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}
and define (derived) modified Hecke CM cycles on
\mathcal{M}
. Finally, we prove basic uniformization results to connect with §
5
.

11.1.
The Kudla–Rapoport divisors over the generic fiber

Consider any neat compact open subgroup
K_{\widetilde{G}}=K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\times K_{G}
of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(not necessarily from a lattice
L
). For any
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\in M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(S)
, endow the
F
-vector space
\Hom^{\circ}_{F}(A_{0},A)
with the hermitian form

(11.1)

(u_{1},u_{2}):=\lambda_{0}^{-1}\circ u_{2}^{\vee}\circ\lambda\circ u_{1}\in\End^{\circ}_{F}(A_{0})\simeq F.

Definition 11.1
.

Let
\xi\in F_{0}
and
\mu\in V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}
. The
Kudla–Rapoport cycle

Z(\xi,\mu)
is the functor sending any locally noetherian
E
-scheme
S
to the groupoid of tuples

(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta},u)
where

\bullet

(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\in M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(S)
.

\bullet

u\in\Hom^{\circ}_{F}(A_{0},A)
such that
(u,u)=\xi
.

\bullet

\overline{\eta}(u)
is in the
K_{G}
-orbit
\mu
.

An isomorphism between two tuples
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta},u)
and
(A_{0}^{\prime},A^{\prime},\overline{\eta}^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is an isomorphism of tuples
(\phi_{0},\phi):(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\simeq(A_{0}^{\prime},A^{\prime},\overline{\eta}^{\prime})
such that
\phi\circ u=u^{\prime}\circ\phi_{0}
.

By positivity of the Rosati involution,
Z(\xi,\mu)
is empty unless
\xi\in F_{0,+}
is totally positive or
\xi=0
. The natural forgetful morphism
i:Z(\xi,\mu)M
is finite and unramified
[
33
, Prop. 4.22]
. It is étale locally a Cartier divisor if
\xi\not=0
.

By taking the image of
Z(\xi,\mu)
(with multiplicities) inside
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
, we view
Z(\xi,\mu)
as elements in the Chow group (of
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients)
{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}})
. For a Schwartz function
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K_{G}}
and
\xi\in F_{0,+}
, the
\phi
-averaged Kudla–Rapoport divisor is the finite summation

(11.2)

Z(\xi,\phi)\colon=\sum_{\mu\in V_{\xi}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}}\phi(\mu)\,Z(\xi,\mu)\in{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}).

Here
V_{\xi}=\{x\in V|(x,x)=\xi\}
is the hyperboloid in
V
of length
\xi
. We put

Z(0,\phi):=-\phi(0)c_{1}(\omega)\in{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}})

where
\omega
is the automorphic line bundle (Hodge bundle) as in
[
25
]
, which is a descent of the tautological line bundle on the hermitian symmetric domain
X
of
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
to
E
.

11.2.
Generating series

For
\xi\in F_{0}
, consider the weight
n
Whittaker function on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\infty})
:

W^{(n)}_{\xi}(h_{\infty})=\prod_{v|\infty}W^{(n)}_{\xi}(h_{v}).

Consider the Weil representation
\omega=\omega_{V}
of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
on
\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))
which commutes with the natural action of
\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
. For a Schwartz function
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K_{G}}
, we form
the generating series of Kudla–Rapoport divisors
on
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
by

(11.3)

Z(h,\phi):=W^{(n)}_{0}(h_{\infty})Z(0,\omega(h_{f})\phi)+\sum_{\xi\in F_{0,+}}W^{(n)}_{\xi}(h_{\infty})Z(\xi,\omega(h_{f})\phi)\in{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}),

for any
h=(h_{\infty},h_{f})\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})={\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\infty})\times{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
.

For any
h_{f}\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
, by definition we have

(11.4)

Z(h,\omega(h_{f})\phi)=Z(hh_{f},\phi).

The known modularity over
E

[
34
,
72
]
is one main input of our modularity result.

Proposition 11.2
.

[
67
, Thm. 8.1]

The function
Z(h,\phi)
is a weight
n
holomorphic automorphic form valued in
{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}})
i.e., we have

Z(h,\phi)\in\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}

where
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
is any compact open subgroup that fixes
\phi
under the Weil representation.

11.3.
The Kudla–Rapoport divisors on the integral model
\mathcal{M}

Consider the level structure for
L
and
\Delta
in §
10.2
:

K_{\widetilde{G}}=K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Delta}\times K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\Delta}\times K_{G,\Delta}\times K_{G}(L)^{\Delta}\leq\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})

So
K_{G,v_{0}}=\mathrm{U}(L_{v_{0}})
and
L_{v_{0}}
is a vertex lattice of type
0\leq t_{0}\leq n
.

For any point
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\in\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(S)
, consider the hermitian form
(\cdot,\cdot)
on
\Hom_{O_{F}}(A_{0},A)[1/\Delta]
given by

(u_{1},u_{2})=\lambda_{0}^{-1}\circ u_{2}^{\vee}\circ\lambda\circ u_{1}\in\End_{O_{F}}(A_{0})[1/\Delta]\simeq O_{F}[1/\Delta].

The polarization
\lambda:AA^{\vee}
gives an injection

\lambda\circ-:\Hom_{O_{F}}(A_{0},A)[1/\Delta]\Hom_{O_{F}}(A_{0},A^{\vee})[1/\Delta]

Definition 11.3
.

Let
\xi\in F_{0,+}
and
\mu_{\Delta}\in V(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}
.
The Kudla–Rapoport cycle

\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})
(resp.
\mathcal{Y}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})
) is the functor sending any locally noetherian scheme
S
over
O_{E}[1/\Delta]
to the groupoid of tuples
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta},u)
where

\bullet

(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\in\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(S)
;

\bullet

u\in\Hom^{\circ}_{F}(A_{0},A)
with
(u,u)=\xi
. And
u\in\Hom_{O_{F}}(A_{0},A)[1/\Delta]
(resp.
\lambda\circ u\in\Hom_{O_{F}}(A_{0},A^{\vee})[1/\Delta]
).

\bullet

\overline{\eta}(u)
is in the
K_{G,\Delta}
-orbit
\mu_{\Delta}
.

Proposition 11.4
.

The morphisms
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
and
\mathcal{Y}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
are representable, finite and unramified, and étale locally Cartier divisors on
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
.

Proof.

This follows from the proof of
[
33
, Prop. 4.22]
.
∎

Remark 11.5
.

We have a natural inclusion
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})\mathcal{Y}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})
. In general, the Cartier divisors
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})
and
\mathcal{Y}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})
may not be flat over
O_{E}
, see the Shimura curve example
[
28
]
.

Choose
\phi^{\Delta,v_{0}}\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}^{\Delta,v_{0}}_{f}))
as the indicator function of the completion of
L
inside
V(\mathbb{A}^{\Delta,v_{0}}_{f})
. For
i\in\{1,2\}
, consider the Schwartz function
\phi_{i}=\phi_{\Delta}\otimes\phi_{i,v_{0}}\otimes\phi^{\Delta,v_{0}}\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K_{G}}
where
\phi_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(V(F_{\Delta}))^{K_{G,\Delta}}
and

(11.5)

\phi_{1,v_{0}}=1_{L_{v_{0}}},\quad\phi_{2,v_{0}}=1_{L_{v_{0}}^{\vee}}.

For
\xi\in F_{0,+}
, the
\phi_{i}
-averaged Kudla–Rapoport divisor is the finite summation

(11.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{1})\colon=\sum_{\mu_{\Delta}\in V_{\xi}(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}}\phi_{\Delta}(\mu_{\Delta})\,\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta}),

(11.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{2})\colon=\sum_{\mu_{\Delta}\in V_{\xi}(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}}\phi_{\Delta}(\mu_{\Delta})\,\mathcal{Y}(\xi,\mu_{\Delta})

viewed as elements in the Chow group
{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}})
.

Proposition 11.6
.

For
\phi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K_{G}}
above (
i=1,2
), the generic fiber of
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{i})
over
E
agrees with
Z(\xi,\phi_{i})
defined by (
11.2
).

Proof.

This follows from PEL type moduli interpretations of the canonical model
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
similar to Definition
10.3
.
∎

11.4.
The Hecke CM cycles on the integral model
\mathcal{M}

Fix a degree
n
monic polynomial
\alpha\in F[t]
that is conjugate self-reciprocal i.e.,

(11.8)

t^{n}\,\alpha(t^{-1})=\alpha(0)\overline{\alpha}(t).

Definition 11.7
.

For any
\mu_{G,\Delta}\in K_{G,\Delta}\backslash G(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}
, the
naive Hecke CM cycle

\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})\mathcal{M}

is the functor sending any locally noetherian scheme
S
over
O_{E}[1/\Delta]
to the groupoid of tuples
(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta},\varphi)
where

(1)

(A_{0},A,\overline{\eta})\in\mathcal{M}(S)
.

(2)

\varphi\in\End_{O_{F}}(A)[1/\Delta]
is a prime-to-
\Delta
endomorphism such that
\text{char}(\varphi)=\alpha\in F[t]
.

(3)

\varphi^{*}\lambda=\lambda
, and
\eta_{1}\varphi\eta_{2}^{-1}\in\mu_{G,\Delta}
for some
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\overline{\eta}
.

Definition 11.8
.

For such
\alpha\in F[t]
, let the Hecke CM cycle
CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
over
E
be the generic fiber of
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
.

Definition 11.9
.

The
Hecke correspondence

{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}

is the functor sending any locally noetherian scheme
S
over
O_{E}[1/\Delta]

to the groupoid of tuples
(A_{0},A_{1},\overline{\eta}_{A_{1}},A_{2},\overline{\eta}_{A_{2}},\varphi)
where

(1)

(A_{0},A_{1},\overline{\eta}_{A_{1}}),\,(A_{0},A_{2},\overline{\eta}_{A_{2}})\in\mathcal{M}(S)
.

(2)

\varphi\in\Hom_{O_{F}}(A_{1},A_{2})[1/\Delta]
is a prime-to-
\Delta
homomorphism such that
\varphi^{*}\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{1}
.

(3)

We have
\eta_{A_{2}}\varphi\eta_{A_{1}}^{-1}\in\mu_{G,\Delta}
for some
\eta_{A_{1}}\in\overline{\eta}_{A_{1}}
and
\eta_{A_{2}}\in\overline{\eta}_{A_{2}}
.

Proposition 11.10
.

The pullback of
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
along the diagonal morphism
\Delta:\mathcal{M}\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}
has the following decomposition as schemes over
O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
:

(11.9)

{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}|_{\mathcal{M}}=\coprod_{\alpha}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta}).

where the index
\alpha
runs over all degree
n
conjugate self-reciprocal monic polynomials
\alpha\in O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}][t]
(c.f.
11.8
).

Proof.

This follows from the moduli interpretation directly similar to
[
67
, Section 7.5]
.
∎

Assume that
\alpha\in F[t]
is irreducible. Then the map
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})\mathcal{M}
is finite and unramified as in
[
67
, Section 7.5]
. Hence
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
is proper over
O_{E}[1/\Delta]
by the assumption
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
.

11.5.
Derived Hecke CM cycles and blow up

Assume that
\alpha\in O_{F}[\Delta^{-1}][t]
is irreducible. Then
F_{\alpha}:=F[t]/\alpha(t)
is a CM number field. The involution on
F
extends to
F_{\alpha}
by sending
t
to
t^{-1}
. The subalgebra
F_{\alpha,0}
fixed by the involution on
F_{\alpha}
is a totally real field over
F_{0}
. We have an isomorphism
F_{\alpha}\cong F_{\alpha,0}\otimes_{F_{0}}F
.

Definition 11.11
.

The modified self product of
\mathcal{M}
is the blow up of
\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}
along union of Weil divisors

\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu}}\times_{k_{\nu}}\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu}}
for all finite places
\nu
of
E
over
v
such that
L_{v}
is not self-dual:

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}.

Proposition 11.12
.

The scheme
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}
is regular and of strictly semi-stable reduction over
O_{E}[1/\Delta]
.

Proof.

We have assumed that
E_{\nu}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. This follows from Theorem
10.8
and computations on completed local rings.
∎

Proposition 11.13
.

The Hecke correspondence
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
lifts naturally along
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}
via strict transforms.

Proof.

This is a global analog of Proposition
5.17
. We only put the level structure
\mu_{G,\Delta}
on
\Delta
, so the first projection
pr_{1}:{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}\mathcal{M}
is smooth and
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
is regular.

From moduli interpretations, the pullback of
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu}}\times_{k_{\nu}}\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu}}
to
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
is the preimage of
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu}}
under
pr_{1}
, hence a divisor in
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
. Due to regularity of
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
,
pr_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}^{\circ}_{k_{\nu}})
is a Cartier divisor hence the strict transform of
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
.
∎

In particular, we get a natural lifting of the diagonal morphism

(11.10)

\widetilde{\Delta}:\mathcal{M}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}}.

We now generalize the construction of derived CM cycles
[
41
, §5.1]
to our parahoric levels.

Definition 11.14
.

The
derived fixed point locus of the Hecke correspondence

{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
is the derived tensor product

{}^{\mathbb{L}}{\mathrm{Fix}}(\mu_{G,\Delta}):=\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[1/\Delta]}\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}},

viewed as an element in the
K
-group of coherent sheaves on
\mathcal{M}
with support on the image of
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
.
The
modified derived Hecke CM cycle

{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})\in K_{0}^{\prime}(\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta}))

is the restriction of
{}^{\mathbb{L}}{\mathrm{Fix}}(\mu_{G,\Delta})
to
K_{0}^{\prime}(\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{\Delta}))
under the decomposition (
11.9
).

By
[
67
, Apend. B.3]
and regularity of the modified self product of
\mathcal{M}
,
{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
is a virtual
1
-cycle in the sense that it lies in the subgroup
F_{1}K_{0}^{\prime}(\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta}))
generated by coherent sheaves supported on
1
-dimensional subschemes of
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
.

Remark 11.15
.

By the theory of complex multiplication, the generic fiber
CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
of
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
is
0
-dimensional over
E
. Moreover,
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
itself is
1
-dimensional away from finitely many primes. Hence
{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})_{E}=CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
by
[
67
, Apend. B.2]
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})
be the group of
1
-cycles on
\mathcal{M}
(with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficient), and
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})_{\sim}
be the quotient of
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})
by the subgroup generated by
1
-cycles on
\mathcal{M}
that are contained in a closed fiber and rationally trivial within that fiber. For a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
with at most
1
-dimensional support
C
. Let
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{k}
be the generic points of the
1
-dimensional irreducible components of
C
. Let
C_{i}
denote the closure of
\eta_{i}
with reduced scheme structure. Then we have a natural map

(11.11)

F_{1}K_{0}^{\prime}(\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta}))\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})_{\sim}:\,\,\mathcal{F}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\length_{\mathcal{O}_{C,\eta_{i}}}\mathcal{F}_{\eta_{i}}[C_{i}].

We still denote by
{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})_{\sim}
the image of
{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
under the above natural map (
11.11
).

Consider the space
\mathcal{S}(K_{G,\Delta}\backslash G(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta})
of bi-
K_{G,\Delta}
-invariant Schwartz functions on
G(F_{0,\Delta})
.

Definition 11.16
.

For
\phi_{CM,\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(K_{G,\Delta}\backslash G(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta})
, the
\phi_{CM,\Delta}
-averaged version of modified derived Hecke CM cycle is the finite summation

(11.12)

{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM})=\sum_{\mu_{G,\Delta}\in K_{G,\Delta}\backslash G(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}}\phi_{CM,\Delta}(\mu_{G,\Delta}){}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M})_{\sim}.

Here
\phi_{CM}:=\phi^{\Delta}_{CM}\otimes\phi_{CM,\Delta}
with
\phi^{\Delta}_{CM}:=1_{K_{G}^{\Delta}}
.

11.6.
Basic uniformization and local-global compatibility

In the rest of this section, we establish basic uniformizations of special cycles at inert places of
F_{0}
, see also
[
48
, Thm. 8.15]
. We show local-global compatibility under blow ups and relate (modified) local and global intersection numbers.

Let
\nu
be a finite place of
E
with
\nu|_{F}=w_{0}
,
\nu|_{F_{0}}=v_{0}
. Assume that
v_{0}\not\in\Delta
is inert in
F
. Recall that if
0<t_{v_{0}}<n
we assume that
v_{0}
is unramified over
p
.

Let
V^{(v_{0})}
be the nearby
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of
V
at
v_{0}
, which is positively definite at all
v|\infty
, and locally isomorphic to
V
at all finite places
v\not=v_{0}
of
F_{0}
.

Consider reductive groups
G^{(v_{0})}:=\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})})
over
F_{0}
and
\widetilde{G}^{(v_{0})}:=Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\times\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})})
over
\mathbb{Q}
. Consider the RSZ Shimura variety

\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E}[1/\Delta]

associated to
L
in §
10
. Denote by
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
the formal completion of
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
along the basic locus of the geometric fiber
\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{\nu}
. The formal scheme
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
is regular and
formally finite type
over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
.

Consider the (relative) unitary Rapoport–Zink space

\mathcal{N}\Spf O_{\breve{F}}

associated to
L_{v_{0}}
in §
5
. Note we use Kottwitz signature condition
(1,n-1)
in the Part
2
and
(n-1,1)
in Part
3
. In this section we identify our Rapoport–Zink spaces of signature
(1,n-1)
to
(n-1,1)
by chang the
O_{F_{v_{0}}}
action with the Galois conjugation in
\Gal(F_{v_{0}}/F_{0v_{0}})
. We have the following basic uniformization theorem.

Proposition 11.17
.

There is a natural isomorphism of formal schemes over
O_{\breve{E_{\nu}}}
:

(11.13)

\Theta:\,\,\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{G}^{(v_{0})}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K_{\widetilde{G}}^{p}),

where

\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\simeq(Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{p}\right)/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}},p})\times\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times\prod_{v|p,v\not=v_{0}}\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0,v})/K_{G,v}.

And there is a natural projection map

(11.14)

\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})

with fibers isomorphic to

\Theta_{0}:\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}].

Moreover, the isomorphism descends to
O_{E_{\nu}}
when we take the descent of
\mathcal{N}
to
\Spf O_{F_{v_{0}}}
as in Remark
5.2
.

Proof.

As
E_{\nu}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have the comparison between absolute and relative Rapoport–Zink spaces
[
6
, Prop. 3.30]
. The result follows from
[
6
, Thm. 4.3]
.
∎

Hence we denote by
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}
any fiber of
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
under the projection map (
11.14
).

11.7.
Basic uniformization for Kudla–Rapoport cycles

In Definition
11.3
, we defined Kudla–Rapoport cycles on
\mathcal{M}
for suitable
\phi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K_{G}}
(
i=1,2
). Denote by
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{i})_{0}^{\wedge}
any fiber of the formal completion
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{i})^{\wedge}
under the projection map (
11.14
).

Proposition 11.18
.

For any
\xi\in F_{0,+}
, we have

\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{1})_{0}^{\wedge}=\sum_{(u,g)\in G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash V^{(v_{0})}_{\xi}(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}}\phi_{1}^{v_{0}}(g^{-1}u)[\mathcal{Z}(u,g)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}},

\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{2})_{0}^{\wedge}=\sum_{(u,g)\in G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash V^{(v_{0})}_{\xi}(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}}\phi_{2}^{v_{0}}(g^{-1}u)[\mathcal{Y}(u,g)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}.

Here
[\mathcal{Z}(u,g)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}
is the descent of

\sum_{(u^{\prime},g^{\prime})\in G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0}).(u,g)\subseteq V^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}}\mathcal{Z}(u^{\prime})\times 1_{g^{\prime}K^{v_{0}}_{G}}

to
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}
. And
[\mathcal{Y}(u,g)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}
is the descent of
\sum\mathcal{Y}(u^{\prime})\times 1_{g^{\prime}K^{v_{0}}_{G}}
over the same index set.

Proof.

This follows from checking moduli definitions on both sides using the uniformization map
\Theta
constructed in
[
6
, Thm. 4.3]
.
∎

11.8.
Comparison of Balloon–Ground stratification

Return to the local set up in §
5.3
and consider

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}\times_{O_{\breve{F}_{v_{0}}}}\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{N}\times_{O_{\breve{F}_{v_{0}}}}\mathcal{N}

the formal blow up morphism along the Weil divisor

\mathcal{N}^{\circ}\times_{k_{v_{0}}}\mathcal{N}^{\circ}
.

Let
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}})^{\wedge}
be the completion of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}}
along the strict transform of the basic locus in the special fiber of
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}
. Let
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\times\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}})^{\wedge}
be any fiber of
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}})^{\wedge}
under the
2
-fold product of the projection map (
11.14
).

Proposition 11.19
.

The uniformization map

\Theta_{0}:\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}]

induces an isomorphism

(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\times\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}})^{\wedge}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\times G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0}))\backslash[\widetilde{\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}}\times(G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}})].

Proof.

Blow up commutes with flat base change
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}O_{E_{\nu}}
so we can work over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
. From Proposition
5.12
and the definition of
\Theta_{0}
,
the base change of the Balloon stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{E_{\nu}},0}^{\circ}
along the flat morphism

(11.15)

G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}\overset{\Theta^{-1}}{\cong}\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}},0}

is equal to the formal subscheme
G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}^{\circ}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}
. The result follows.
∎

11.9.
Basic uniformization for Hecke CM cycles

Let
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}^{\wedge}
