be the locally noetherian formal scheme over
O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}
obtained as pullback of
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}
along the map

\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times_{O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]}\mathcal{M}^{\wedge}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\mathcal{M}\times_{O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]}\mathcal{M}.

There is a natural projection

(11.16)

{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}.

Denote by
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta},0}^{\wedge}
any fiber of
{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}^{\wedge}
of the projection. Consider the discrete set

{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta},0}^{(v_{0})}=\{(g_{1},g_{2})\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}|g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}\in K_{G}\mu_{G,\Delta}K_{G}\}.

Proposition 11.20
.

We have an isomorphism:

{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta},0}^{\wedge}\cong G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times{\mathrm{Hk}}_{\mu_{G,\Delta},0}^{(v_{0})}]

compatible with the projection under the isomorphism
\Theta
(
11.13
).

Proof.

This follows from the proof of
[
67
, Prop. 7.16]
.
∎

For
(\delta,h)\in G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}
, consider the Hecke CM cycle

(11.17)

\mathcal{CM}(\delta,h)_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}:=[\mathcal{N}^{\delta=id}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times 1_{hK_{G}^{v_{0}}}]\rightarrow[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}].

The summation

\sum_{(\delta^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\mathcal{CM}(\delta,h)_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}

over the
G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})
-orbit of
(\delta,h)
in
G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}
descends to a cycle
[\mathcal{CM}(\delta,h)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}
on the quotient
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}
. Consider
\varphi_{CM}=\varphi_{CM}^{\Delta}\otimes\phi_{CM,\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}),K)
where
\varphi_{CM}^{\Delta}=1_{K_{G}^{\Delta}}
(including
v_{0}
). As in the proof of
[
67
, Prop. 7.17]
, we have

Corollary 11.21
.

The restriction of the formal completion
\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM})^{\wedge}
to any fiber of the projection
\Theta
(
11.13
) is the sum

(11.18)

\sum_{(\delta,h)\in G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash G^{(v_{0})}(\alpha)(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}}\phi_{CM}^{v_{0}}(h^{-1}\delta h)[\mathcal{CM}(\delta,h)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}.

Here
G^{(v_{0})}(\alpha)(F_{0})\subseteq G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})
consist of elements with characteristic polynomial
\alpha
.

Note
\mathcal{N}^{\delta=id}={\mathrm{Fix}}(\delta)
is introduced in Definition
5.18
. Replace
{\mathrm{Fix}}(\delta)
by
\widetilde{{\mathrm{Fix}}}^{\mathbb{L}}(\delta)
in the definition, we get a virtual
1
-cycle
[{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\delta,h)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}
. By Proposition
11.19
we get

Corollary 11.22
.

The restriction of the basic uniformization
{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM})^{\wedge}
to any fiber of the projection
\Theta
(
11.13
) is the sum

(11.19)

\sum_{(\delta,h)\in G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash G^{(v_{0})}(\alpha)(F_{0})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}}\phi_{CM}^{v_{0}}(h^{-1}\delta h)[{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\delta,h)]_{K_{G}^{v_{0}}}.

12.
Modification over
\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{F}_{q}

In this section, we use basic geometric results on the generic fiber and special fibers of our Shimura varieties to understand
1
-cycles on our integral model by modification. Firstly, we show that the degree function of any Hecke CM cycle
CM(\alpha,\mu)E
is equidistributed on geometric connected components of
M=M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
under mild assumptions. This shows we can use any
1
-cycle on
M
with non-empty complex points to modify general
1
-cycle to a cycle of degree zero over
\mathbb{C}
. Secondly, we use Balloon–Ground stratification and basic uniformization to approximate
1
-cycles on non-smooth special fibers by very special
1
-cycles in the basic locus.

12.1.
Modification over
\mathbb{C}

We firstly recall the Hecke and Galois actions on connected compoenents of complex Shimura varieties. Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum,
G^{\mathrm{ab}}=G/G^{\text{der}}
be the maximal abelian quotient of
G
which is a torus over
\mathbb{Q}
, and
Z
be the center of
G
.

For any neat compact open subgroup
K\leq G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, consider the complex uniformization:

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

Assume that
G^{\text{der}}
is simply connected.

Proposition 12.1
.

The natural projection
\det_{G}:GG^{\mathrm{ab}}
induces an isomorphism of finite sets

(12.1)

\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}))\cong G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{Q})^{\dagger}\backslash G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathrm{det}_{G}(K),

where
G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{Q})^{\dagger}=G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\Im(\det_{G}|_{Z}:Z(\mathbb{R})G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{R}))
.

Proof.

This follows from the strong approximation theorem for non-compact type, simply connected semisimple algebraic groups over number fields, see
[
43
, Thm. 5.17]
.
∎

Remark 12.2
.

The Hecke action of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on the tower
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\}_{K}
is compatible with the projection
\det_{G}:G(\mathbb{A})G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{A})
, hence is transitive on
\lim_{K}\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}))
.

Let
E
be the reflex field of
(G,X)
. The conjugacy class of the Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{h}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}G_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined on
E
. The composition

\det_{G}\circ\mu_{h}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}G^{\mathrm{ab}}_{\mathbb{C}}

is independent of the choice of
\mu_{h}
in the conjugacy class
\{\mu_{h}\}
and is defined over
E
. We denote by

(12.2)

r_{G,E}:(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{E}G^{\mathrm{ab}}_{E}

the descent of
\det_{G}\circ\mu_{h}
to
E
.

We have the norm map
\Nm_{E/\mathbb{Q}}:\Res_{E/\mathbb{Q}}(G^{\mathrm{ab}}_{E})G^{\mathrm{ab}}
(from
E
to
\mathbb{Q}
).
The reflex norm map
is defined as the composite map

(12.3)

r_{G}=\Nm_{E/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\Res_{E/\mathbb{Q}}r_{G,E}:\Res_{E/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\Res_{E/\mathbb{Q}}G^{\mathrm{ab}}G^{\mathrm{ab}}.

We describe the Galois action on
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}))
via the theory of Shimura reciprocity law as in
[
43
, Section 12-13]
. By global class field theory, there is a continuous surjective homomorphism
{\mathrm{Art}}_{E}:\Res_{E/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A})\Gal_{E}
. By
[
43
, p. 119]
and Proposition
12.1
, we have

Proposition 12.3
.

For any Galois automorphism
\sigma\in\Gal_{E}
, choose some
s=(s_{\infty},s_{f})\in\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}=\Res_{E/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A})
such that
{\mathrm{Art}}_{E}(s)=\sigma|_{E^{ab}}
such that
s_{\infty}
lies in the identity connected component of
(E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})^{\times}
. Then
\sigma
acts on the finite abelian group
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}))\cong G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{Q})^{\dagger}\backslash G^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\mathrm{det}_{G}(K)

via multiplication with
r_{G}(s_{f})
:

(12.4)

\sigma[t]=[r_{G}(s_{f}).t].

Remark 12.4
.

We can compute connected components of PEL type Shimura varieties using moduli interpretation. For the Siegel modular variety
\mathcal{A}_{g,N}
with level structure
\Gamma(N)
, we have
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{g,N}(\mathbb{C}))\cong(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
: given
(A,\lambda)\in\mathcal{A}_{g,N}(\mathbb{C})
, its image in
(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
is the ratio of a fixed perfect pairing on
(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{2g}
and the Weil pairing on
A[N]
under
\eta:A[N]\cong(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^{2g}
.

Return to our set up. We have
\widetilde{G}=Z^{\mathbb{Q}}\times\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G
where
G=\mathrm{U}(V)
is a reductive group over
F_{0}
. Denote by
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
(resp.
M_{G,K}
) the canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}(\widetilde{G},\{h_{\widetilde{G}}\})
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G,\{h_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}\})
) over the reflex field
E
(resp.
\varphi_{0}(F)\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
). We consider the abelianization

T^{1}:=(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G)^{\mathrm{ab}}=\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1}.

As
T^{1}(\mathbb{R})
is connected, we have
T^{1}(\mathbb{Q})^{\dagger}=T^{1}(\mathbb{Q})
. Consider the reflex norm map (
12.3
) for the Shimura datum
(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G,\{h_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}\})
:

r_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}:\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}T^{1}.

Lemma 12.5
.

The reflex norm map for the Shimura datum
(\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G,\{h_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}\})
could be identified with the map

(12.5)

r_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}:\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\Nm_{F/F_{0}}=1},\quad z\bar{z}/z.

Proof.

This is computed in
[
5
, Section 2.4-2.5]
via the equivalence between tori over a number field and
\mathbb{Z}
-lattices with actions of the Galois groups of the number field, given by sending a torus
T
to the cocharacter lattice
X_{*}(T_{\mathbb{C}})=\Hom({\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{\mathbb{C}},T_{\mathbb{C}})
.
∎

Corollary 12.6
.

The Galois action of
\Gal_{F}
on
\pi_{0}(M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C}))
is transitive. In other words,
M_{G,K}
is connected over
F
.

Proof.

By above lemma and Hilbert 90, the reflex norm map
r_{\Res_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}G}
induces a surjection
\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\twoheadrightarrow T^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. We conclude by Proposition
12.3
.
∎

12.2.
Complex uniformization of Hecke CM cycles

Denote by
X
the Grassmannian of negative definite
\mathbb{C}
-lines in
V\otimes_{F,\varphi_{0}}\mathbb{C}
. Consider any neat compact open subgroup
\widetilde{K}=K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\times K
.
We have the following complex uniformization
[
48
, Prop. 3.7]
of
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
along the complex embedding
E\mathbb{C}
induced by
\varphi_{0}
.

Proposition 12.7
.

There is an isomorphism between complex algebraic varieties over
\mathbb{C}
:

(12.6)

M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\otimes_{E,\varphi_{0}}\mathbb{C}\cong\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}}(Z^{\mathbb{Q}},\{h_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\})_{\mathbb{C}}\times M_{G,K}\otimes_{F,\varphi_{0}}{\mathbb{C}}.

Each fiber of the projection
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(\mathbb{C})Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
is isomorphic to

M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C})=\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})\backslash X\times\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}.

Let
\mu_{G,\Delta}\in K_{G,\Delta}\backslash G(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}
and
\alpha\in F[t]
be a degree
n
conjugate self-reciprocal monic polynomial (c.f.
11.8
). Assume
\alpha
is irreducible. For any
(\delta,h)\in\mathrm{U}(V)(\alpha)(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}
, the cycle
[CM(\alpha,h)]_{K_{G}}M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C})
is the descent of the summation

\sum_{(\delta^{\prime},h^{\prime})}X^{\delta^{\prime}=id}\times 1_{g^{\prime}K_{G}}

where
(\delta^{\prime},h^{\prime})
runs over the
G(F_{0})
-orbit of
(\delta,h)
. Similar to
[
67
, Prop. 7.17]
, the complex points of Hecke CM cycles have the following description.

Proposition 12.8
.

The restriction of
CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})(\mathbb{C})
on each fiber of the projection
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(\mathbb{C})Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
is the disjoint union

\coprod[CM(\alpha,h)]_{K_{G}}\rightarrow M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C})

where the index runs over the set

A_{\mu_{G,\Delta}}:=\{(\delta,h)\in\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})\backslash(\mathrm{U}(V)(\alpha)(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}|h^{-1}\delta h\in K_{G}\mu_{G,\Delta}K_{G}\}.

12.3.
Hecke CM cycles are Galois stable

Denote by
\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}}
the degree function of
C^{\prime}=CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
on

\pi_{0}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(\mathbb{C}))\cong Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}\times\pi_{0}(M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C})).

The Hecke action of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(\mathbb{C})
preserves
CM(\alpha,\mu)
by moduli interpretation, and induces a transitive action on
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Hence

\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}}=c\times\deg_{C^{\prime}}

where
c
is a constant and
\deg
is a function on
\pi_{0}(M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C}))
.

Note the degree function

\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}}
is
\Gal_{E}
-invariant because
CM(\alpha,\mu)
is defined over
\Spec E
.

Corollary 12.9
.

Assume that
E=F
. Then
\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}}
is a constant function on
\pi_{0}(M_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(\mathbb{C}))
.

Proof.

As
E=F
the degree function
\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}}=c\times\deg_{C^{\prime}}
is
\Gal_{F}
-invariant. By Corollary
12.6
,
\Gal_{F}
acts transitively on
\pi_{0}(M_{G,K})
, this shows
\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}}
is a constant function.
∎

Conjecture 12.10
.

The degree
\deg_{C^{\prime}}
is constant on
\pi_{0}(M_{G,K}(\mathbb{C}))
.

Remark 12.11
.

By Proposition
12.8
, the conjecture is equivalent to the image of the natural projection

\coprod_{(\delta,h)}X^{\delta=id}\times 1_{gK_{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0})\backslash X\times\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}

has constant degrees, where
(\delta,h)
runs over
\mathrm{U}(V)(\alpha)(F_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{G}
with
g^{-1}\delta g\in K_{G}\mu_{G,\Delta}K_{G}
.
Using the explicit description of Galois actions on special points, it might be possible to prove the conjecture for
E\not=F
by firstly showing the image of the projection could be descent into a cycle over the canonical model
M_{G,K}
over the reflex field
F
and then apply the Galois action.

Proposition 12.12
.

Assume that
E=F
. For any
0
-cycle
CM_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
over
E
stable under the Hecke action of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, there exists some
C^{\prime}=\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
such that

\widetilde{\deg}_{C}=a(\widetilde{\deg}_{C^{\prime}})

for some
a\in\mathbb{Q}
. Moreover, we can assume
\alpha
is maximal order at any finite place
v
of
F_{0}
outside
\Delta
, and is unramified at
v_{0}
.

Proof.

The degree function
\widetilde{\deg}_{C}
is
\Gal_{F}
-invariant and stable under Hekce action of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, hence also a constant. It suffices to show
C^{\prime}=\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
can be chosen to be non-empty. This follows from the complex uniformization (
12.8
) above.

∎

Remark 12.13
.

As Kudla–Rapoport divisors are invariant under Hecke action of
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the assumption that
C
is invariant under
Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is harmless for the application to our modularity results.

12.4.
Modification over
\mathbb{F}_{q}

Let
v\not\in\Delta
be a finite place of
F_{0}
such that
L_{v}
is not self-dual. So
L_{v}
is a vertex lattice of type
0<t_{v}\leq n
. The case
t_{v}=n
is same to the case
t_{v}=0
by duality, so assume
0<t_{v}<n
. By assumption,
v
is inert in
F
and
E_{\nu}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Hence
\mathcal{M}\Spec O_{E_{\nu}}
is of strictly semi-stable reduction by Theorem
10.8
.

For a
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
on the special fiber
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, consider its degree function on irreducible components
\mathcal{X}_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}

which is obtained via the intersection pairing
(-,-)_{\mathcal{M}}
between
\mathcal{X}_{i}
and
\mathcal{C}
inside the regular scheme
\mathcal{M}
. Recall the Balloon–Ground stratification in §
10.4
:

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}=\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}\cup\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}.

Note
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
are closed smooth subvarieties of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
over
k_{\nu}
of dimension
n-1
. So irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
are exactly connected components of the Balloon and Ground stratum:

\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}=\coprod_{i=1}^{a}\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}\cup\coprod_{j=1}^{b}\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\bullet}.

Consider the basic uniformization in Proposition
11.17
:

\Theta_{0}:\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}].

Here
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu},0}}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}
is any fiber of the projection
\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

Definition 12.14
.

Call a
1
-cycle
C^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\text{basic}}

very special
, if via basic uniformization it can be generated from the projection of a cycle in an embedding
\mathcal{N}_{L^{\flat}_{v}}\mathcal{N}_{L_{v}}
for a decomposition of vertex lattice
L_{v}=L^{\flat}_{v}\oplus M_{v}
where
L^{\flat}_{v}
has rank
2
and type
1
.

12.5.
The case
t_{v}=1
or
n-1

Proposition 12.15
.

If
t_{v}=1
, then
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
is contained in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
.

Proof.

This is
[
35
, Thm. 5.3.4]
, whose proof works over any prime that is unramfied in
F
. It is done by the computation of slopes of associated Dieudonne modules and by the construction of basic isogenies. Alternatively, as the formal Balloon strata
already has top dimension
n-1
if
t_{v}=1
via our Bruhat–Tits stratification (Theorem
7.11
), using the basic uniformization in (Proposition
11.17
) and that any connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
has a basic point (Proposition
12.19
), we see that
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
must lie in the basic locus.
∎

Proposition 12.16
.

Any irreducible component
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}
in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
is isomorphic to the
n-1
dimensional projective space
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}\cap\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}_{k_{\nu}}
is isomorphic to the degree
q+1
Fermat hypersurface in
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
is in the basic locus, we can use basic uniformization (Proposition
11.17
, which descends to
k_{\nu}
) to prove similar statements in the basic locus. Then we use explicit descriptions of Bruhat–Tits strata in Theorem
7.11
and Remark
7.12
. The irreducible components of
(\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}})^{\circ}
are exactly
\mathbb{T}(L^{\circ})\cong\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
where
L^{\circ}
is a vertex lattice of type
t_{v}-1=0
. The intersection of
\mathbb{T}(L^{\circ})
with
(\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}})^{\bullet}
is given by the condition
B^{\vee}\subseteq\varpi^{-1}B
which is the equation of Fermat hypersurface in
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
.
∎

Proposition 12.17
.

Assume
n\geq 3
. In any given connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, the Ground stratum is geometrically irreducible.

Proof.

Otherwise, we can write
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}
in this component as a disjoint union of two non-empty smooth varieties
X^{\bullet}
and
Y^{\bullet}
. For any
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}
in this component, we know
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}\cap\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}_{k_{\nu}}
is a Fermat hypersurface and in particular connected for
n-1\geq 2
. This shows either
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}\cap X^{\bullet}=\emptyset
or
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}\cap Y^{\bullet}=\emptyset
. This divides this connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
into two non-empty disjoint unions, a contradiction!
∎

Let
{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{M})
be the subgroup of the Chow group
{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})
generated by irreducible components
\mathcal{X}_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
.

Proposition 12.18
.

If
t_{v}=1
or
n-1
, then for any linear functional
\ell:{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}
, there exists a very special
1
-cycle
C^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\text{basic}}
such that

(\mathcal{X}_{i},C^{\prime})_{\mathcal{M}}=\ell(\mathcal{X}_{i})

for any irreducible component
\mathcal{X}_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. In particular, the statement applies to
\ell=(-,\mathcal{C})_{\mathcal{M}}
for any
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathcal{M}
.

Proof.

We can work in any connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, hence in some fiber
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu},0}}
. We only need to deal with the case
t_{v}=1
, and the case
t_{v}=n-1
follows by applying the dual isomorphism.

If
n=2
, then the Shimura curve
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
has Drinfeld uniformization the union of two families of projective lines and any
1
-cycle is in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\text{basic}}
very special. Any linear functional
\ell
can be regarded as a function on irreducible components of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. We are done by non-degeneracy of the intersection pairing (intersection matrix) between irreducible components of the curve
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
(inside the semi-stable proper scheme
\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}
).

Now assume that
n\geq 3
. By Proposition
12.17
, there is only one irreducible component
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{j_{0}}
in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu},0}^{\bullet}
, and possibly many irreducible components
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{i}
(
i=1,\ldots,r
) in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu},0}^{\circ}
. Then
\ell
is given by a collection of numbers
\{\ell_{j_{0}}=\ell(\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{j_{0}}),\ell_{1}=\ell(\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{1}),\ldots,\ell_{r}=\ell(\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{r})\}
such that
\ell_{j_{0}}=-\sum_{i=1}^{r}\ell_{i}.
It suffices to find for each
1\leq i\leq r
a very special cycle
C_{i}^{\prime}
such that

C_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}^{\circ}_{i},\quad(\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{j_{0}},C_{i}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{M}}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}.

Then
C^{\prime}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\ell_{i}}{(\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ},C_{i}^{\prime})}C^{\prime}_{i}
is what we want. To find such
C_{i}^{\prime}
, by applying basic uniformization we only need to find special
1
-cycle
C_{i}^{\prime}
in a given Bruhat-Tits stratum
\mathrm{BT}(L^{\circ})\cong\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
. Here
L^{\circ}
is a vertex lattice in
V^{(v_{0})}
of type
t_{v}-1=0
. Choose an orthogonal decomposition of vertex lattices

L^{\circ}_{v}=(L^{\circ}_{v})^{\flat}\oplus M_{v}

corresponding to an orthogonal decomposition
L_{v}=L_{v}^{\flat}\oplus M_{v}
such that
(L^{\circ}_{v})^{\flat}
is a self-dual lattice of rank
2
. Then we can choose

C^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{Z}(M_{v})=\mathrm{BT}(L^{\circ})^{\flat}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{L^{\flat}_{v}}.

∎

12.6.
The case
1<t_{v}<n-1

If
1<t_{v}<n-1
, the irreducible components
(\mathcal{N}^{\circ})^{\mathrm{red}}
and
(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet})^{\mathrm{red}}
are of dimension less than
n-1
by Theorem
7.11
. So the Balloon stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\circ}
and Ground stratum
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\bullet}
don’t lie in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
.

Proposition 12.19
.

For
0\leq t_{v}\leq n
, any geometric connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
contains a basic point
x\in\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
16
, Prop. 3.5.4]
, using the compatibility of the basic uniformization (
11.17
) and the complex uniformization of geometric connected components (
12.1
). Firstly, we can work on any fiber
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E_{\nu}}},0}
of the projection
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E_{v}}}}Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/K_{Z^{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

As the scheme
\mathcal{M}_{O_{\breve{E_{v}}},0}
is proper and flat over
O_{E_{\nu}}
with reduced special fiber, by Lemma
[
56
, Lemma 0E0N]
we have an identification of geometric connected components
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\overline{k_{\nu}},0})\cong\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\overline{E_{\nu}},0})
by specialization.
Recall by Proposition
12.1
we have

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\overline{E_{\nu}},0})=G^{\mathrm{ab}}(F_{0})\backslash G^{ab}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/\det(K_{G}).

The basic uniformization

\Theta_{0}:\mathcal{M}^{\wedge,\text{basic}}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}]

is compatible with the projection

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}^{\text{basic}}_{\overline{k_{\nu}},0})\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\overline{k_{\nu}},0})\cong\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\overline{E_{\nu}},0})=G^{\mathrm{ab}}(F_{0})\backslash G^{ab}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\det(K_{G}).

We are reduced to show the projection

G^{(v_{0})}(F_{0})\backslash[\mathcal{N}_{O_{\breve{E}_{\nu}}}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})/K_{G}^{v_{0}}]G^{\mathrm{ab}}(F_{0})\backslash G^{ab}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})/\det(K_{G})

is surjective, which follows from
[G^{(v_{0})}]^{\mathrm{ab}}=G^{\mathrm{ab}}=T^{1}
in our cases.
∎

Conjecture 12.20
.

For
1<t_{v}<n-1
, any geometric connected component of the Balloon (resp. Ground) stratum contains a basic point.

We make the following topological definitions.

Definition 12.21

(Good stratification)
.

Let
X
be a proper scheme over a field
k
. A stratification

X=\coprod_{i\in I}X_{i}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\overline{X_{i}}

indexed by a finite partial order set
I
is good, if

(1)

The closure relation
\overline{X_{i}}=\bigcup_{j\leq i}X_{j}
holds.

(2)

The stratum
X_{i}
is locally closed and equi-dimensional.

(3)

If
i<j
, the dimension of
X_{i}
is strictly less than
X_{j}
.

(4)

X_{i}
is quasi-affine.

For a good stratification on
X
, any irreducible component of
X
is an irreducible component of some
\overline{X_{i}}
(
i
maximal). By dimensional assumptions on
X_{i}
, this gives a bijection between the set of irreducible components of
X
and disjoint union of the set of irreducible components of
\overline{X_{i}}
for all maximal
i
. We have following topological lemmas.

Lemma 12.22
.

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a good stratification of
X
. If
Z
is a (non-empty)
connected component of
\overline{X_{i}}
for some
i
, then there exists a minimal element
j
in
I
such that
Z\cap X_{j}\not=\emptyset
and
\dim X_{j}=0
.

Proof.

There is a minimal
i^{\prime}\leq i
such that
Z\cap X_{i^{\prime}}\not=\emptyset
. The minimality implies
Z\cap X_{i^{\prime}}=Z\cap\overline{X_{i^{\prime}}}
, which is proper and quasi-affine hence finite. As
Z
is a connected component of
\overline{X_{i}}
,
Z\cap\overline{X_{i^{\prime}}}
is a
union of connected components of
X_{i^{\prime}}
. As
X_{i^{\prime}}
is equi-dimensional, we see that
\dim X_{i^{\prime}}=0
hence
i^{\prime}
is minimal in
I
. The result follows for
j=i^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 12.23
.

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a good stratification of
X
. Let
Y
be a closed subset of

X
. Fix
i\in I
. Set
\overline{Y_{i}}:=\overline{X_{i}}\cap Y
. Assume that for any connected component
Z
of
\overline{X_{i}}
,
Z\cap\overline{Y_{i}}\not=\emptyset
. If
\overline{Y_{i}}

is connected, then
\overline{X_{i}}
is connected. In particular, if
\overline{X_{i}}
is normal then

\overline{X_{i}}
is irreducible.

Proof.

Firstly, if
\overline{X_{i}}
is not connected hence a disjoint union of opens, then
\overline{Y_{i}}
is disjoint union of opens. The non-emptyness of theses opens comes from the assumption
Z\cap\overline{Y_{i}}\not=\emptyset
. A contradiction to that
\overline{Y_{i}}
is connected. Hence
\overline{X_{i}}
is connected.
∎

By
[
54
, Thm. C]
, the EKOR stratification on special fiber of abelian type Shimura varieties at parahoric level is good, if the level is Iwahori at
p
or the axiom
(4c)
of Rapoport–He
[
20
]
holds. The following proposition is an adaption of
[
16
, Thm. 3.7.1]
to our cases.

Proposition 12.24
.

In any geometric connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
, the Balloon and Ground stratum are geometrically irreducible.

Proof.

Using the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}
) as vanishing locus of
\Lie\lambda
(resp.
\Lie\lambda^{\vee}
) and the computation of local models, they correspond to different orbits of the parahoric group scheme on the local model. Therefore, our Balloon (resp. Ground) stratum is the closure of a maximal dimensional Kottwitz–Rapoport stratum hence the closure of a non-basic EKOR stratum
[
54
]
. We are reduced to prove the claim that closure of non-basic EKOR strata in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
are irreducible in each geometric connected component.

We consider the canonical integral model of unitary Shimura varieties
\mathcal{M}^{Iw}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}
by replacing the maximal parahoric level with the Iwahori level at
v
with PEL type modulis. Note we have the axiom
(4c)
of Rapoport–He
[
20
]
for our Shimura variety
\mathcal{M}^{Iw}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}\mathcal{M}_{O_{E_{\nu}}}
by basic uniformization (Proposition
11.17
) and
[
54
, Proposition A.4.5]
for PEL type Shimura varieties. The axiom 4 (c) implies that for every EKOR stratum at our parahoric level, there is
a EKOR stratum (i.e. Kottwitz–Rapoport stratum) at Iwahori level surjecting onto it. We are reduced to prove the claim that closure of each EKOR stratum is irreducible on any connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{Iw}
.

From now on, we consider the EKOR stratification on a given connected component of the proper scheme
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{Iw}
. We firstly recall that this is a good stratification in the sense of Definition
12.21
. Its strata are quasi-affine, equi-dimensional and have the closure relation in Definition
12.21
by
[
54
, Thm. C]
. Now choose a non–basic EKOR strata
X_{i}
. We need to show that
\overline{X_{i}}
is irreducible. We apply Lemma
12.23
with
Y
being the basic locus of (the given connected component of)
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{Iw}
, where the normality of
\overline{X_{i}}
is known by
[
54
, Thm. C]
. We need to check the non-empty condition in Lemma
12.23
: let
Z
be any connected component of
\overline{X_{i}}
. By Lemma
12.22
,
Z
must have non-empty intersection with a dimension
\leq 1
Kottwitz–Rapoport stratum
W
. By
[
12
, Prop 5.6]
, we see that
W
is in the basic locus hence
Z\cap\overline{Y_{i}}\not=\emptyset
. Finally, we need to check that
\overline{Y_{i}}
is connected. Via basic uniformization, we have connectedness of the basic locus and
\overline{Y_{i}}
by
[
16
, Thm. 3.7.1, Lem. 3.7.4]
(where our notation
\overline{Y_{i}}
agrees with
Y_{w}
in
loc. cit.
). By Lemma
12.23
(
Y
=the basic locus), we obtain the claim that
\overline{X_{i}}
is irreducible.
∎

Proposition 12.25
.

If
1<t_{v}<n-1
, then for any linear functional
\ell:{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}
, there exists a very special
1
-cycle
C^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}^{\text{basic}}
such that

(\mathcal{X}_{i},C^{\prime})_{\mathcal{M}}=\ell(\mathcal{X}_{i})

for any irreducible component
\mathcal{X}_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. In particular, the statement applies to
\ell=(-,\mathcal{C})_{\mathcal{M}}
for any
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathcal{M}
.

Proof.

We may work in a given connected component
X
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
. By Proposition
12.24
, there are only
2
irreducible components in this component i.e., the Balloon stratum
X^{\bullet}
and the Ground stratum
X^{\circ}
. So
X^{\bullet}+X^{\circ}
is principal. We only need to show there exists a very special
1
-cycle
C^{\prime}
such that

(X^{\bullet},C^{\prime})=-(X^{\circ},C^{\prime})\not=0
. Then
\frac{\ell(X^{\circ})}{(X^{\circ},C^{\prime})}C^{\prime}
is the
1
-cycle we want. By basic uniformization, any minimal dimensional Bruhat–Tits strata
\mathbb{P}^{n-t_{v}}
and
\mathbb{P}^{t_{v}}
with non-empty intersection will intersect at a point. Choose any
\mathbb{P}^{1}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{n-t_{v}}
passing exactly one such point will do the job.
∎

In fact, Conjecture
12.20
and Proposition
12.24
make sense for mod
p
fibers of general Shimura varieties (with suitable integral models). We make the following conjectures.

Conjecture 12.26
.

(1)

Any (geometric) irreducible component of the mod
p
fiber contains a basic point.

(2)

Closure of any (geometric) non-basic Kottwitz–Rapoport strata of the mod
p
fiber is irreducible in given connected component of the mod
p
fiber.

The first part
(1)
is closely related to the Hecke orbit conjecture for PEL type Shimura varieties
[
65
]
. Conditional on
[
65
, Prop. 4.2 (2)]
which is to be filled, a prime-to-
p
Hecke orbit is finite if and only if it contains basic points
[
65
, Prop. 4.8-4.9]
). The second part
(2)
is proved
[
16
, Thm. 3.7.1, Prop. 3.7.3]
for Hodge type Shimura varieties under certain quasi-split assumptions.

13.
Global modularity via modification

In this section, we introduce the modification method towards arithmetic modularity. We establish the modularity of arithmetic theta series at maximal parahoric levels when intersecting with (derived)
1
-cycles on
\mathcal{M}
, in particular with modified derived Hecke CM cycles.

Let
\mathcal{X}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
be a pure dimensional, regular, flat and proper scheme with smooth generic fiber
X
. Set the quotient
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space

\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}=\mathbb{R}/\text{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{\log\ell|\exists\nu\in\Delta,\,\nu|\ell\}.

13.1.
Truncated arithmetic intersection pairing

We recall basics of the Gillet–Soulé arithmetic intersection theory following
[
41
, §4.1]
and
[
9
]
.

We consider arithmetic divisors to relate local and global intersection numbers. An arithmetic divisor on
\mathcal{X}
is a finite
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combinations of tuples
(Z,g_{Z})
, where
Z
is a divisor on
\mathcal{X}
, and
g_{Z}=(g_{Z,\nu})_{\nu}
(
\nu
runs over all infinite places of
E
) is a tuple of Green functions on
X_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})\backslash Z_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})
with

dd^{c}g_{Z,\nu}+\delta_{Z_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})}=[\omega_{\nu}],\quad\text{$\omega_{\nu}$ a smooth $(1,1)$-form on $Z_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})$. }

Fix a Kähler metric on
X_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})
. The Green function
g_{Z,\nu}
is called
admissible
if
\omega_{v}
is harmonic with respect to the metric. Then the arithmetic Chow group
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{X})
(with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients)
is generated by arithmetic divisors, modulo the relation given by the
\mathbb{Q}
-span of principal arithmetic divisors i.e., tuples
\widehat{{\mathrm{div}}}(f):=({\mathrm{div}}(f),(-\log|f|_{\nu}^{2})_{\nu|\infty})
associated to rational functions
f\in E(\mathcal{X})^{\times}
:

\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{X}):=\{\text{Arithmetic divisors}\}/\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{(\text{div}(f),(-\text{log}|f|^{2}_{\nu})_{\nu|\infty})\}_{f\in E(\mathcal{X})^{\times}}.

An admissible Green function for a given divisor
Z
exists, and is unique up to adding locally constant functions on
X_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})
. Consider the admissible Chow group
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X})
as the subgroup of the arithmetic Chow group generated by tuples
(Z,(g_{Z,\nu})_{\nu})
with admissible Green functions
g_{Z,\nu}
. There is a natural map

(13.1)

\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X}){\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(X),\quad(Z,g_{Z})Z_{E}.

For any place
\nu
of
E
, define
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X})
as the subgroup generated by

•

(0,c_{\nu})
where
c_{\nu}
is a locally constant function on
X_{\nu}(\mathbb{C})
, if
\nu|\infty
.

•

(\mathcal{X}_{k_{\nu},i},0)
where and
\mathcal{X}_{k_{\nu},i}
is an irreducible component of the special fiber
\mathcal{X}_{k_{\nu}}
at
\nu
, if
\nu
is a finite place with residue field
k_{\nu}
.

Let
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X})
be the subgroup generated by
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{X})
for all places
\nu
of
E
, and
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{\infty}}(\mathcal{X}):=\sum_{\nu_{\infty}|\infty}\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\nu_{\infty}}(\mathcal{X})
.

Lemma 13.1
.

(1)

The projection
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X}){\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(X)
,
(Z,g_{Z})Z_{E}
induces an isomorphism
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X})/\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{X})\cong{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(X)
. Hence we have a short exact sequence:

0\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{X})\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X}){\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(X)0.

(2)

For any finite place
\nu
such that
\mathcal{X}_{\nu}:=\mathcal{X}\otimes O_{E,\nu}
is smooth over
O_{E,\nu}
, we have
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{\infty}}(\mathcal{X})
.

Proof.

The first part: by taking Zariski closure and using the existence of admissible Green function, we see the natural map
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,{\mathrm{adm}}}(\mathcal{X}){\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(X)
is surjective. Then we show the natural map has kernel
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\mathrm{Vert}}(\mathcal{X})
. If
[Z]_{E}=\emptyset
, then
Z
is a finite linear combination of arithmetic divisors supported on special fibers of
\mathcal{X}
. By adding elements in
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\nu}(\mathcal{X})
(
\mu
finite place of
E
), we may assume
Z=\emptyset
, then by admissibility of
g
, we see
g
must be a constant. This finishes the proof.

The second part: assume that
\mathcal{X}_{\nu}
is smooth over
O_{E,\nu}
. Then any
\mathcal{X}_{k_{\nu},i}
is smooth and is the reduction of a connect component
\mathcal{X}_{\nu,i}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\nu}
. The argument of
[
41
, Lemma 4.1]
applies. We use the Stein factorization
\mathcal{X}\Spec R\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]
where
R=H^{0}(\mathcal{X},O_{\mathcal{X}})
. The connected components of
\mathcal{X}_{\nu}
are in bijection with
|\Spec R/\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}|
. By finiteness of class number of
R
, we may find an element
a
in
R
whose divisor is supported on the prime ideal of
R
corresponding to
\mathcal{X}_{k_{\nu},i}
. As the arithmetic divisor
\widehat{{\mathrm{div}}}(a|_{\mathcal{X}_{\nu,i}})
is principal, we see that
(\mathcal{X}_{k_{\nu},i},0)\in\sum_{\nu_{\infty}|\infty}\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\nu_{\infty}}(\mathcal{X})
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{X})
be the group of
1
-cycles on
\mathcal{X}
(with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficient). Consider the
\Delta
-truncated version standard arithmetic intersection pairing
[
9
]

[

, Section 4.2]
between
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces:

(13.2)

(\cdot,\cdot):\widehat{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(\mathcal{X})\times\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{X})\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

Consider the subgroup
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{X})_{\deg=0}
consisting of
1
-cycles with zero degree on each connected component of
X
over
E
, which is the orthogonal complement of
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{X})
be the orthogonal complement of
\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}_{\text{Vert}}(\mathcal{X})
.

From Lemma
13.1
, the
\Delta
-truncated arithmetic intersection pairing (

) induces a pairing

(13.3)

(\cdot,\cdot)^{\text{adm}}:\,{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(X)\times\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{X})^{\perp}\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

13.2.
Green functions and arithmetic special divisors

Let
K
be a neat compact open subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
, and fix an embedding
\nu:E\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

Choose a Schwartz function
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K}
. Choose any compact open subgroup
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
such that
\phi
is invariant under
K_{0}
by the Weil representation.

For
\xi\in F_{0}
, we have two kinds of Green functions
[
41
, Section 3]
for the Kudla–Rapoport divisor
Z(\xi,\phi)_{\mathbb{C}}:=Z(\xi,\phi)\otimes_{E,\nu}\mathbb{C}
on
M_{G,K}\otimes_{E,\nu}\mathbb{C}
:

•

The
Kudla Green function

[
26
]

\mathcal{G}^{\bf K}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi)
with a variable
h_{\infty}\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\infty})
, which occurs in the analytic side naturally via computations of archimedean orbital integrals.

•

The
automorphic Green function

[
3
]

\mathcal{G}^{\bf B}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi)=\mathcal{G}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi)
for
\xi\in F_{0,+}
which is admissible by the work of Bruinier
[
3
, Cor. 5.16]
.

Consider the difference
\mathcal{G}^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{G}^{\bf K}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi)-\mathcal{G}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi),&\text{for }\xi\in F_{0,+},\\
\mathcal{G}^{\bf K}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi),&\text{else.}\end{cases}

We form the generating series of Green functions as the archimedean part of the generating series of arithmetic special divisors (
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
):

(13.4)

\mathcal{G}^{?}(h,\phi):=\sum_{\xi\in F_{0}}\mathcal{G}^{?}(\xi,h_{\infty},\omega(h_{f})\phi)W^{(n)}_{\xi}(h_{\infty}),\quad?=\bf K,\bf B,\bf K-\bf B.

Theorem 13.2
.

The generating function
\mathcal{G}^{\bf K-\bf B}(h,\phi)
when evaluating at a degree zero cycle on
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}(\mathbb{C})
, is a smooth function on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
, invariant under left
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
-action and right
K_{0}
-action, and of parallel weight
n
.

Proof.

See
[
41
, Thm. 3.13]
.
∎

Definition 13.3
.

[Hodge bundle]
We take
\omega_{\mathcal{M}}=(\Lie A_{0})\otimes(\Lie A)_{1}^{\vee}
as in
[
4
, Section 7.2]
as an extension of the automorphic line bundle
\omega
to
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}
. Define the arithmetic line bundle
\widehat{\omega}=(\omega_{\mathcal{M}},\lVert-\rVert_{\mathrm{Pet}})
endowed with the natural Petersson metric.

Define constant terms

(13.5)

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{B}(0,\phi):=-\phi(0)c_{1}(\widehat{\omega})\in\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}),

(13.6)

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{K}(0,h_{\infty},\phi)=-\phi(0)c_{1}(\widehat{\omega})+(0,(\mathcal{G}^{\bf K}_{\nu}(\xi,h_{\nu},\phi))_{\nu})\in\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}).

Set
\mathcal{G}^{\bf K}(\xi,\phi):=\mathcal{G}^{\bf K}(\xi,1,\phi)
. For
\xi\in F_{0}
, define
arithmetic Kudla–Rapoport divisors
(
?=\bf K,\bf B,\bf K-\bf B
)

(13.7)

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{?}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi)=(\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi),(\mathcal{G}^{?}_{\nu}(\xi,h_{\nu},\phi))_{\nu})\in\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}),

(13.8)

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{?}(\xi,\phi)=(\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi),(\mathcal{G}^{?}_{\nu}(\xi,\phi))_{\nu})\in\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}).

We have that
\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(0,\phi),\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi)\in\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1,\text{adm}}(\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}})
.

13.3.
Modularity of arithmetic theta series at maximal parahoric levels

Return to the set up in §

, we have the integral model (recall

)

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}].

The level

is related to a chosen lattice

is a vertex lattice of type

.

Theorem 13.4
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K}
be a Schwartz function such that
\phi
is invariant under a compact open subgroup
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})

\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{R}_{\Delta,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Moreover, the same is true if we replace
(-,C^{\perp})
by any linear functional
\ell:{\mathrm{Ch}}^{1}(\mathcal{M})\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}
that is trivial on

.

Proof.

This follows from by applying Lemma

to the regular scheme

and Proposition

.
∎

Let

\phi=\phi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K}

be of the form
\phi_{i}

(i=1,2)
introduced in §

. Then
\phi
is invariant under a compact open
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
such that for

we have

if

is self dual;

and

is not self dual;

if

and

is a lower triangular congruence subgroup if

. Consider the generating series

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi)=\sum_{\xi\in F_{0}}\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi)W_{\xi}^{(n)}(h_{\infty}).

Theorem 13.5
.

Assume
E=F
and
F_{0}\not=\mathbb{Q}
.

(1)

Assume that
\Delta
is large enough so that there exists a non-empty Hecke CM cycle

(c.f. (

) ), where

-cycle
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{M}
over

of level

.

(2)

let

be a linear functional that has degree

over
\mathbb{C}
, i.e.

over

, i.e.

of parallel weight
n
of level

.

Proof.

Firstly, we know the arithmetic modularity for specific
1
-cycles
\mathcal{C}
of the following two forms:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
is a very special
1
-cycle in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
where
\nu
is a place of
E
over an inert place
v_{0}
of
F_{0}
. This is done by Theorem
13.9
.

(2)

(Only needed for part
(1)
)
\mathcal{C}
is the normal integral model of a Hecke CM cycle
C=CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
. We use the set up in §
14
. We choose a standard partial transfer
\Phi^{\prime}
(Definition
14.7
) in
\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V^{\prime})(\mathbb{A}_{0}))
of the standard function

\Phi=\Phi_{\Delta}\otimes\prod_{v\not\in\Delta}(1_{U(L_{v})}\otimes 1_{L_{v}}),\,\,\Phi_{\Delta}:=\phi_{\Delta}\otimes 1_{\mu_{0}}.

For
\xi\in F_{0}^{\times}
, by Proposition
14.8
and the proof in Theorem

we have the matching

2\partial_{\alpha}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi,\Phi)=-(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi),\mathcal{C}).

Note by known ATCs for unramified maximal orders in §
9
, the AFL for maximal orders in
[
40
, Cor. 9.1]
the assumption in Proposition
14.8
holds.
Then the modularity follows from the modularity of the automorphic generating function
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h_{\infty},\Phi^{\prime})
known by the Poisson summation formula and modularity of
\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(h_{\infty},\Phi_{f})
known by
[
3
]
.

Now we consider a general
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}
. From the modifications over
\mathbb{C}
(Proposition

which uses

) and

(Proposition

and Proposition

), we know there exists a

that is a finite linear combination of specific

follows from
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
