∎

Remark 13.6
.

We may use complex uniformization (
12.8
) to pin down the condition that a Hecke CM cycle
CM(\alpha,\mu_{G,\Delta})
is non-empty. Starting with an unramified maximal order
\alpha_{v_{0}}\in F_{v_{0}}[t]
, We may enlarge
\Delta
to make sure the condition holds.

13.4.
Modularity for very special
1
-cycles in basic locus

Assume
\mathcal{C}
is a very special
1
-cycle in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
where
\nu
is a place of
E
over an inert place
v_{0}
of
F_{0}
.

To show
(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi),\mathcal{C})
is modular, we use basic uniformization and local modularity results (Section
8
). In the end, we will find
(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi),\mathcal{C})
can be adelized into a theta series for suitable Schwartz function
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(V^{(v_{0})}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))
in the nearby hermitian space of
V
at
v_{0}
, hence is modular. Without loss of generality, we can assume
\mathcal{C}
is of the form

\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{0}\times 1_{g_{0}K^{v_{0}}}\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu},0}

for some
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{red}}
and
g_{0}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{(v_{0})})/K^{v_{0}}
. Note the functions
(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathcal{C}_{0})
and
(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathcal{C}_{0})
(
u\in V^{(v_{0})}(F_{0,v_{0}})-0
) extend to smooth functions on
V^{(v_{0})}(F_{0,v_{0}})
by Theorem
8.1
under the assumption
\mathcal{C}
is a very special
1
-cycle. So we have a Schwartz function
\phi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathcal{S}(V^{(v_{0})}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))
defined by

\phi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C},v}(u):=\begin{cases}\phi_{v}(g_{0}^{-1}u)&\text{if $v\not=v_{0}$},\\
(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathcal{C}_{0})&\text{if $v=v_{0},\,i=1$},\\
(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathcal{C}_{0})&\text{if $v=v_{0},\,i=2$}.\\
\end{cases}

13.5.
Constant terms

Recall that
\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{B}(0,\phi)=-\phi(0)c_{1}(\widehat{\omega})
. By moduli interpretation, the pullback of the Hodge bundle
\widehat{\omega}
(
13.3
) to the basic locus is the Hodge bundle
\omega=(\Lie X)_{0}^{\vee}
. We need to show

-\phi(0)\deg(\omega|_{\mathcal{C}})=\phi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}(0).

Identifying the prime-to-
v_{0}
part, we reduce this to the following proposition.

Proposition 13.7
.

For a very special
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{L_{v_{0}}}
, we have

-\deg\omega|_{\mathcal{C}}=\lim_{u0}(\mathcal{Z}(u),\mathcal{C}_{0}),\quad q\deg\omega|_{\mathcal{C}}=\lim_{u0}(\mathcal{Y}(u),\mathcal{C}_{0}).

Proof.

As
\mathcal{C}
is very special, via pullback we only need to do the case
n=2
and
t_{v}=1
i.e., the unitary Shimura curve case with Drinfeld uniformization at
v_{0}
. We use computations in Theorem
8.4
. Consider the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L^{\circ}}=\mathbb{P}(L^{\circ}/\varpi L^{\circ})
for a type
0
lattice
L^{\circ}
. Then by Theorem
8.7
, the right hand side is
1
for
\mathcal{Z}
-cycles and
-q
for
\mathcal{Y}
-cycles. So we are reduced to show

\deg\omega|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L^{\circ}}}=-1.

This is true by relative Dieudonne theory: by equation
7.17
, a special pair
(A,B)
(recall
A=M_{0},B=M_{1}^{\perp},B^{\vee}=\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{X}}M_{1}
) determines a point
x=\varpi B/\varpi A^{\vee}
in
\mathbb{P}(L^{\circ}/\varpi(L^{\circ})^{\vee})
if
A=L^{\circ}
. The line bundle
(\Lie X)_{0}
at
x
gives the line
M_{0}/\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{X}}M_{1}=A/\sigma^{-1}(B)\overset{\sigma}{\cong}A^{\vee}/B
in
\mathbb{P}(L^{\circ}/\varpi(L^{\circ})^{\vee})
. So
\omega
is the tautological line bundle
O(-1)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L^{\circ}}
, hence has degree
-1
. The case
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{L^{\bullet}}
for a type
2
lattice
L^{\bullet}
is similar.
∎

Remark 13.8
.

See
[
32
, Section 11.1]
for similar computations on orthogonal Shimura curves.

Theorem 13.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a very special
1
-cycle in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu}}
where
\nu
is a place of
E
over an inert place
v_{0}
of
F_{0}
. Then we have an equality

(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi),\mathcal{C})=\phi_{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}(0)+\sum_{\xi\in F_{0,+}}\sum_{u\in V^{(v_{0})}_{\xi}(F_{0})}\phi_{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}(u)W_{\xi}^{(n)}(h_{\infty}).

Hence
(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi),\mathcal{C})
is the
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient of the theta series
\Theta(h,\phi_{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})

for
\phi_{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
on
V^{(v_{0})}
. In particular,
(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi),\mathcal{C})
is modular.

Proof.

The non-constant term follows from basic uniformization in Proposition
11.18
directly. The constant term is done by Proposition
13.7
.
∎

Remark 13.10
.

For
n=2,t=1
, basic uniformization is used in
[
28
, Section 4.3]
to show the modularity of their arithmetic theta series on orthogonal Shimura curves when intersecting with vertical projective lines
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We will use the local duality between
\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Y}
cycles in Theorem
8.1
later to relate
(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi_{1}),\mathcal{C})
and
(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(h_{\infty},\phi_{2}),\mathcal{C})
.

Part IV
The proof of ATC for
p>2

Let
p
be an odd prime. Assuming
F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is unramified if
0<t<n
, now we prove arithmetic transfer Conjectures
6.9
and
6.4
.

By equivalences in Theorem
6.15
, we only need to prove the semi-Lie version ATC in part
(1)
of Conjecture
6.4
for any vertex lattices.

The strategy is local-global via a double induction:

•

We globalize the data and produce a pair of automophic forms (using modularity) on both geometric side and analytic side.

•

Non-singular Fourier coefficients of geometric and analytic side (away from
\Delta
) with “boundary valuations” at the distinguished finite place
v_{0}
can be matched by Theorem
4.5
and previous local reductions and inductions.

•

By double induction Lemma
15.1
, we obtain global identities for all Fourier coefficients.

•

We deduce local identities at
v_{0}
and finally the desired local equalities by subtracting local terms in the modification.

14.
The global analytic and geometric side

In this section, we construct the global analytic side and geometric side in the semi-Lie setting at maximal parahoric levels, following
[
67
, §11-15]
. We use the set up in §
10
:

•

F/F_{0}
is a totally imaginary quadratic extension of a totally real number field. Choose a CM type
\Phi
of
F
and
uished element
\varphi_{0}\in\Phi
.

•

V
is a
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space of dimension
n\geq 1
of signature
\{(n-1,1)_{\varphi_{0}},(n,0)_{\varphi\in\Phi-\{\varphi_{0}\}}\}
.

•

Fix a finite place
v_{0}
of
F_{0}
that is inert in
F
, with residue characteristic
p>2
. Fix an integer
0\leq t_{0}\leq n
.

•

\Delta
is a finite collection of places of
F_{0}
. Assume that
p\nmid\Delta
, i.e. all places
v
of
F_{0}
above
p
are not in
\Delta
, and that all places
v\not\in\Delta
is unramified in
F
.

•

L
is a hermitian lattice satisfying assumptions in §
10.2
. Assume that
L
is self–dual away form
\Delta
and
v_{0}
, and
L_{v_{0}}
is a vertex lattice of type
t_{0}
.

Choose a level structure
\widetilde{K}
as in (
10.6
) for
L
and
\Delta
. We have the integral model (
10.3
)

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}].

of the RSZ unitary Shimura variety for the hermitian space
V
with level
\widetilde{K}
.

Fix a degree
n
conjugate self-reciprocal (
11.8
) monic polynomial
\alpha\in F[t]
. Assume that
\alpha
is irreducible. Then
F^{\prime}:=F[t]/(\alpha(t))
is a field with a Galois involution on
F^{\prime}
extending the non-trivial Galois involution of
F/F_{0}
and sending
t
to
t^{-1}
. Denote by
F_{0}^{\prime}
the fixed subfield of
F^{\prime}
under this involution.

14.1.
Analytic generating functions and derivatives at
s=0

We firstly globalize the analytic side in §
2
based on
[
66
, §12]
. Fix an orthogonal
F
-basis of
V
to endow it with a
F_{0}
-rational structure
V_{0}
and a
F/F_{0}
semi-linear involution
\overline{(-)}
with fixed subspace
V_{0}
. Consider the symmetric space

(14.1)

S(V_{0})=\{\gamma\in\mathrm{GL}(V)|\gamma\overline{\gamma}=id\}

and the natural
(F_{0}\times F_{0})/F_{0}
-hermitian space

V^{\prime}=V_{0}\times(V_{0})^{*}.

Let
\eta:\mathbb{A}_{0}^{\times}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the quadratic character associated to
F/F_{0}
by global class field theory. Consider the subscheme

S(V_{0})(\alpha)\subseteq S(V_{0})

of elements with characteristic polynomial
\alpha
. Then
S(V_{0})(\alpha)(F_{0})
consists of exactly one
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(F_{0})
-orbit.

Definition 14.1
.

A decomposable function
\Phi^{\prime}=\otimes_{v}\Phi^{\prime}_{v}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V^{\prime})(\mathbb{A}_{0}))
is called Gaussian, if for every place
v|\infty
of
F_{0}
,
\Phi^{\prime}_{v}
is the chosen partial Gaussian test function (relative to
\alpha
) in
[
67
, Section 12.4]
, which partially transfers to the Gaussian function
e^{-\pi(u,u)}
on
(g,u)\in\mathrm{U}(n)(\mathbb{R})\times\mathbb{C}^{n}
.

Choose a Gaussian decomposable function
\Phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V^{\prime})(\mathbb{A}_{0}))
. Consider the action of
h\in\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})
on
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in S(V_{0})\times V^{\prime}
by

(14.2)

h.(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})=(h^{-1}\gamma h,h^{-1}u_{1},u_{2}h).

Consider a pair
(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})
that is regular semisimple, which is equivalent to
u^{\prime}\not=0
as
\alpha
is irreducible. Define the global orbital integral (
v
runs over all places of
F_{0}
,
s\in\mathbb{C}
):

(14.3)

\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime},s)=\prod_{v}\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v},s)

Denote by
[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})]
(resp.
[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}
) the set of (resp. regular semisimple)
\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(F_{0})
-orbits in
(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})
. Set the adelic quotient
[\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})]:=\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(F_{0})\backslash\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})(\mathbb{A}_{0})
.

Let
\omega
be the Weil representation of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})\times\mathrm{U}(V)
on
V(\mathbb{A}_{0})
as in
[
67
, Section 11.1]
. For
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
, consider the regularized integral (
s\in\mathbb{C}
):

(14.4)

\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s)=\int_{g\in[\mathrm{GL}(V_{0})]}\left(\sum_{(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})}\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime}(g^{-1}.(\gamma,u^{\prime}))\right)|g|^{s}\eta(g)dg.

By
[
67
, Thm. 12.14]
, we see that
\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s)
is a smooth function of
(h,s)
, entire in
s\in\mathbb{C}
and is left invariant under
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
by Poisson summation formula.

We have a decomposition:

(14.5)

\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s)=\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s)_{0}+\sum_{(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}}\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime},s),

where
\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s)_{0}
is the term over the two regular nilpotent orbits as in
[
67
, Section 12.6]
.

For
\xi\in F_{0}
, let
\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi,h,\Phi^{\prime},s)
be the
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient (
1.4
) of
\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\cdot,\Phi^{\prime},s)
. If
\xi\not=0
, we have

(14.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi,h,\Phi^{\prime},s)=\sum_{(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime}_{\xi})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}}\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime},s).

Here
V^{\prime}_{\xi}
is the subscheme of
(u_{1},u_{2})\in V^{\prime}
defined by the condition
u_{2}(u_{1})=\xi
. Consider the derived functions and orbital integrals:

(14.7)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime}):=\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s).

(14.8)

\operatorname{\partial Orb}((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v}):=\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v},s).

By similar properties of
\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime},s)
,
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime})
is a smooth function on
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
, left invariant under
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})
, and of parallel weight
n
. If
\Phi^{\prime}
is
K_{0}
-invariant under the Weil representation, then
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime})
is right
K_{0}
-invariant.

By Leibniz’s rule, we have the standard decomposition (
v
runs over all places of
F_{0}
):

(14.9)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime})=\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime})_{0}+\sum_{v}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime}),

where

(14.10)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime}):=\sum_{(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}}\operatorname{\partial Orb}((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime}_{v})\cdot\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime v}).

and the nilpotent term
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime})_{0}
is part of the
0
-th Fourier coefficient of
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime})
. We have the Fourier expansion

(14.11)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime})=\sum_{\xi\in F_{0}}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,h,\Phi^{\prime}).

For
\xi\in F_{0}^{\times}
, we have

(14.12)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,h,\Phi^{\prime}):=\sum_{(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime}_{\xi})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}}\operatorname{\partial Orb}((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime}_{v})\cdot\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\omega(h)\Phi^{\prime v}).

As
\Phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V^{\prime})(\mathbb{A}_{0}))
, for all but finitely many finite place
w
of
F_{0}
, we know that
\Phi^{\prime}_{w}
is the standard function (
1.2
) for the self-dual lattice
L_{0,w}\subseteq V_{0,w}
(spanned by the chosen orthogonal basis of
V
):

\Phi^{\prime}_{w}=1_{S(L_{0,w})}\times L_{0,w}\times(L_{0,w})^{*}.

14.2.
Comparison to intersection numbers

Let
V
be the
n
-dimensional hermitian space we start with. Consider a decomposable function
\Phi=\otimes_{v<\infty}\Phi_{v}\in\mathcal{S}((\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))
.

Definition 14.2
.

We say a decomposition function
\Phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}((S(V_{0})\times V^{\prime})(\mathbb{A}_{0}))
is a partial transfer (relative to
\alpha
) of
\Phi
, if
\Phi^{\prime}
is Gaussian (
14.1
) and for any finite place
v
of
F_{0}
, equalities for
\Phi_{v}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{v}
in Definition
2.2
are required to hold only for
(\gamma,u_{1},u_{2})\in(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V_{0}\times V_{0}^{*})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
.

Choose a partial transfer
\Phi^{\prime}
of
\Phi
, which exists by the existence of smooth Jacquet–Rallis transfers
[
68
]
. Let
v
be a place of
F_{0}
. We hope to compare
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime})
(
14.10
) and
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,h,\Phi^{\prime})
(
14.12
) on the analytic side with relevant geometric terms for
\Phi
.

Proposition 14.3
.

If
v
splits in
F
, then
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime})=0
.

Proof.

This follows the argument in
[
67
, Lem. 14.2]
(see also
[
66
, Prop. 3.6]
for the group case). ∎

If
v
is a non-split place (including the case
v|\infty
), consider the
v
-nearby hermitian space
V^{(v)}
of
V
. As
\Phi^{\prime}
is a partial transfer of
\Phi
, the
(\gamma,u^{\prime})
-term in the summation (
14.10
) of
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime})
is
0
, unless
(\gamma,u^{\prime})
matches an orbit
(g,u)\in[(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v)})\times V^{(v)})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}
.

Let
v
be a finite non-split place. For
h=h_{\infty}h_{f}\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{(}\mathbb{F}_{0,\infty}){\mathrm{SL}}_{(}\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
, as
\Phi^{\prime}
is Gaussian the decomposition (
14.11
) becomes

(14.13)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(h,\Phi^{\prime})=\sum_{\xi\in F_{0},\xi\geq 0}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,1,\omega(h_{f})\Phi^{\prime})\frac{W_{\xi}^{(n)}(h_{\infty})}{W_{\xi}^{(n)}(1)}.

Here
W_{\xi}^{(n)}(-)
is the product of standard weight
n
Whittaker functions (
1.3
) over infinite places
v|\infty
of
F_{0}
.
We normalize the value at
h=1
by

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{W^{(n)}_{\xi}(1)}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,1,\Phi^{\prime}).

By the computation of archimedean orbital integrals
[
67
, §12]
, we have the following decomposition

Proposition 14.4
.

(14.14)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime})=\sum_{(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime}_{\xi})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}}\operatorname{\partial Orb}((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v})\cdot\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime v,\infty}).

The form (
14.14
) on the analytic side could be compared with the geometric side (see Proposition
14.6
below). Recall
K
is the level associated to
L
and
\Delta
. Choose
\Phi
to be of the standard form
\Phi_{i}=\phi_{CM}\otimes\phi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}((\mathrm{U}(V)\times V)(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}))^{K}
for
i=1,2
, where

(1)

For
v\notin\Delta
and
v\not=v_{0}
, we have
\Phi_{i,v}=1_{\mathrm{U}(L_{v})}\otimes 1_{L_{v}}
.

(2)

At
v_{0}
, we have
\Phi_{1v_{0}}=1_{\mathrm{U}(L_{v_{0}})}\otimes 1_{L_{v_{0}}}
and
\Phi_{2v_{0}}=1_{\mathrm{U}(L_{v_{0}})}\otimes 1_{L_{v_{0}}^{\vee}}
.

We have introduced Kudla–Rapoport divisors (
11.6
) and derived Hecke CM cycles (
11.16
). Moreover, we have introduced relevant Green functions in §
13.2
and the
arithmetic Kudla–Rapoport divisors
(
13.7
) for
\xi\in F_{0}
and
?=\bf K,\bf B,\bf K-\bf B
:

(14.15)

\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{?}(\xi,h_{\infty},\phi_{i})=(\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi),(\mathcal{G}^{?}_{\nu}(\xi,h_{\nu},\phi_{i}))_{\nu})\in\widehat{{\mathrm{Ch}}}^{1}(\mathcal{M}).

Recall the volume factor
\tau(Z^{\mathbb{Q}})=\#Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z^{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. For
\xi\in F_{0}
, we define
global arithmetic intersection numbers

(14.16)

\Int^{?}(\xi,\Phi_{i}):=\frac{1}{\tau(Z^{\mathbb{Q}})[E:F]}(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{?}(\xi,\phi_{i}),{}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM}))\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

We set
\Int(\xi,\Phi_{i})=\Int^{\bf B}(\xi,\Phi_{i})
.

If
\xi\not=0
, we may lift
\Int(\xi,\Phi_{i})
to
\mathbb{R}
using the physical divisor
\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi_{i})
.

Theorem 14.5
.

Assume that
\xi\neq 0
, consider a place
\nu
of
E
above a finite place
v
of
F_{0}
. Consider the support of
\mathcal{Z}(\xi,\phi_{i})\cap\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM})
.

(i)

The support does not meet the generic fiber
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{O_{E}}E
.

(ii)

If
v
is split in
F
, then the support does not meet the special fiber

\mathcal{M}\otimes_{O_{E}}k_{\nu}
.

(iii)

If
v
is inert in
F
, then the support meets the special fiber

\mathcal{M}\otimes_{O_{E}}k_{\nu}
only in its basic locus.

Proof.

The proof of
[
67
, Theroem 9.2]
can be applied directly to our levels.
∎

Hence
\Int(\xi,\Phi)

(\Phi=\Phi_{i})
are well-defined, and can be decomposed into a sum of archimedean contributions and non-archimedean contributions away from
\Delta
in
\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}
(
v
means a place of
F_{0}
.):

(14.17)

\Int(\xi,\Phi_{f})=\sum_{v|\infty}\Int_{v}^{\bf B}(\xi,\Phi_{f})+\sum_{v\in\mathrm{Inert}(F/F_{0})-\Delta}\Int_{v}(\xi,\Phi_{f})\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

Proposition 14.6
.

Assume
\xi\neq 0
and
v\not\in\Delta
.

(1)

If
v
is inert in
F
and
v\not=v_{0}
, then

(14.18)

\Int_{v}(\xi,\Phi_{i})=2\log q_{v}\sum_{(g,u)\in[(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v)})(\alpha)\times V^{(v)}_{\xi})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}}\Int_{v}(g,u)\cdot\Orb\left((g,u),\Phi^{v}_{i}\right).

Here
\Int_{v}(g,u)
is the local quantity defined in the AFL (i.e., Conjecture
6.4
) for rank
n
self-dual lattices) with respect to the quadratic extension
F_{v}/F_{0v}
.

(2)

If
v=v_{0}
, then

(14.19)

\Int_{v_{0}}(\xi,\Phi_{i})=2\log q_{v_{0}}\sum_{(g,u)\in[(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})})(\alpha)\times V^{(v_{0})}_{\xi})(F_{0})]}\Int_{v_{0}}^{i}(g,u)\cdot\Orb\left((g,u),\Phi^{v_{0}}_{i}\right).

Here
\Int_{v_{0}}^{1}(g,u)
(resp.
\Int_{v_{0}}^{2}(g,u)
) is the local quantity defined in Part
(1)
(resp.
(2)
) of Conjecture
6.4
for rank
n
and type
t_{0}
vertex lattice with respect to the quadratic extension
F_{v_{0}}/F_{0v_{0}}
.

(3)

If
v|\infty
, then

(14.20)

\Int_{v}^{\bf K}(\xi,\Phi_{i})=\sum_{(g,u)\in[(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v)})(\alpha)\times V^{(v)}_{\xi})(F_{0})]}\Int_{v}(g,u)\cdot\Orb\left((g,u),\Phi_{i}\right).

Here
\Int_{v}(g,u)
is defined as the value
\Int_{v}(g,u)=\,{\bf\mathcal{G}}^{\bf K}(u,h_{\infty})(z_{g})
where
z_{g}
is the unique fixed point of
g
on the Grassmanian
X_{v}
.

Proof.

This follows the proof of
[
67
, Thm. 9.4, Thm. 10.1]
, replacing basic uniformization results in
loc.cit.
by basic uniformization results for Kudla–Rapoport cycles and Hecke CM cycles proved in §
11.6
.
∎

We form the generating series (
h_{\infty}\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\infty})
)

(14.21)

\Int(h_{\infty},\Phi)=\Int(0,\Phi)+\sum_{\xi\in F_{0,+}}\Int(\xi,\Phi)W_{\xi}^{(n)}(h_{\infty}).

Definition 14.7

(standard partial transfers)
.

Let
\Phi=\Phi_{i}
be of the form (
14.2
). A partial transfer
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
of
\Phi
is said to be standard, if

(1)

For
v\notin\Delta
and
v\not=v_{0}
, we have
\Phi_{v}^{\prime}=1_{S(L_{v})}\otimes 1_{L_{v,0}}\times 1_{L_{v,0}^{*}}
.

(2)

At
v=v_{0}
,
\Phi_{1,v}^{\prime}=1_{S(L_{v},L_{v}^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{v,0}}\times 1_{(L_{v,0}^{\vee})^{*}}
and
\Phi_{2,v}^{\prime}=1_{S(L_{v},L_{v}^{\vee})}\times 1_{L_{v,0}^{\vee}}\times 1_{(L_{v,0})^{*}}
are standard test functions as in Definition (
1.2
).

By the proven explicit Jacquet–Rallis transfers (Theorem
2.7
) for
v\nmid\Delta
, the above definition makes sense and standard partial transfer always exists.

Based on (
14.14
) and Proposition
14.6
, we make the following comparisons for a finite non-split place
v\not\in\Delta
. Let
\Phi^{\prime}
be a standard partial transfer of
\Phi
.
We obtain the following key proposition.

Proposition 14.8
.

Consider a finite non-split place
v\not\in\Delta
and
\xi\in F_{0}^{\times}
. Assume part
(i)
of the arithmetic transfer conjecture
6.4
for
L_{v}
holds when
g\in\mathrm{U}(V^{(v)})(\alpha)
and
(u,u)=\xi
. Then we have the semi-global identity

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime}_{i})=-\Int_{v}(\xi,\Phi_{i})\in\mathbb{Q}\log p.

Proof.

For matching orbits
(\gamma,u^{\prime})\in[(S(V_{0})(\alpha)\times V^{\prime}_{\xi})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}
and

[(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v)})(\alpha)\times V^{(v)}_{\xi})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}
, we have

\Orb((g,u),\Phi^{v})=\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime v,\infty}).

Part
(i)
of the arithmetic transfer conjecture
6.4
at
v
for
(g,u)
predicts

\partial\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v})=-\Int_{v}(g,u)\log q_{v}.

The proposition now follows from comparing the terms in equation (
14.14
) and the terms (
14.18
) and (
14.19
) in Proposition
14.6
.
∎

15.
The end of proof

15.1.
The double induction method

Recall
\psi
is a fixed non-trivial additive character of
\mathbb{A}_{0}
. For any finite place
v
of
F_{0}
, let
\varpi_{v}
be a uniformizer of
F_{0,v}
. Let
c_{v}
be the conductor of
\psi
. By definition,
\psi_{v}
is trivial on
\varpi_{v}^{-c_{v}}O_{F_{0,v}}
.

For a left
N^{+}(F_{0})
-invariant continuous function
f:{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})\mathbb{C}
and
\xi\in F_{0}
, consider its
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient (
1.4
):

W_{\xi,f}(h)=\int_{F_{0}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{0}}f\left[\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}h\right]\psi_{F_{0}}(-\xi b)db.

Here we use left multiplication. There is a Fourier expansion (by an absolute convergent sum):

(15.1)

\displaystyle f(h)=\sum_{\xi\in F_{0}}W_{\xi,f}(h),\quad h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}).

Lemma 15.1
.

Let
B
be a finite collection of finite places of
F_{0}
with
v_{0}\in B
. Let
(f_{1},f_{2})
be a pair of continuous functions on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0})\backslash{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
right invariant under some compact open subgroup
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
. Assume

(1)

(Dual relation) We have
f_{1}\left(h\left(\begin{matrix}0&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{-c_{v_{0}}}\\
-\varpi_{v_{0}}^{c_{v_{0}}}&0\end{matrix}\right)\right)=f_{2}(h),\quad\forall\,h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
.

(2)

(Support condition)
f_{i}
are right invariant under

\prod_{v\in B,v\not=v_{0}}\diag\{\varpi_{v}^{-c_{v}},1\}{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(O_{F_{0,v}})\diag\{\varpi_{v}^{c_{v}},1\}.

For
v\in B,v\not=v_{0}
, set
k_{iv}:=-c_{v}
. Assume that there exists
k_{1v_{0}},\,k_{2v_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
f_{i}
is right
\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{k_{iv_{0}}}O_{v_{0}}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
-invariant, and
k_{1v_{0}}+k_{2v_{0}}+2c_{v_{0}}\leq 1
.

(3)

(Boundary condition) For all
\xi\in F_{0}
with
v(\xi)=-k_{iv}-c_{v}
for all
v\in B
and any element
h_{\infty}\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\infty})
, we have

W_{\xi,f_{i}}(h_{\infty})=0.

Then
f_{1},f_{2}
are both constant.

Proof.

Fix
i=1,2
. Fix any element
b_{v_{0}}\in F_{0,v_{0}}
such that
v_{0}(b_{v_{0}})\geq k_{iv_{0}}-1
. Set
\widetilde{B}:=B-\{v_{0}\}
. Consider the function on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
:

\widetilde{f}_{i}(h):=f_{i}(h\left(\begin{matrix}1&b_{v_{0}}\\
&1\end{matrix}\right)_{v_{0}})-f_{i}(h).

We claim that
\widetilde{f}_{i}
is a constant. If
\widetilde{B}
is empty, then
\widetilde{f}_{i}(h_{\infty}),h_{\infty}\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\infty})
is left invariant under
\left(\begin{matrix}1&F_{0,\infty}\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)
and right invariant under
K_{0,\infty}
, hence a constant. By strong approximation of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2,F_{0}}
at
\infty
, we see that
\widetilde{f}_{i}(h)
is a constant. In general, we apply
[
67
, Lem 13.6]
to the pair
(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{f}_{i})
. We check the assumptions in
loc. cit.
: for any
v\in\widetilde{B}
, as
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{v})
commutes with
\left(\begin{matrix}1&b_{v_{0}}\\
&1\end{matrix}\right)_{v_{0}}
, so the invariance at
\widetilde{B}
for
\widetilde{f}_{i}
holds. Let
\xi\in F_{0}
such that
v(\xi)=-c_{v}-k_{iv}=0
for all
v\in\widetilde{B}
. By condition
(2)
,
f_{i}
is right
\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{k_{iv_{0}}}O_{v_{0}}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
-invariant. If
v_{0}(\xi)\leq-k_{iv_{0}}-c_{v_{0}}-1
then
\psi_{-\xi}
is non-trivial on
\varpi_{v_{0}}^{k_{iv_{0}}}O_{v_{0}}
, which implies that
W_{\xi,\widetilde{f}_{i}}(h_{\infty})=0
. By condition
(3)
, if
v_{0}(\xi)=-k_{iv_{0}}-c_{v_{0}}
we have that
W_{\xi,f_{i}}(h_{\infty})=0
hence
W_{\xi,\widetilde{f}_{i}}(h_{\infty})=0
. Moreover, if
v_{0}(\xi)\geq-v_{0}(b_{v_{0}})-c_{v_{0}}
then
W_{\xi,\widetilde{f}_{i}}(h_{\infty})=(\psi(\xi b_{v_{0}})-1)W_{\xi,f_{i}}(h_{\infty})=0.
As
v_{0}(b_{v_{0}})\geq k_{iv_{0}}-1
, we see
W_{\xi,\widetilde{f}_{i}}(h_{\infty})=0
. We have checked the assumptions in
loc. cit.
, hence
\widetilde{f}_{i}
is a constant.

Therefore, for any
\xi\in F_{0}^{\times}
,
W_{\xi,\widetilde{f}_{i}}=0
. Hence
W_{\xi,f_{i}}
is right
\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{k_{iv_{0}}-1}O_{v_{0}}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
-invariant for
i=1,2
. We have

W_{\xi,f_{1}}\left(h\left(\begin{matrix}0&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{-c_{v_{0}}}\\
-\varpi_{v_{0}}^{c_{v_{0}}}&0\end{matrix}\right)_{v_{0}}\right)=W_{\xi,f_{2}}(h),

hence
W_{\xi,f_{2}}
is right invariant under the subgroup of
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,v_{0}})
generated by
\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\varpi^{k_{1v_{0}}-1+2c_{v_{0}}}_{v_{0}}O_{v_{0}}&1\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{k_{2v_{0}}-1}O_{v_{0}}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
. As
k_{1v_{0}}+k_{2v_{0}}+2c_{v_{0}}\leq 1
, the subgroup is the whole group
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,v_{0}})
: by a conjugacy we are reduced to the standard computation that
\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
\varpi^{-1}O_{v_{0}}&1\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}1&O_{v_{0}}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
generates
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,v_{0}})
. So
W_{\xi,f_{2}}(h)
is right invariant under
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,v_{0}})
and therefore is a constant by strong approximation at
v_{0}
. It follows that, for all
\xi\in F_{0}^{\times}
,
W_{\xi,f_{2}}
is a constant and hence must vanish (as
\psi
is non-trivial). Therefore
f_{2}
is a constant. As
f_{1}\left(h\left(\begin{matrix}0&\varpi_{v_{0}}^{-c_{v_{0}}}\\
-\varpi_{v_{0}}^{c_{v_{0}}}&0\end{matrix}\right)\right)=f_{2}(h)
,
f_{1}
is also a constant. The result follows.
∎

Remark 15.2
.

In classical languages, consider a modular form
f
of weight
k
and level
\Gamma_{0}(N)
and a prime
p|N
. If for all
(n,p)=1
, the Fourier coefficients
a_{n}(f)=0
and
a_{n/p}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{p}.f)=0
, then
f
is a constant.

15.2.
Globalization

Let
F_{v_{0}}/F_{0,v_{0}}
be an unramified quadratic extension of
p
-adic local fields with residue fields
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}/\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Let
L_{v_{0}}
be a vertex lattice of type
t_{0}
in a
n
-dimensional
F_{v_{0}}/F_{0,v_{0}}
hermitian space
V_{v_{0}}
. Denote by
V_{v_{0}}^{(v_{0})}
the nearby hermitian space of
V_{v_{0}}
. We have formulated the semi-Lie ATC
6.4
for
L_{v_{0}}
and any regular semisimple pair
(g_{0},u_{0})\in(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})}_{v_{0}})\times V_{v_{0}}^{(v_{0})})(F_{0,v_{0}})_{\mathrm{rs}}
. Now we give a proof assuming that
F_{0,v_{0}}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
if
0<t_{0}<n
.

We can find a CM quadratic extension
F/F_{0}
of a totally real field
F_{0}
with a distinguished embedding
\varphi_{0}:F_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}
, a
n
-dimensional
F/F_{0}
-hermitian space
V
, a CM type
\Phi
of
F
such that

•

\Phi
is unramified at
p
.

•

There exists a place
v_{0}|p
of
F_{0}
inert in
F
such that the completion of
F/F_{0}
at
v_{0}
is exactly
F_{v_{0}}/F_{0,v_{0}}
. All places
v\not=v_{0}
of
F_{0}
above
p
are split or inert in
F
.

•

V
is of signature
(n-1,1)
at
\varphi_{0}
, and
(n,0)
at all other real places of
F_{0}
.

•

The localization
V\otimes_{F}F_{v_{0}}
is isomorphic to the hermitian space
V_{v_{0}}
we start with. The space
V_{p}=\prod_{v|p}V_{v}
contains a lattice
L_{p}=\prod_{v|p}L_{v}
such that
L_{v}
is self-dual for
v\not=v_{0}
above
p
, and that
L_{v_{0}}
a vertex lattice of type
t_{0}
at
v_{0}|p
.

If
0<t_{0}<n
, then we choose
F/\mathbb{Q}
to be Galois hence the assumption in Theorem
13.4
holds, i.e. the reflex field
E=F
. For example, we take
F/F_{0}=\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m})/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{m}+\zeta_{m}^{-1})
with
m=q+1
.

Let
V^{(v_{0})}
be the nearby
F/F_{0}
hermitian space of
V
at
v_{0}
so the localization of
V^{(v_{0})}
at
v_{0}
is isomorphic to
V^{(v_{0})}_{v_{0}}
. As in
[
41
, Section 10.1]
, by local constancy of intersection numbers
[
42
]
(whose argument is quite general and applies to our Rapoport–Zink space
\mathcal{N}_{L_{v_{0}}}
), we can find a pair
(g,u)\in(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})})\times V^{v_{0}})(F_{0})_{\mathrm{rs}}
such that

•

(g,u)
is
v_{0}
-closely enough to
(g_{0},u_{0})
such that two sides of Conjecture
6.4
are the same for
(g,u)
and
(g_{0},u_{0})
. The characteristic polynomial
\alpha
of
g
is irreducible over
F
.

•

The norm
\xi_{0}=(u,u)_{V^{v_{0}}}\in F_{0}
is non-zero.

•

For all places
v\not=v_{0}
of
F_{0}
above
p
, the orbital integral for the
\mathrm{U}(V_{v})
-orbit of
(g,u)

\Orb((g,u),1_{\mathrm{U}(L_{v})}\times 1_{L_{v}})\not=0.

Moreover, if
v
is inert in
F
, then
\alpha_{v}
is of maximal order.

Choose a finite collection
\Delta
of finite places of
F_{0}
such that
p\nmid\Delta
, i.e. all places
v
of
F_{0}
above
p
are not in
\Delta
.

We can enlarge
\Delta
(keeping
p\nmid\Delta
) to assume that
\Delta
and
L
are in the set up of §
10.2
and
\Phi
is unramfied at all
\ell\not\in\Delta
. Consider a level structure
\widetilde{K}
for
L
and
\Delta
. We consider the RSZ integral model

\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}}\Spec O_{E}[\Delta^{-1}]

for the RSZ Shimura variety associated to
V
with level for
\Delta
and
L
. By
[
67
, Prop. 13.8]
, we can enlarge
\Delta
and shrink
K_{G,\Delta}
to find
\phi_{\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(V(F_{0,\Delta}))
and
\phi_{CM,\Delta}\in\mathcal{S}(K_{G,\Delta}\backslash\mathrm{U}(V)(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta})
such that the following holds:

•

L
is self-dual and
\alpha(t)
is a maximal order at any finite place
v\nmid\Delta
and
v\not=v_{0}
.

•

For the function
\Phi^{v_{0}}=(\phi_{CM,\Delta}\otimes 1_{K_{G}^{\Delta,v_{0}}})\otimes(\phi_{\Delta}\otimes 1_{\widehat{L}^{\Delta,v_{0}}})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{U}(V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}}))\times V(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}^{v_{0}})),

its orbital integral
\Orb((g_{1},u_{1}),\Phi^{v_{0}})
at any
(g_{1},u_{1})\in(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})})\times V^{(v_{0})})(F_{0})
is zero, unless
(g_{1},u_{1})=(g,u)
in
[(\mathrm{U}(V^{(v_{0})})\times V^{(v_{0})})(F_{0})]_{\mathrm{rs}}
. Moreover,
\Orb((g,u),\Phi^{v_{0}})
is non-zero.

Let
\Phi^{\prime}
be a standard partial transfer of
\Phi
(Definition
14.7
).

Theorem 15.3
.

(1)

For
i=1,\,2
, part
(i)
of the Conjecture
6.4
for
(g_{0},u_{0})
follows from the semi-global identity:

(15.2)

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v_{0}}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i})=-\Int_{v_{0}}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})\in\mathbb{Q}\log p.

(2)

The semi-global identity (
15.2
) is equivalent to the global identity

(15.3)

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})=-\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

Proof.

The first property, we use the comparison argument in Proposition
14.8
. As the orbital integrals

\Orb((g,u),\Phi^{v_{0}})=\Orb((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime v_{0},\infty})
are non-zero, we can divide it and deduce local AT identity (Conjecture
6.4
) from the semi-global identity (
15.2
).

For the second property, using the computation (
14.20
) in Proposition
14.6
and the decomposition (
14.17
), we see

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})+\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})

is equal to (
\ell
runs over prime numbers not lying in
\Delta
)

\sum_{v\in\mathrm{Inert}(F/F_{0})-\Delta}2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i})+\Int_{v}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})\in\sum_{\ell\not\in\Delta}\mathbb{Q}\log\ell\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

We use
\mathbb{Q}
-linear independence of all
\log\ell
. We see the global identity is equivalent to the identities
(\ell)
for all
\ell

(\ell),\,\,\,\sum_{v\in\mathrm{Inert}(F/F_{0})-\Delta,v|\ell}2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i})=-\Int_{v}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})=0\in\mathbb{Q}\log\ell\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

If
\ell\not=p
. Then the identity
(\ell)
is known by the known AFL
[
41
]
and Proposition
14.8
. Similarly, the semi-global identity is known for all
v\not=v_{0}
above
p
by the known AFL
[
41
]
and Proposition
14.8
. Hence the result follows.
∎

15.3.
Modification

Possibly enlarging
\Delta
(keeping
p\nmid\Delta
), we do the following modifications (similar to the proof of Theorem
13.5
):

(1)

(After enlarging
\Delta
) We choose another irreducible conjugate self-reciprocal monic polynomial
\alpha_{m}\in O_{F}[1/\Delta][t]
of degree
n
such that
\alpha_{m}
is a maximal order away from
\Delta
and unramified at
v_{0}
. Choose
\mu_{0}\in K_{G,\Delta}\backslash G(F_{0,\Delta})/K_{G,\Delta}
such that the cycle
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha_{m}}:=\mathcal{CM}(\alpha_{m},\mu_{0})
is non-empty. From §
12.1
, there exists a rational number
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{Q}
, such that

\mathcal{C}_{1}={}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM})-\lambda_{0}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha_{m}}

is of degree
0
over
\mathbb{C}
. On the analytic side, the corresponding contribution is

2\lambda_{0}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha_{m}}(\xi,\Phi_{im}^{\prime})

where
\Phi^{\prime}_{im}
are (relative to
\alpha_{m}
) Gaussian transfers for
\Phi_{im}:=(\Phi_{i})^{\Delta}\otimes(1_{\mu_{0}}\otimes\phi_{\Delta})
. We can enlarge
\Delta
such that
\Phi_{im}
are also standard away from
\Delta
.
By the known ATC (and AFL) for unramified maximal orders (Section
9
), the local intersection number at
v_{0}
for
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha_{m}}
can be computed directly to match the
v_{0}
-part of
2\lambda_{0}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha_{m}}(\xi,\Phi_{im}^{\prime})
on the analytic side.

(2)

By §
12.4
, for any place
\nu_{0}
of
E
over
v_{0}
we can find a very special
1
-cycle
\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}
in the basic locus of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}
such that

(X_{j},\mathcal{C}_{1})_{\mathcal{M}}=(X_{j},\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}})_{\mathcal{M}}
for all irreducible components
X_{j}
of
\mathcal{M}_{k_{\nu_{0}}}
. On the analytic side, the corresponding contribution is

(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{B}(\xi,\phi_{i}),\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}})

which is
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient of a theta series
\Theta(h,\phi_{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})

on
V^{(v_{0})}
hence modular by Theorem
13.9
.

For
i=1,2
, consider the
1
-cycle (independent of
i
)

(15.4)

\mathcal{C}^{\perp}:={}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{CM}(\alpha,\phi_{CM})-\lambda_{0}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha_{m}}-\sum_{\nu_{0}|v_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\mathcal{M}).

We define the difference function on the geometric side as the modified generating function on
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
:

(15.5)

\Int(h,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}:=\frac{1}{\tau(Z^{\mathbb{Q}})[E:F]}(Z(h,\phi_{i}),\mathcal{C}^{\perp})^{\text{adm}},

and the modified archimedean intersection number on
h\in{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0})
:

(15.6)

\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(h,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}:=\frac{1}{\tau(Z^{\mathbb{Q}})[E:F]}{\sum_{\nu|\infty}}\mathcal{G}^{\bf K-\bf B}_{\nu}(h,\phi_{i})(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}).

For
\xi\in F_{0}
, we may consider its
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient
\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}
.

We define the difference function on the analytic side:

(15.7)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(h):=2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime}_{i})-2\lambda_{0}\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha_{m}}(h,\Phi_{im}^{\prime})+\sum_{\nu_{0}|v_{0}}\Theta(h,\phi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(h,\Phi^{\prime})^{\perp}.

For
\xi\in F_{0}
, we may consider its
\xi
-th Fourier coefficient
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(\xi)
.

By modularity on the generic fiber (Theorem
11.2
) and Poisson summation formulas, we know
\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(h,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}
and
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(h)
are automorphic forms on
{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(A_{0})
and
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}
is holomorphic.

For a compact open subgroup
K_{0,\Delta}\leq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(F_{0,\Delta})
, consider the compact open subgroup
K_{0}\subseteq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f})
given by

K_{0}=\diag\{c_{v_{0}}^{-1},1\}\Gamma(v_{0})\diag\{c_{v_{0}},1\}\times K_{0}^{\Delta,v_{0},\circ}\times K_{0,\Delta}\leq{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0,f}).

where
K_{0}^{\Delta,v_{0},\circ}:=\prod_{v\not\in\Delta,v\not=v_{0}}\diag\{c_{v}^{-1},1\}{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(O_{F_{0},v})\diag\{c_{v},1\}
and
\Gamma(v_{0}):=\text{Ker}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(O_{F_{0,v_{0}}}){\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(k_{v_{0}}))
. Choose
K_{0,\Delta}
to be sufficiently small so that the pair
\Phi_{1}
is invariant under the Weil representation by
K_{0}
. Recall we use the standard additive character
\psi=\psi_{F_{0}}=\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}\circ\tr_{F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}}
on
\mathbb{A}_{0}
for the Weil representation. The level
c_{v}
of
\psi
at
v
is
0
if
F_{0}/\mathbb{Q}
is unramified at
v
. In particular, by assumption
c_{v_{0}}=0
if
0<t_{0}<n
.

Corollary 15.4
.

For
i=1,2
, the generating function
\Int(h,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}
lies in
\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{R}_{\Delta,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Moreover, if
c_{v_{0}}=0
the dual relation holds:

\Int(h\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{v_{0}},\Phi_{1})^{\perp}=\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}\vol(L_{v_{0}})\Int(h,\Phi_{2})^{\perp}.

Proof.

The first part follow from Theorem
13.4
. If
c_{v_{0}}=0
, we know
\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{v_{0}}.1_{L_{v_{0}}}=\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}\vol(L_{v_{0}})1_{L_{v_{0}}^{\vee}}
hence
\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{v_{0}}.\Phi_{1}=\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}\vol(L_{v_{0}})\Phi_{2}
. Then the dual relation follows from equation
11.4
.
∎

Proposition 15.5
.

For
i=1,2
, the difference function
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(h)
lies in
\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)
. Moreover, if
c_{v_{0}}=0
the dual relation holds:

(15.8)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,1}(h\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{v_{0}})=\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}\vol(L_{v_{0}})\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,2}(h).

Proof.

We show the dual relation holds summand by summand in the decomposition (
15.7
).

The archimedean part
\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(h,\Phi^{\prime})^{\perp}
follows from
[
41
, Prop. 10.5]
. We compute
\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}=(-1)^{t}
and
\vol(L_{v_{0}})=q^{-t}_{v_{0}}
, hence by Theorem
8.1
(where
c_{v_{0}}=0
) we have
\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{v_{0}}.\phi^{\prime}_{1,\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}}=\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}\vol(L_{v_{0}})\phi^{\prime}_{2,\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}}.

So we have the dual relation for two theta series
\Theta(h,\phi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}})
lying in
\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)
.

The dual relations for
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi_{i}^{\prime})
and
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha_{m}}(h,\Phi_{im}^{\prime})
follows from the equivariant definition
\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(h,\Phi^{\prime},s)
in equation (
14.4
).
∎

15.4.
Proof by double induction

We may substract the global identity (
15.3
) by known global identities.

Theorem 15.6
.

For
i=1,2
, the Conjecture
6.4
for
(g_{0},u_{0})
follows from the identity of Fourier coefficients

(15.9)

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(\xi_{0})=-\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})^{\perp}.

Proof.

We use Theorem
15.3
. Assume
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(\xi_{0})=-\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})^{\perp}
holds. We only need to show the global identity (
15.3
)

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})=-\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}

We apply Proposition
14.8
and Theorem
15.3
to the maximal order
\alpha_{m}
. As we already prove the AT conjecture for unramified maximal orders in particular for
\alpha_{m}
(Section
9
), we have

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha_{m}}(\xi_{0},\Phi^{\prime}_{i,m})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i,m})=-\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i,m})\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

By the computation of the
\xi_{0}
-th Fourier coefficient
\Theta(\xi_{0},\phi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}})
in Theorem
13.9
, we have

\Theta(\xi_{0},\phi^{\prime}_{i,\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}})=(\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}^{\bf B}(\xi,\phi_{i}),\mathcal{C}_{\nu_{0}}).

Add above known global terms together with respect to the decomposition of the analytic side (
15.7
) and the geometric side
\mathcal{C}^{\perp}

(
15.4
), the proposition is proved.
∎

We now do inductions and assume the semi-Lie version Conjecture
6.4
holds for all maximal parahoric levels for all dimensions (of the hermitian space) smaller than
n
.

Theorem 15.7
.

For
i=1,2
, we have the identity

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(h)=-\Int(h,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}.

in the function space
\mathcal{A}_{\rm hol}({\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{0}),K_{0},n)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathbb{R}_{\Delta,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. In particular, we have
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(\xi_{0})=-\Int(\xi_{0},\Phi_{i})^{\perp}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
15.4
and Proposition
15.5
, both sides are holomorphic modular forms. For
i=1,2
, denote the differences by
f_{i}(h):=\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(h)+\Int(h,\Phi_{i})^{\perp}
. The theorem follows by applying the double induction lemma
15.1
for the pair
(f_{1},(\gamma_{V_{v_{0}}}\vol(L_{v_{0}}))f_{2})
and
B=\{v_{0}\}
with
k_{1v_{0}}=-c_{v_{0}},\,k_{2v_{0}}=-c_{v_{0}}+1
. For this, we check the assumptions in Lemma
15.1
:

(1)

we may assume that
F_{0v_{0}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is unramified so
c_{v_{0}}=0
if
0<t_{0}<n
. For general
c_{v}
, it follows from how the local Weil representation change under the change of additive characters. The dual relation in Lemma
15.1
holds by Corollary
15.4
and Proposition
15.5
.

(2)

the valuation of
L_{v_{0}}
and
L_{v_{0}}^{\vee}
is
0
and
-1
respectively, so the support condition in Lemma
15.1
follows.

Now we only need to show the boundary condition in Lemma
15.1
, namely equalities of Fourier coefficients

\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,i}(\xi,h_{\infty})=-\Int(\xi,h_{\infty},\Phi_{i})^{\perp}.

for all
\xi\in F_{0}^{\times}
such that
v_{0}(\xi)=0
(resp.
v_{0}(\xi)=-1
) if
i=0
(resp.
v_{0}(\xi)=-1
). Since both sides are holomorphic of the same weight
n
, we can assume
h_{\infty}=1
. It is sufficient to prove the following two claims:

‘
Claim
\mathcal{Z}
. For
\xi
with
v_{0}(\xi)=0
, we have
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,1}(\xi)=-\Int(\xi,\Phi_{1})^{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

Claim
\mathcal{Y}
. For
\xi
with
v_{0}(\xi)=-1
, we have
\partial\mathbb{J}_{\rm hol,2}(\xi)=-\Int(\xi,\Phi_{2})^{\perp}\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

Adding these local error terms at
\xi
again, the Claim
\mathcal{Z}
follows if we can show the identity

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime}_{1})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi,\Phi_{1})=-\Int(\xi,\Phi_{1})\in\mathbb{R}_{\Delta}.

By the factorization and vanishing of archimedean terms, it is sufficient to show

2\partial\mathbb{J}_{\alpha,v}(\xi,\Phi^{\prime}_{1})+\Int^{\bf K-\bf B}(\xi,\Phi_{1})=-\Int_{v}(\xi,\Phi_{1})\in\mathbb{R}.

holds for any finite place
v\not\in\Delta
of
F_{0}
. By local-global decompositions on both sides (see Proposition
14.6
and equation
14.14
), it is sufficient to prove the local identity

\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v})=-\Int_{v}(g,u)\log q_{v}.

We prove this. If
v=v_{0}
, then
v_{0}(\xi)=0
, hence the ATC identities
\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{iv_{0}})=-\Int_{v_{0}}^{i}(g,u)
holds by Theorem
6.15
and induction. If
v\not=v_{0}
, then due to our enlargement of
\Delta
we know that
O_{F}[t]/(\alpha(t))
is a maximal order at
v
. In this case the above identity
\operatorname{\partial Orb}\bigl((\gamma,u^{\prime}),\Phi^{\prime}_{v})=-\Int_{v}(g,u)\log q_{v}
is also known by the known maximal order AFLs in
[
40
, Cor. 9.1]
(which is reproved for unramified maximal orders in §
9
).
This finishes the proof of Claim
\mathcal{Z}
. The Claim
\mathcal{Y}
is proved in the same way by applying the dual isomorphism.
∎

By Theorem
15.6
and Theorem
15.7
, the proof of arithmetic transfer identities for the vertex lattice
L_{v_{0}}
(
6.4
) is complete, assuming that
F_{0}
is unramified over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
if
0<t_{0}<n
.

∎
